# Assistant and panel

← [README](../../README.md)

## Voice chat

The **Assistant** page needs the microphone, which browsers only allow over https; the panel therefore also listens on `https://SPARK:31443` with a self-signed certificate (one browser warning). The LLM connection is under Settings → Language model (default: the own language model behind the LLM-Tor, `http://127.0.0.1:31006/v1`). "Model" offers the models the server reports (`/v1/models`, admin only, kept 60 seconds, "Reload" asks at once); "Automatic" takes the first, "Type another …" opens a text field, which also stays (with the reason) when the list cannot be read. Thinking is off for the chat so the answer starts at once. An animated face shows listening, thinking and speaking, its mouth follows the voice; it stands only in the assistant, on every other page "Ask Spark" at the top (computer: next to "Spark" in the side menu, phone: top bar) starts talking. Faces: *Robot* (default), *Comic* (spiky hair, short mustache above the upper lip) *Comic (woman)* (long dark hair, side parting, lashes, lips that open when speaking) and *Wave* (no face: on a calm tile one line that runs out softly at both ends; while speaking the middle swings with the loudness, a faint echo behind it, while listening it follows the microphone, on an error it turns red; colours only from the panel, light and dark by themselves; companions as a still, Pebble as a line). The comic faces look around, blink, raise their brows, tilt their head and show the state with a ring: grey idle, blue listening, yellow thinking, green speaking, red error. Every profile picks its face under Me → Conversation → *Display* (*Default*, *Robot*, *Comic*, *Comic (woman)*, *Wave*), valid on all its devices (panel, iPhone app under Settings → *Face*, companions as a still, Pebble); *Default* takes the admin's pick under Settings → Defaults → *Face*, which applies to guests, shared devices and everybody without an own pick; the presets never copy a face into a profile. The server only takes fixed names (`profiles.FACES`), every device asks through `profiles.face_of`; more faces go into `static/js/face.js`. The small picture before each answer shows the chosen face too. The switch "Gesicht zeigt, was es tut" (off) shows a sign beside the head, taken only from panel events, never from the model's text: a magnifier for searches (web, documents, earlier conversations), a calendar sheet for calendar and briefing, a letter for mail, a light bulb for Home Assistant, a note pad for memory and reminders, thought bubbles while thinking. After a completed action it is pleased and winks, on errors it looks puzzled with a question mark, after 5 minutes without conversation or use it sleeps ("zzz"); a tap, key or the mouse wake it.

**Start page and password:** the panel opens with the assistant. Status, settings, voices and logs need the panel password (button "Settings"; the login ends after 7 days without use and renews while in use). "Assistant without password" under Settings → Security is off on new installs: guests are locked out, profiles sign in with name and PIN (devices with a key keep working), the admin with "Admin". Switched on, the assistant is open to everyone on the network. **Guests only get questions and answers**: the model answers from its own knowledge without any tool (no web search, timers, briefing, memory, history on the Spark, smart home); above the history it says "Without a profile: questions and answers only" with *Sign in*. This also holds for a voice a shared speaker does not recognize as its owner's, and for the admin in a browser without an own profile. Change the password under Settings → Security; scripts can use HTTP Basic.

**Release:** what the admin switches on under Features works at once for every profile, without a per-profile switch. Only the admin blocks single profiles (People and devices → Blocked functions or in the function's detail). A profile only sets up its part (place, account, device) and can only switch off what the Spark does unasked (notes on its own, phone notifications, the morning sheet, night speaker updates); these are on from the start.

**Blocked, but own data:** when the admin blocks a function for a profile, its page under Me disappears; the data stays the profile's. While something is left, Me shows a card: "The admin blocked "Weather" for you (People and devices → Blocked functions). Stored: 1 entry." with "Delete data" (asks first). It deletes exactly what deleting on the function's page removes, passwords and keys included (place, stop, calendars, mailboxes, address books, reminders, voice samples, Home Assistant connection, Telegram link, paired devices, MCP connections and services, the reMarkable with its documents, knowledge folders, rules of "Proactive" and of inbox tidying); then the card goes. Not included: all documents at once and chats, because others belong to them. Technically every module registers its data in `features.OWN` (count, delete); `/api/whoami` names under `locked` only function, page and count, never contents; deleting goes through `DELETE /api/profile/owndata/{key}` (only the own browser login, never a device key or guest, only while blocked, rate limit `owndata`). Journal: "profiles: own data deleted while blocked | weather | 1 entries …" (area memory).

**On the phone:** below 760 px the page opens with the big face (tap = talk) and two buttons: "Settings" opens "Me" ("Hey Spark" at the top), "History" the messenger view (bar with arrow back to the face, small face, state, profile, menu; the history; at the bottom text field and mic button, stop during the answer). The browser remembers the view open last. The menu (☰) holds new conversation, earlier conversations, conversation settings, language, light/dark and, for admins, every tab.

**Like the app:** on a phone (below 760 px) the page looks like the iPhone app, the same on iPhone and Android. At the bottom the tabs *Spark* (conversation), *Today* (setup, cards and sentences as Me → Overview), *Documents*, *Me* and *Manage*, by role (guests only Spark, Manage only admin, Documents only when allowed). Spark shows the face (smaller once there are bubbles), history, mic button and text field; top left "History" (sheet from below, swipe down closes) and "New". Me and Manage are lists like the iOS settings; sub-pages slide in from the right, "‹ Back" or the back gesture goes one level back. Manage: status, Features, People and devices, Settings, Logs, Update, Backups, Appearance. Under Appearance "Show the computer layout" switches to the other phone view (this browser only), the menu's "Show like the app" returns. Presentation only: no own endpoints, the server checks rights. Bubbles start about 16 px from the edge and run up to 40 px before the other edge; "Read again" and "Delete" show only after a tap on the message, below the bubble.

**Typing comes first:** while something is typed into the text field, the face draws only about ten instead of sixty pictures a second (until 1.5 s after the last letter), and the page asks only four times a second which face is on screen. Typing stays smooth on slow phones, tablets and in the Android app. In the iPhone app the line to type in is its own view; a letter redraws only that line.

**Typing comes first, part 2:** while text stands in the field, the field is marked "hastext" (not "typing", so the waiting dots `.typing::after` do not run invisibly). The face's loading wheel turns only while it shows (thinking, searching). The conversation is closed off (`#chatlog{contain:strict}`), so a letter does not lay out every bubble of a long conversation again.

**Quick start and tidier pages:** an admin's page start on a slow phone takes about 2 s. Jump links check menu visibility once per frame. The cards under Status (Listening now, Services, NAS, Usage and others) ask the Spark only while Status is open, all together when it opens. The text behind „Guide“ is written at the first tap; there is no page of all guides any more. The start page goes out gzipped (about 40 KB), every script and style file carries its fingerprint: after an update the browser loads only what changed and keeps the rest up to a year. On computers the face column is 320 px wide (buttons and switches one below the other). Away from the assistant every page ends with room for the floating face. Status has a menu like Settings: symbols and the blocks Watch (Summary, Live, Logs), Check (Checks, Improve, Hands-on test) and Technology (Usage, Models, Architecture); the footer stands at the bottom of the window. On phones „Manage“/„Verwalten“ fits whole into the back button. Features and People and devices have symbols too; every group of Features carries the symbol of the same group under Me. The profile list shows a coloured circle with initials, one line "last … · 2 devices · 30 facts", setup as a bar with "14/15", a chevron (the whole line opens the person), search field and selects of one height, "+ New profile" beside the count; devices show their kind as a symbol (speaker, iPhone app, watch, terminal, Home Assistant, own key).

**As an app on your phone:** open `https://SPARK:31443` in the phone's browser, accept the certificate warning, then "Add to Home screen" (iPhone: Safari → Share → "Add to Home Screen"). The assistant starts full screen with its own icon. Because of the self-signed certificate Android creates a shortcut, without an entry in the app list.

**Better recognition:** a fixed default language (Settings → Speech recognition) instead of "auto" helps most with short sentences. The "Context" field takes often misheard names and terms, which the model then prefers. 1.7B is more accurate than 0.6B and needs about 2–3 GiB more.

**Second recognizer: Parakeet.** Under Settings → Speech recognition → "Recognizer" the admin picks Qwen3-ASR (GPU) or Parakeet ([primeline/parakeet-primeline](https://huggingface.co/primeline/parakeet-primeline), a German fine-tune of NVIDIA Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3; the download is the int8 ONNX export [flozen1981/parakeet-primeline-onnx](https://huggingface.co/flozen1981/parakeet-primeline-onnx), run via sherpa-onnx; idea from [winidi/dictate](https://github.com/winidi/dictate)). Parakeet runs on the CPU (about 1 GiB), the Qwen3-ASR engine is stopped meanwhile. It ignores the context field, a stream returns the text in one piece. The first start downloads about 640 MB.

**Conversation comfort:** the assistant knows the date and time (the browser's time zone; can be turned off under Settings → Language model). Conversations are kept in the browser and can be reopened or deleted at the top of the conversation. With "live transcript" the text appears while you speak; recognition is usually done by the pause.

**Guides:** every service that can be switched on has an openable guide (what it does, what you need, setup, example phrases, what leaves the house, switching off, troubleshooting): next to its switch under Features and at the top of its page under "Me". There is no page "Guides" of its own any more since V01.1.391 (it repeated in some 300 folding lines without a search what stands next to every switch): the search (Ctrl K, group "Guides") finds every guide and opens it at its place. The four guides without a switch and without a Me page stand where they are needed: "Hey Siri, ask Spark", "Pebble watch" and "Open WebUI and own programs" in their card under People and devices → Apps and interfaces, "Firmware by hand" at the bottom of the details of the system updates (the button "Install by hand" opens it); "Set up a Synology" stands at the top of Me → Storage. A manager no longer has the menu entry Features (it only showed the guides there). Groups are the same everywhere: Conversation, My day, Mail and documents, Home, On the go and devices, Security and access. Texts in `app/panel/static/js/guides.js`; a self-test checks that no switch is left without a guide and group.

**Weather, contacts, parcels:** three small services, off by default; once the admin releases them under Features they work for every profile (each profile sets up place and address book under "Me"), never for guests. *Weather* comes straight from Open-Meteo (no key, only the coordinates leave, own address configurable); the Spark builds fixed sentences from the numbers, also for the briefing and the proactive weather note. *Contacts* reads an address book over CardDAV (iCloud: https://contacts.icloud.com with an app-specific password, Nextcloud, others) once a day, kept encrypted: phone numbers, e-mail addresses, birthdays (briefing and notice), names instead of addresses in mail; contact text counts as outside text. *Parcels* recognises shipping mails of DHL, Hermes, DPD, GLS, UPS and Amazon in the own mailbox (14 days; the inbox and every other folder except sent, drafts, trash, spam and the collected views, since V01.1.412, also own folders like "Deliveries"; the mail server searches only for the carriers' senders, nothing is changed) and says what comes today, is on its way, waits for pick-up or was delivered; no carrier is asked, parcel lines count as mail text.

**Telegram:** your own bot from @BotFather (guide in the panel). The admin switches it on under Features and enters the token (encrypted); the Spark polls Telegram itself, no open port needed. Each profile links its Telegram under "Me" with a one-time code (10 minutes) and writes or sends voice messages (recognition on the Spark). Strangers get one fixed sentence, groups nothing. Per profile, all off at first: answers as voice messages, notifications over Telegram, personal data (calendar, mail, contacts, parcels, documents; also in notifications) and switching the smart home, only with the code word. Commands: /neu, /stimme, /trennen. When a message names a panel place ("Me → Telegram"), it gets "Open in the panel" if the panel address for links (https) is set under Features → Telegram.

**Own speakers (ESP32):** ESP32-S3 boards (16 MB flash, PSRAM, INMP441 microphone, MAX98357A amplifier, e.g. XiaoZhi breadboard boards) become a voice assistant in the room. Firmware: the open XiaoZhi firmware at a fixed version, German, wake word "Jarvis", no cloud address; a GitHub Action (`.github/workflows/firmware.yml`, settings in `firmware/xiaozhi.json`) builds it as release `fw-<version>`. The Spark fetches the newest daily (or on a button), checks every file against `manifest.json` and keeps the previous one. Set up under "Me" → Speakers: over USB from Chrome or Edge (Web Serial, esptool-js 0.5.7 in the panel) the browser writes firmware and a settings block (Wi-Fi, Spark address, device key); the Wi-Fi password never reaches the Spark. Without a PC a board shows or says a 6-digit code. Each speaker is a device key of its profile (voice, memory, smart home with code word, room). Updates at start over Wi-Fi (per device "auto" can be switched off) or over USB. Admin switch `chat.esp32`, off at first, released for every profile; the reverse proxy must pass WebSockets. Variants: no display, OLED 128×32, OLED 128×64, colour display, Waveshare ESP32-S3-AUDIO-Board (round, ES8311 audio chip, two microphones; display and camera optional). Updates follow the board type reported at start; the no-display variant never touches the display pins and loads no font or emoji. Start check only by POST of at most 16 KB, at most 3 open codes per address, firmware only from github.com downloads with a size limit.

**Device updates:** Features → "Device updates" (admin, off by default) makes the Spark look for firmware for speakers, Android app and Spark companion every 6 hours, only from GitHub's download addresses, with size and checksum from the manifest; *Look now* in Status → Device updates asks at once. The card shows per kind of device the version on the Spark, the last check and every device with its version. Profiles with such a device and the profile for admin notes get one notification per new version (not for the version present at switch-on). The companion shows its own updates. My status has one row per kind of device ("update comes tonight", "1 of 2 up to date"), the live view the target "Geräte-Updates" with counts. **Update speakers at night** (sub-switch; on for every profile, can be switched off under Me → Conversation → Devices → "Update my speakers at night", plus "Updates by themselves" at the speaker): boards fetch firmware only when they start. In the night window (default from 05:15 for 60 minutes, after the nightly Spark update) the Spark first restarts one connected, unused speaker; if it comes back with the new version within 10 minutes, the others follow one by one at least 30 minutes apart. Otherwise the round stops for this version, owner and admin get a notification, and it goes on only after *Go on distributing* in Status. Never during a conversation, in room mode, during a test, a Notaus or own Notaus, and never during a Spark update. **Note on a new XiaoZhi version** (sub-switch): the Spark compares the newest release of github.com/78/xiaozhi-esp32 with our base and sends the admin one note per version; nothing is built by itself, only the version number is used, never the text. GitHub's answer may be up to 4 MB (otherwise "XiaoZhi check failed: TooLarge" in the error log). **Firmware 2.5.2.8** builds on XiaoZhi 2.5.2 (commit 49b4380): uniform I2S for microphone and speaker, an unreadable serial number is ignored; all own patches (own server only, board without display, wait instead of dropping sound, Notaus button, quiet at once on the wake word, stop while it speaks) apply. Boards take only newer versions by update; going back to 2.5.1.7 only over USB.

**Interrupting a speaker:** while an own speaker talks, the board only listens for its wake word "Jarvis" (with echo cancelling where the board has a reference channel, such as Waveshare) or the button, and is then quiet at once: the firmware drops the rest of its play queue (`firmware/patch_unterbrechen.py`) and listens. Saying only "Stopp", "Halt", "Ruhe", "Hör auf" or "Danke, das reicht" (fixed word list `stoppwort.py`, whole sentence) sends nothing to the language model: the speaker stays quiet and waits for its wake word; Check shows "Stopp gehört". Other questions are answered. **Stop while it speaks** (admin: Features → Own speakers → Stop while it speaks, off at first, then for every profile) works without the wake word: at every connection the Spark asks the board through the MCP tool `self.spark.stop_word` (`firmware/patch_stoppwort.py`) to keep its microphone on while speaking; only boards with a reference channel do so (breadboard boards do not). The Spark cuts what it hears into pieces (at most `STOP_PIECE` = 3 s, one recognition at a time; a piece arriving meanwhile is checked after it, never thrown away); only a whole sentence from `stoppwort.stop` counts, not when it is in the last four sentences said (echo). It then sends `tts stop` with `spark_quiet` (the board drops its queue), ends the answer without a second plain `stop` and closes the connection as after a whole "Stopp": the board waits for its wake word, nothing listens on. Nothing is stored; the log only says "stop word while speaking", the live overview counts "Lautsprecher hört auf „Stopp““ under connections. When the switch goes off, an asked board is told "off" once at its next connection.

**Stopping means off:** a whole sentence of stop words or a goodbye ("Stopp", "Halt", "Hör auf", "Schon gut", "Danke, das reicht", "Tschüss", "Danke, das war's", in English "stop", "be quiet", "that's all", "bye") ends the talk everywhere by a fixed rule, never through the model: one list in `stoppwort.py` for page, apps, Begleiter, Telegram, Siri, speakers, phone, barge-in and room mode. The chat checks before the switch (`chat_turn.prepare`): no model answer, no tool, nothing stored, a waiting question back lapses, log only "chat: ende stopp | web". The answer is the event `ende`: page and Android app write "Stopped.", the Begleiter stops reading aloud, Telegram and Siri get "Okay." or "Tschüss!". Afterwards no device opens the microphone by itself, not even with hands-free. "Stopp die Musik" or "Halt, wie spät ist es?" stay questions; "Gute Nacht" only ends a phone call; "Nicht jetzt" to a note of the Spark stays that note's feedback (two hours' pause). "Hey Spark, stopp" only stops: no tone, no microphone. The page keeps a turn number: Stop, a new or another conversation (also from Find, Open and Me) and a stop word end the turn; afterwards neither the end of the sound nor a running recognition nor returning to the tab opens the microphone; during "Transcribing …" Stop works and the question is not sent. When nothing listens any more, the page releases the microphone track. An answer always lands in the conversation it was asked in. The Android app closes the connection on Stop; the Begleiter does not ask a wake-word question when "microphone closed" or pause came meanwhile. Kill switch stage 3 ends whatever is talking (`laufend.py`, see [Security and operation](security-and-operation.md)); Home Assistant Assist stops computing as soon as Home Assistant aborts. The iPhone app has its own list.

**Checking a speaker:** under Me → Speakers every speaker has a *Check* button. It shows whether the admin switch is on and the profile not blocked, whether there is firmware for the board variant, whether the board ever checked in, which address is in the board (warning for `https://<IP>` or port 31443, whose self-signed certificate the board refuses, for `localhost` and a missing address) and what the speaker did lately: start check, connection, refused connection with reason, wake word, microphone (length, loudest moment and mean in dB, speech yes/no), what was understood and sound sent back. These lines stay in memory only (40 per speaker, gone when the panel restarts) and follow live (every 2 seconds while the page is visible); in room mode it only says that a sentence was heard and whether it speaks in a pause, never what was heard. *Check network* asks the address from the Spark the way the board does: `GET /api/esp32/ping` (answers with a marker of this Spark) and a WebSocket with `x-spark-probe: 1`; this shows whether the address leads to this Spark, the certificate is accepted, the reverse proxy redirects or blocks WebSockets. Allowed like Home Assistant (home network yes, the Spark's own service ports no), only these two paths, answers of at most 4 KB, six checks in ten minutes per profile; without NAT loopback on the router it can fail although the board reaches the Spark. *Test: sound and microphone* plays the soft tone and a test sentence at the next "Jarvis" or button press (at once when connected) and checks the microphone with the next sentence: the speaker says what it understood or that it heard nothing. *Read the board's log (USB)* connects the board in Chrome or Edge, restarts it and reads for 45 seconds; the page translates known lines (Wi-Fi, certificate refused, Spark unreachable, status 403, pairing code, conversation connection, crash, brownout, sound error). The text stays in the browser, words like token or password are blacked out; *Copy* takes it along for a bug report. Every speaker has a *Volume* slider (0 to 100; the Spark sends it at every connection through the MCP tool `self.audio_speaker.set_volume`, at once when connected) and a *Microphone* choice less sensitive / normal / more sensitive (speech threshold about −41, −47 or −53 dB). A level from about −47 dB (normal) and three times above the background noise counts as speech; quiet recordings are raised up to 20 times before recognition. The log shows "esp32: microphone at …" with length and level, the start checks ("esp32: start check from …") and the reason of every refused connection. The Spark buffers the sound: it starts only with 0.6 seconds ready, keeps at most 0.7 seconds in flight (the board holds 1.2 seconds) and after a stall collects 0.3 seconds again. When speech output could not keep up, *Check* shows "Ton stockte N-mal" and the log "esp32: sound stalled"; if it stutters without that line, the board's Wi-Fi is the cause. When its buffer is full the board waits instead of dropping sound; the connection then slows the Spark down. The board fetches new firmware at its next start (unplug it briefly).

**Speaker page:** Me → Speakers shows "My speakers" at the top (one line per speaker with its state: connected, room mode until, update available) and "Add a speaker" with *Set up a new board over USB* (Chrome/Edge on a PC) and *Board shows a code*. Tapping a speaker opens its page: **Sound** (volume, microphone), **Room mode** ("Listen now" Start/Stop, duration, how much it may say, *What it may offer* with the same seven kinds as in the browser, stored per speaker on the Spark, room in Home Assistant, detect more, and with "Ignore TV voices" also who it listens to and trial), **Voice** (teach "My voice through this speaker"), **Firmware** (version, update now, updates by themselves, rewrite the firmware over USB), **Check** (checks with a readable board name, check network, sound and microphone test, live recent events, board log over USB) and *Remove speaker* at the bottom. Admin: Features → Own speakers shows the firmware state and the address with *Check address* (like "Check network", admin only, at most 6 checks in 10 minutes). Under People and devices and Me → Security speaker keys are marked "Speaker, managed under Me → Speakers" and cannot be moved to another profile.

**Lists for shopping and tasks:** "Put milk on the shopping list", "What is on my to-do list?". Admin switch, off at first, then for every profile. Each of the two lists lives either on the Spark (an iPhone shortcut with the device key fetches new entries via `POST /api/tasks/inbox?list=einkauf`, header `X-Speech-Device`, into the Reminders app; a GET only reads) or in a CalDAV task list of a calendar account (Nextcloud, mailbox.org, Radicale). Adding happens at once, ticking off and deleting by voice only after "Yes". After outside text the assistant adds nothing in the same answer.

**Page Lists:** the lists have their own page in the menu (after E-mail, only with the function) and in the iPhone app under My things → Lists; Me → Overview and Today in the app show the shopping with its number and first entries. Besides shopping and tasks there are up to 20 own lists (name, symbol; rename, delete with entries); "Put screws on the DIY store list" reaches an own list, the assistant never makes an unknown one. Every entry shows who put it on, when and from where (page, app, voice, Telegram, phone, watch, reMarkable, job, …). Ticking off on the page is one tap without a question; ticked off entries stay 14 days under "Recently done" with "Bring back". The shopping list sorts by departments (fixed word list, never the model) and adds up amounts ("milk 2x" and "3 milk" become "milk 5×"). Endpoints: `GET /api/profile/tasks`, `POST /api/profile/listen`, `PUT`/`DELETE /api/profile/listen/{id}`, `POST /api/profile/tasks/{list}` (`/done`, `/undo`), `DELETE /api/profile/tasks/{list}/{entry}`; each with login, profile binding, CSRF, 8 KB and rate limit `listen`; looking and deleting also work while the profile is blocked. Log: lines `tasks:` with fixed words and numbers only (an own list is "own", never its name); Status → Live shows with the calendar only counts of own lists and of entries added and ticked off today. Tests: `app/tests/test_listen.py`.

**Lists like Apple Reminders** (Apple style stage 4, plan "7a"): on the left the search across all lists, four tiles Today (due today or earlier), Scheduled (with a date, by day), All and Flagged, below them the lists with colour (ten fixed colours) and symbol; lists and entries are dragged with the mouse into one's own order, an entry dropped on a list on the left moves there. At the end of every list stands an empty row: type, Enter, the next entry (several with commas). ⓘ on every entry opens beside it (on a phone as a sheet from below) title, note, date, time, remind, flagged, priority (!, !!, !!!) and list; every field saves at once, "Delete" there only for the list's owner. The date is the same field "due" as for tasks from meetings. *Remind* needs date and time and the function *Timers and reminders* (Functions → Timers and reminders): then it becomes a reminder of the profile that set it and rings on its devices like any other; ticking off, deleting or a new time take it back. Ticked off entries stand under "N done · Show". The iPhone app shows the same tiles, colours, the row "New reminder", info per entry and "Edit" to sort. *Lists in Reminders* passes time, note and priority (DUE, DESCRIPTION, PRIORITY) and takes them back from there. Endpoints: `PUT /api/profile/tasks/{list}/{entry}` (info, with `to` move: away only as the owner, there only with write along), `PUT /api/profile/tasks/{list}/order`, `PUT /api/profile/listen/order`, colour via `PUT /api/profile/listen/{id}` (`color`); the same rules as above. Log: `tasks: edit|move|sort …` and `tasks: list order` with numbers only.

**Share lists:** its own admin switch under Functions → Lists for shopping and tasks, off at first; released, it holds for every profile, guests never. The owner of a list shares it on the page Lists with "Share …" (or in the iPhone app) per profile: off, see or add to it (adding and ticking off; deleting, renaming and sharing stay with the owner). Lists from a calendar account (CalDAV) cannot be shared. Shared lists stand under "Shared with me" with "from …", also in the app; by voice: "Put butter on Sabine's shopping list". Whoever a list is not shared with does not learn it exists (same answer as for no list); whoever may only see gets a clear no when adding. Others' entries arrive at most every 10 minutes bundled as a note ("Anton put 3 entries on "Shopping"."), switched off with "Note on new entries" (each profile's setting). The assistant reads shared entries as outside data (`tasks_show` outside), never as a command. Endpoint: `PUT /api/profile/listen/{id}/share` with `{to, level}`, login, profile binding, CSRF, 8 KB, rate limit `listen`; a shared list is `u_….{list}` there. Log: `tasks: list share | ok see|add|off` and `tasks: add einkauf shared | …` with `profiles.log_name`, never names or contents; Status → Live only counts shared lists. Tests: `Teilen` in `app/tests/test_listen.py`.

**Lists in Reminders:** its own admin switch under Functions → Lists for shopping and tasks, off at first; it works with the calendar server for calendar apps (Functions → Calendar and daily briefing). A profile's own lists on the Spark (shopping, tasks, own lists; not a list in a calendar account, never another profile's, no shared one) show as task lists (VTODO) under `/caldav/<profile>/liste-<list>/` in the iPhone's Reminders app (or Thunderbird, DAVx5 with tasks.org), through the same CalDAV account and app password as the Spark calendars. Both ways: putting on, ticking off, bringing back and deleting in Reminders change the list on the Spark and vice versa (ctag and etags). What comes in is data: exactly one VTODO with a title, of it only the title (cut) and whether it is done; else 415; a wrong If-Match 412; at most as many entries as a list holds. An entry from Reminders shows the source "iPhone Reminders" and keeps object name and UID. The door is the calendar server's: app password, only the own path, My emergency stop, rate limit `caldav`. Log: `tasks:` lines with "from calendar app", never the entry; Status → Live counts changes from Reminders with the calendar. Plus a widget "Shopping" for the home and lock screen of the iPhone app: the open entries of the shopping list, reloaded on every list change in the app, else about every 30 minutes (the iPhone's key, only with the lists released). Code: `app/panel/listendav.py`, `ios/SparkWidgets/SparkWidgets.swift`; tests: `app/tests/test_listendav.py`.

**Write link:** its own admin switch under Functions → Lists for shopping and tasks → "Write link", off at first; released, it holds for every profile, only the admin blocks single ones. On the page Lists at an own list "⋯" → "Share …" → "Link for people without a profile": expiry 7 days, 30 days or 1 year, optionally "may see the list", then "Make link". Link (`https://<panel>/#liste=<code>`) and QR code show only once; the Spark keeps only the code's hash (`mitschreib-links.json` in the state folder), at most 20 open links per profile. The link opens a small page with the list's name: entry (several with commas), optionally a name, "Put on"; with "see" also the open entries (texts only). No ticking off, no deleting, no other list, no tool, no model. The entry shows the source "write link" and the typed name, the note to the others says "name (Link)". The assistant reads such entries as outside text. "Revoke" works at once; expiry, a deleted list, a list in a calendar account or the switch turned off end the link as well. Protection: the code stands after the `#` and reaches neither the proxy nor the panel log, other sites (Origin) are refused, its own rate limit, wrong codes lock like a wrong PIN, the owner's Mein Notaus holds every entry off. Log only "tasks: link made 30d | Profil …", "tasks: link refused | bad code"; State → Live counts open links, entries through them and refusals. On the iPhone under Lists → list → "Share …", with QR code and "Pass on the link".

**Everything open in the assistant:** what needs you comes as a **card with buttons into the day's conversation** (the main conversation of "One conversation for all channels"): urgent finds from "For you" (bill, contract, reply, suspicion) and reminders that rang. On the card you deal with it at once ("Done", "Remind me", "Not important", "More of this") or open the place to go on ("Add to calendar …", "Draft a reply …", "Draft a cancellation …"; nothing is sent or saved before that place). "Later" puts the card away: this evening (6 pm, in the evening in three hours), tomorrow morning (8 am), next week (Monday 8 am) or no time; at that time it comes back **once** as "Wieder da: …" into the conversation and as a notification on one device. The **page Open** in the menu (after Lists, only with the function; Android through the panel view) shows *Needs you*, *Later* (by time), *Done* (7 days, with "Bring back"), the list on the left, the card on the right; above the conversation stands "Still open: N", the menu entry carries the number, Me → Overview a card. iPhone app: cards in the conversation, My things → Open, a Today card. Done on one device is done everywhere.
Technology: `vorgaenge.py` keeps `vorgaenge.json` per profile (one Vorgang per key `fuerdich:<id>` or `erinnerung:<id>`, state offen · gesehen · spaeter · erledigt · weg, counter `rev`). The conversation keeps only the number (`vg`) on an assistant message; every page draws the card from the store (`GET /api/profile/vorgaenge?seit=<rev>`; the stream `/api/profile/vorgaenge/stream` sends numbers only). Buttons are fixed names (`vorgaenge.AKTIONEN`); `POST /api/profile/vorgaenge/act` checks owner, kind, the source's rules (`fuerdich.acts`), state (`rev`, else 409 with the new state) and emergency stop, then the source's function runs. For you and the Vorgang are marked together. Only the panel makes a Vorgang, never the model; a sender from a mail stays text. Admin switch "Everything open in the assistant" under Timers and reminders, off at first, then for every profile (can be blocked), guests never. Open → Settings removes the done ones or all; mails and reminders stay.
**Everything open: proposals with a yes button:** when the assistant proposes something that happens only after your yes (a new appointment, moving or deleting one, sending a mail by voice, tidying the mailbox, saving a draft), the card **"Waiting for your yes"** stands under the answer with exactly what would happen (what, when, where, calendar; for a mail to, subject, text). The first button says what it does ("Add", "Move", "Delete", "Send", "Save as draft", "Do it") and acts like a typed "yes"; then the card shows the checked result ("Added to „Privat“"). "Change …" opens the Calendar page's form for an appointment, the E-mail page's writing window for a mail, both filled in; only there is it saved or sent. "Cancel" drops the proposal. The card also stands on the page Open under "Needs you" and in the iPhone app, so you confirm proposals from a speaker or from Telegram on the phone. It lasts as long as the proposal (15 minutes, mail by voice 5 minutes), has no "Later" and closes after an answer in the conversation, a newer proposal or the time ("Answered in the conversation", "New proposal", "Expired"). At the press the panel checks owner, state, emergency stop and the module's switches again (such as "Send mails"); a mail's text stays only while it waits. The card comes with "Everything open in the assistant", also without "Show steps and approvals"; without it the yes/no card of "Show steps and approvals" stays.
**Meeting tasks under Open:** when the Spark companion puts a meeting's tasks onto a list ("Ausgewählte auf die Liste"), every new task also becomes a card "From a meeting" under Open (not in the conversation), with the list, the meeting time and "due" as its deadline. "Done" ticks the list entry off, "Bring back" brings it back, "Not important" only hides the card. The card follows the list: ticked off (page, voice, app, iPhone Reminders) done, taken back open, deleted gone. Overdue tasks stay open. Technology: kind `besprechung` in `vorgaenge.ARTEN`, key `besprechung:<list>:<entry>`, `vorgaenge.from_meeting`, `tasks.card_follows`, `tasks.tick_from_card`.
Technology: `profiles.pending_set` reports every waiting proposal to `vorgaenge.from_pending` (kind `vorschlag`, key `vorschlag:<name>:<time>`, words from the module's `card()`: `calendars`, `senden`, `tidy`). The yes button (`vs_ja`, stage `ja`) calls the same function as the typed "yes" (`calendars.carry`, `senden.carry`, `tidy.carry_out`) and answers `{text, gelungen}`; `vorgaenge.sweep` closes the cards after every answer and every minute. The approval card (`ablauf.waiting`) carries the number (`vg`), the panel and the iPhone then show the card. Live (calendar node) counts waiting proposals and those confirmed by button today.

**Bus and train:** "When does the next bus leave?", "How do I get to Karlsruhe at 8?". Admin switch, off at first, then for every profile. Timetable from transport.rest (default https://v6.db.transport.rest, own address configurable); fixed sentences with delay, platform and cancellations. With a commute (destination, time, days) "Proactive" speaks up 45 to 5 minutes before when the connection is 5 or more minutes late or cancelled. Timetable text counts as outside text.

**Reddit and feeds:** "What's new on Reddit?", "Did anybody reply to me on Reddit?", "What's new in my feeds?". Admin switch Features → Reddit and feeds, off at first, then for every profile, never for guests. Each profile connects its Reddit inbox under Me → Reddit and feeds with the private RSS link (reddit.com → Settings → RSS feeds; needs no API app), adds subreddits (r/LocalLLaMA) and other RSS or Atom feeds (blogs, YouTube channels, Mastodon) and up to 20 keywords. The link carries Reddit's feed key: connecting asks for the code (level "Confirm"), it is kept sealed in the vault and never sent back. The panel fetches each source at most every 30 minutes through the guard (https only, public addresses, 2 MB, after a 429 only after two hours), reads XML only with defusedxml (no entities, no DTD) and decides with fixed rules: the first fetch counts as known; new in the inbox means "a reply to you" and comes at once through "Proactive" ("News from feeds", preference on), a keyword as a whole word in a title at most 3 times a day, everything else into the daily briefing (up to 3 replies and 5 posts of the last 24 hours, keyword hits first, then feed order). Titles, senders and links are outside text: `feeds_news` hands them over as data, after it switching, memory and proposals are locked; the page shows links only with https and `noopener`. The Spark never posts, votes or sends anything to Reddit.

**Agent functions:** the assistant works longer for you. Admin: Features → "Jobs" on and a level per profile: *off* (default), *read and report only* or *also act after "Yes"*. The profile sees its jobs under Me → Jobs. Never guests, other voices or ESP32 speakers.
- *Background jobs:* "Research which heat pump fits the pool and let me know." The job runs in the background (search, read pages, with "my data" also appointments, reminders, documents), then a notification comes by browser, iPhone app and Telegram (as set under Me → Notifications). The report is under Me → Jobs and in the log, on request as a document under "My documents" (switch "Also keep every report under "Documents"" or "As document" at the job). Limits: one job at a time on the Spark, it waits while someone talks to the assistant; 8 rounds, 4 searches, 5 pages, 12 tool calls, 15 minutes, 20,000 characters; 3 waiting and 20 jobs per day and profile. Reading tools only; the report counts as outside text and is never learned.
- *Scheduled jobs:* "Sum up my appointments of the week every Monday at 7." The assistant reads the plan back in fixed words, it is saved only after "Yes". Once, daily, weekdays, weekly or monthly, at most 10, pause and delete under Me → Jobs. If the Spark was off for more than 3 hours at the planned time, that run is skipped.
- *Watching and own tasks:* "Let me know when there is new firmware for my FRITZ!Box." or "Tell me as soon as the flight to Rome costs less than 100 euros." The panel recognizes the sentence itself, reads the watch out in fixed words (name, how often, price limit) and makes it only after "Yes"; default daily at 8. Each run is a reading job; the model only names candidates (`FUND: value | address | quote`). A find is new only when the panel checks: page read in this run or returned by the search, quote stands there word for word, value is in the quote, amount below the price limit, value not yet known. Only then a notification in fixed words (value and address) goes out, otherwise only the history (14 days) records the run. After 5 errors in a row or 90 days without anything new the watch pauses itself and says why. At most 10 per profile. Change by voice ("Watch that every Monday instead", after "Yes", can be undone) or under Me → Jobs → Own tasks: one list with search and filters for watches, scheduled jobs, repeating reminders, smart home rules of "Speaking up by itself" and important mail senders; every row has a switch (off = paused, stays saved), the detail has change, "Check now", "Forget what is known", history and delete. A paused reminder rings on no device. The iPhone app shows watches and scheduled jobs under "By itself" with a switch and delete. Admin: Features → Jobs → "Watching" (off), for every profile with agent functions, never for guests. Logs: `agent: watch run | result neu|still|bericht|fehler | new … | dropped …`.
- *Jobs with plan, questions and proposals:* a job has three tools that only concern itself. `job_plan` writes its steps as a list (at most 6) and ticks them off; Me → Jobs shows it live. `job_ask` asks one question with up to 4 answers to tap and waits up to 10 minutes, only in jobs you started yourself (scheduled jobs and watching never ask); the notification only says that a question waits. The panel refuses questions about passwords, PINs or codes. `job_propose` makes one proposal at most: a reminder, an appointment or an entry on your shopping or task list, never smart home, mail or anything outside your profile. The job carries out nothing: under the report the proposal stands in full in fixed words (with a warning when outside text was read before) and "Yes" / "No". Only your yes carries it out, only unchanged (checksum), open and at most 24 hours old; emergency stop and the function's release apply as always. With "Add" you add to a running job up to three times; the sentence reaches the model in its next step as your own words. All also in the iPhone app (open the job); Android shows the panel page. Admin: Features → Jobs → "Jobs with plan, questions and proposals" (off), for every profile with agent functions, never for guests. Logs only fixed words: `agent: job plan | steps … | done …`, `agent: job asks`, `agent: job question answered|unanswered|refused`, `agent: job proposal saved|done|declined|failed|refused`, `agent: job steer taken|refused`. Self-tests `test_auftragsplan`.
- *Routines (level "also act" only):* "Remember as routine Evening: terrace light on, pool to 36 degrees." After "Yes", later "Evening" is enough. Every step is a Home Assistant sentence and runs like a spoken command (same allowlist, same code word); at most 8 steps and 20 routines.
- *Services over MCP:* switch "Services over MCP" below "Agent functions". The admin enters name, address (Streamable HTTP) and token (login plus a fresh code; token in the vault, never sent back). Every tool is off until the admin sets it to "read" or "act"; profiles are ticked per service. Reading answers count as outside text; acting tools only propose and run after "Yes". If a service changes a tool's description or inputs, it is off again after "Fetch tools again". The Spark never starts a program itself; home network addresses only with the permission under Security.
- *Own MCP services:* switch "Own MCP services for profiles" below "Services over MCP". Every profile connects up to five own services under Me → My MCP services (fresh code, key encrypted) and releases every tool itself ("Read" or "Act after yes"). Optionally in background jobs (read only) and with sign-in (OAuth). The admin sees all of them under Apps and interfaces and can block each at once. Details: [APIs and integration](apis-and-integration.md).

**Chats with other Sparks:** write to people on another Spark by their address. Admin: Features → Chats → "Chats with other Sparks" (off), below it this Spark's address from outside (e.g. `speech.example.de`, the name only, reachable over https; without it the function stays off) and "Who may connect": only paired Sparks (default) or every Spark like e-mail. Pairing: "Make a pairing code" (valid one day and once, `spark.example.de#…`), the other Spark's admin enters it under "Code of another Spark"; both compare the fingerprint in "Connected Sparks" (there Allow, Block, Remove, contacts, in/out, waiting). No profile switch: for everyone with Chats, Chats → ＋ New chat → "🌐 Someone on another Spark" shows the own address (`name@spark.example.de`, with QR code and Share), a field for a request and the contacts. Requests to me show on top of the chat list as "Contact requests" with Accept, Decline, Block; only after Accept does a message get through, Decline tells the other Spark nothing (no new request from there for 30 days), at most 20 open requests per person. The chat for two carries 🌐: ticks (✓✓ = the other Spark has the message), read receipts, replies, reactions, change, take back. Technically every Spark has its own Ed25519 key (derived from the Spark's key, moves only with a move backup), public at `/.well-known/spark-verbund`. Every message is an HTTPS POST to the other Spark's `/api/verbund/in`, signed together with the receiving domain, with a time (±10 minutes) and a one-time number; a different key later is refused. Outbound only to public addresses (Wächter), nothing during the emergency stop. When the other Spark is away, retries follow after 1 and 5 minutes, then hourly, at most 7 days, then ⚠ "not delivered". What comes from another Spark is someone else's text: never to the language model or a tool, the own speaker can read it out. The other Spark only learns address, display name and message and gets the same answer to every request, whether the person exists or not. Live (tile Chats): "🌐 Verbund: Sparks, contacts, in/out today, waiting"; logs (area "Messages and chats"): `verbund: sent`, `verbund: refused signature`, `verbund: retry`, never a domain, name or text. The service watch checks "Reachable for other Sparks". The iPhone app shows such chats with 🌐; asking and accepting work in the browser.
**Photos and files to other Sparks:** Admin: Features → Chats → Chats with other Sparks → "Photos and files to other Sparks" (off, needs "Photos, files and voice in chats"). Then photos, files and voice messages go too, with the same buttons. The own Spark sends the file first, alone and signed (a head with time, one-time number, SHA-256 and size in a header), to `/api/verbund/blob`, then the message with the hash. The other Spark takes a file only from an allowed Spark with a known key (checked before a byte is read), exactly with the head's size and hash, at most 500 MB per Spark and day and 2 GB waiting, keeps it sealed until the message (at most a day) and makes it anew like an own upload: a photo drawn again (without place), a file recognised by its content (else nothing arrives), a voice message encoded again and written down by its own speech recognition. None of it goes to the language model. When the other Spark takes no files, the message shows ⚠. Live: tile Chats "files today n in / n out"; logs: "verbund: file in | kb 812", "verbund: refused file limit", "verbund: files dropped | n 1", numbers only.

**Groups with other Sparks:** Admin: Features → Chats → Chats with other Sparks → "Groups with other Sparks" (off, needs "Group chats"). Group admins also pick accepted contacts on other Sparks (🌐) when creating it or under "Add people". The group belongs to the Spark it was created on: only there its admins change name and members, and it passes every message on to the other Sparks (once per Spark, signed, same queue as chats for two). On the others it reads "managed on another Spark"; leaving works there, the home Spark hears it. Text only; photos, editing and taking back do not work in these groups. At most 10 other Sparks per group. Nothing goes to the language model; logs with numbers only ("verbund: group …"), Status → Live shows the number of groups from here and from others.

**Messages in chats:** messages to others go through the chats. Admin: Features → "Chats" (off), below it "Message to everybody" and "Announcements on speakers" (both off); once released they work for every profile. Me → Conversation → Chats holds "Who may write to me" (every profile with chats or only picked ones, plus Blocked and the own call name), "New messages on my speaker" (nothing, only who wrote, who wrote and read the text on request) and "Message to everybody" (on, can be switched off). Guests can neither send nor receive.
- Sending: "Tell Anna dinner is ready", "Write sb that I'll be late" or "Message to Ben: …". The panel reads recipient and text itself (fixed rules), searches only among profiles that take messages from you, reads the message back and writes it into the chat only after a plain "Yes" (marked 🎙). When chats are off for you, the assistant asks whether to switch them on; your "Yes" switches on only your profile. When sending is not possible it names the reason.
- Message to everybody: "Tell everybody we leave at eight" goes into the fixed group "Alle", which holds everybody who did not switch off "Message to everybody"; nobody adds or removes members, no admins. "Stop taking part" under ⓘ switches it off for you. At most 5 messages to everybody per hour.
- On the speaker: when no page of the profile is open, the own speaker says a new chat message at once ("Nachricht von Anna."), else at the next wake word within two hours (at most 3). Never in quiet hours, never from muted chats, from private chats only who wrote. It never reads the text into the room unasked: with "read the text on request" it adds "Sag „Lies die Nachricht vor“, dann lese ich sie dir vor.", and the text is read only to the profile's recognized voice (speaker ID on, voice taught; otherwise the offer is left out).
- Announcement: "Announcement in the living room: dinner is ready" (or "everywhere") after "Yes", or Chats → ＋ → "📢 Announcement": pick speakers, type the text. Only on speakers whose owner allows announcements; one not connected says it at its next wake word within ten minutes.
- Reading and replying: "Do I have new messages?" reads unread chat messages as outside text, never as an instruction; "Reply to her: coming" and "Yes". A message's content locks everything that changes something for the rest of the answer and the next turn, and never goes into the memory.
- iPhone: "Message with Spark" (Siri) writes into the chat, "everybody" into the group "Alle"; the Today card "Chats" counts unread chat messages.
- Logs (area "Messages and chats") and Live (tile "Chats", with "Alle: N people" and "N read aloud") show numbers only. The "Who may write to me" lists (`messages.json`) are part of the backup.

**Chats:** writing like in a messenger, everything on the Spark. Admin: Features → "Chats", below it "Group chats" (both off); released for every profile. Under Me → Conversation → Chats each profile sets "Read receipts" (on) and "Text in the notification" (off). The menu has "Chats" (on a phone the "Chats" tab with the number of unread messages): the list on the left, the chat on the right; on a phone two pages with "‹" back. Ticks: ✓ sent, ✓✓ arrived on a device, blue ✓✓ read (only when both have read receipts on). Tap a message: reaction (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢 🙏), reply, copy, change (15 minutes), take back for all (1 hour). ⓘ: mute (1 hour, 8 hours, always), "messages disappear" (24 hours, 7 or 90 days; in groups the admins set it), clear the chat for me, in groups name, members, admins and leave. Only who the other person takes may write (Me → Conversation → Chats → "Who may write to me": from whom, blocked); the sender only hears "does not take messages from you right now", never why. "Tell Anna dinner is ready" lands in the chat with Anna (marked 🎙), "Do I have new messages?" reads unread messages as foreign text, never as an instruction. Texts, group names and previews are stored encrypted with AES-256-GCM under `users/_chats/` (key derived from the Spark's key, each message bound to its chat and number) and are part of the backup. An open Chats page gets changes at once over a connection that carries numbers only; otherwise a notification goes to every set-up device (iPhone, Android, companion, browser), only "💬 name" and "New message", never through Telegram, not when muted or in quiet hours. Limits: 4000 characters per message, 240 messages per hour, 50 people per group. When a profile is deleted, its messages disappear from every chat. Logs (area "Messages and chats") and Live (tile "Chats": chats, messages today, open connections, notifications) show numbers only.

**Photos, files and voice in chats:** Admin: Features → Chats → "Photos, files and voice in chats" (off). Then the chat shows 📎 on the left (photo or file) and 🎤 on the right (voice message: tap, speak, ➤ sends, "Discard" throws it away; after 5 minutes it sends by itself). Photos: JPEG, PNG or WebP up to 15 MB, saved anew by the Spark (at most 2048 pixels, preview 480 pixels), without place and camera data. Files: PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument and text (txt, csv, md) up to 25 MB, recognised by content, never by name; the name is cleaned and gets the extension of the real type. Files are only for download (never opened in the panel). Voice messages are stored as AAC (plays everywhere, iPhone included), speech recognition writes what was said underneath. Everything is stored encrypted (AES-256-GCM, bound to chat, message and part) under `users/_chats/<chat>/`, at most 2 GB per person; taken back, disappeared and deleted messages take their files with them. The file name travels in the request header, search words in the body (POST), so nothing lands in the access log. "Do I have new messages?" names photos and files only as "📷 Photo" or "📄 File". Live (tile "Chats") shows count and size, never names.

**Invite into a chat:** Admin: Features → Chats → "Invite into a chat" (off) and People and devices → New people "only by invitation". Then Chats → ＋ New chat shows "Invite someone": a name as a suggestion, "Make invitation", QR code and link (copy or share). The invited person picks a name and PIN and lands in the chat with you at once ("… joined with your invitation"); chats are on for them, both take messages from each other (unless someone is blocked). Each invitation works 7 days and once, at most 3 open per person; the admin sees them under New people and can withdraw them. When the switch is off or the admin blocks the inviting person, the link stops working. The new profile gets what the admin released, never a role; the second sign-in step applies as for every invitation.

**Private chats, end-to-end:** Admin: Features → Chats → "Private chats (end-to-end)" (off). Then Chats → ＋ New chat shows "🔒 Private" beside every person. The first time, the device gets its own key (ECDH P-256, made in the browser, not exportable, in IndexedDB); that counts like a new device and needs the second step's code. The device encrypts every message itself (AES-256-GCM, the message key wrapped for every added device of both people, HKDF over ECDH); photos are drawn anew first (no place or camera data), files and voice messages go with their own key. The Spark stores only the encrypted form and cannot read it; the assistant only says "private message", the search finds nothing, notifications say "🔒 New message". Under ⓘ are the safety number (12 × 5 digits, compare once face to face) and "Your devices" with Remove; when a device is added or removed, a line shows in the chat. History for a new device: when the person already has a device, the new one waits a day for the history and shows a 6-character code in the private chat; an older device shows "🔒 New device" on top of Chats with the same code and "Transfer" or "Not me" (takes the new device out at once). "Transfer" wraps each message key once more for the new device on the old one; the Spark stores only that wrap (marked with the device that made it) and never changes the encrypted text. The code comes from the new device's public key: a slipped-in key would give another code. The Spark takes wraps only from an older own device of the same person, once per new device and only within that day. Without an older device a new device only reads what comes after it. Needs https. Browser, Android app and iPhone app (key in the Secure Enclave, the same encryption with CryptoKit, also safety number and history transfer; the app key reaches the private chat paths only while the iPhone app is on, other device keys never). Live: tile Chats "🔒 private, devices, today, history transfer: n waiting"; logs: "private key added", "sent | kind priv", "private history shared | n 120", "private history refused", numbers only.
**Calls, voice and video:** Admin: Features → Chats → "Calls (voice and video)" (off). Then every chat of two on this Spark (ordinary or 🔒 private, not with other Sparks) shows 📞 and 🎥 at the top. Every open Chats page of the other person rings, their devices get "📞 Name is calling"; they answer with or without video or decline, and their other devices stop ringing. Sound and picture go encrypted over WebRTC (DTLS-SRTP) straight from device to device; the Spark only brings the two together (offer, answer, network candidates, checked for form and size only, never stored or logged) and passes each signal only to the one device holding the call on the other side. Fixed rules: both have Chats and Calls, the other person takes messages from the caller, nothing rings in their quiet hours or with "Do not disturb" (missed), whoever is on a call is busy, 20 calls per hour, 45 seconds of ringing, at most 4 hours. Every call leaves one line in the chat ("📞 Call · 4:12", "Missed call", "Call declined"). When the admin switches calls off, running calls end within a minute. Browser, Android app (with camera from 1.3.1) and iPhone app with Apple's call screen (see iphone-app.md, "Voice and video calls"); iPhones ring through a VoIP push from Apple that carries only the call's random number, the app asks the Spark who calls (`GET /api/anrufe/info`, only for the two in the call), without a second "is calling" notification. Live counts "rang on the iPhone". Running and ringing calls show as connections "Anruf im Chat" and "Anruf klingelt" (count only) on Live, the summary and the HDMI Steuerzentrale, under devices one entry per call from ringing to hanging up with both sides: devices ("Browser ↔ iPhone-App"), voice or video, ringing or connected ("Videoanruf · verbunden"), both people ("Anna ↔ Tom"), each name only with that profile's consent (else "Person"), never the chat.
**Calls by the assistant (`rufen.py`):** Admin: Features → Chats → "Calls by the assistant" (off, needs "Calls (voice and video)"). The assistant understands "Ruf Sabine an", "Videoanruf mit Sabine", "Call Sabine", "Video call Sabine", "Can you call Ben?". The panel reads name and kind (voice, video, phone) from the person's own words by fixed rules; no tool for the model, the model only says the sentence. The name works as for messages (name or call name, several matches get the question with buttons) and only for someone who takes calls from the caller (`anrufe.why_not`). Only the "Yes" on the same device in the same conversation calls (approval card "Call"); "Yes, with video" or "voice only" changes the kind, anything else drops the proposal. After the yes the panel opens the chat of the two; browser, Android app (page) and iPhone app start the call at once (event `anruf`). Asked at a speaker, on the phone, over Telegram (with personal data), at the watch, with Siri, CarPlay or the companion, the call waits three minutes on the Spark: every device of the profile gets "📞 Sabine anrufen" (at most 6 per hour, never over Telegram), the next page or app opened shows "Sabine anrufen? · Anrufen · Nicht jetzt" at the bottom (iPhone: a question when opened). "Anrufen" starts it, "Nicht jetzt" removes it on every device. "By phone": when the name fits no profile or the person says "übers Telefon"/"by phone", the iPhone app calls with the iPhone contacts (`iphone_action`); on Android the panel looks the name up in the own address book (Contacts → Settings, exactly one contact with a number, mobile first) and after the yes opens the phone app with the number (event `waehlen`, Android app 1.4.2 and later); only the tap on the green button dials. Never for guests, someone else's voice or under the Notaus. Spark diary: "Call started". Live, tile Chats: "per Assistent heute … gefragt, … gestartet, … aufs Handy, … übers Telefon, … abgelehnt" (numbers only). Journal: `chats: call assistant proposed|started here|sent to devices|phone app opened|declined|refused | why …`, never a name or a number.
**Calls when away:** Admin: Features → Chats → "Calls when away" (off) and the address from outside (empty: the address for other Sparks). Then the Spark runs its own relay (coturn, unit speech-spark-turn, only while the switch is on; the distribution's coturn service stays masked). In the router forward UDP 3478 and UDP 49160 to 49200 to the Spark. Each device gets an access per call that expires after an hour (TURN REST, secret derived from the Spark's key); the relay only passes packets to public addresses, never into the home network or to the Spark itself, without console, with limits per person and in total, without log. Status → Services shows "Relay for calls". Live: tile Chats "📞 Calls: running, ringing, today, relay on/off"; logs: "call started | video 1", "call answered | secs 4", "call ended | secs 252", "call not answered | why rejected", "call refused | why limit", "call relay started", numbers and fixed words only. The relay takes the address from outside only as a public IPv4 address (an extra IPv6 record, often an fd… home address, does not get in the way). When the Spark cannot find it for a moment, a running relay keeps its last address ("call relay kept | why not found", Live "alte Adresse"); otherwise Live names the reason ("Adresse von außen zeigt ins Heimnetz", "Notaus: kein Internet", "Adresse von außen nicht gefunden") and the log says it once per reason and panel start, "call relay refused | why home address"; when it is back: "call relay back". **Reason "address from outside points into the home network":** inside the home network the name only gives the Spark a home address (mostly a local DNS entry in the router, such as the UniFi console's local DNS records, or on the NAS). The relay needs the public IPv4 address and does not start otherwise; this is a correct refusal, not a bug: under Features → My day → Calls when away enter a name that gives the public address also at home (e.g. an own DynDNS name without a local entry), or remove the local entry. Status → Logs shows a fixed hint for it.

**No envelope in the assistant:** there is no envelope beside the input and no "Message" button above the conversation; you write under Chats, "Tell Anna …" goes into the chat.

**Many profiles:** under People and devices the admin searches by name, call name or device, filters with a segment (All, Unused = not used for 90 days, No device, No 2FA = without second step), sorts (name, last used, newest) and pages through 25 at a time. "Details" shows each profile's devices, last use, call name, change PIN, reset second step and delete; deleting needs the fresh code like changing the PIN. The device list shows only the devices of the profiles shown. The profile of an own device key (script, shortcut) and of a new device is picked from a list of all profiles; the iPhone app, speakers, Pebble watch and Home Assistant keys are set up by each profile itself under Me, show "🔒 fixed" with where they are managed, and can only be deleted here (the server refuses moving them too). "Last used" counts the browser login (without the address) and device keys. **Call name:** optional per profile ("Tom", "Dad"), set by the admin in the details or by the profile under Me → Conversation → Chats; unique among all names and call names. **Same names:** when several profiles fit (two "Thomas"), the assistant asks "Thomas Müller or Thomas Meier?" (at most 4); the answer ("Müller", "the first", call name) picks only among these, then comes the "Ja". When more than 4 fit it asks for the full name. An unknown name gets at most 3 similar ones, never the whole list; "to everybody" names at most 4 and "and N more".

**Home Assistant Assist over Wyoming:** Home Assistant uses the Spark's speech recognition and voice (Voice PE, HA app, ESPHome speakers). Admin: Features → "Home Assistant Assist (Wyoming)" on and enter Home Assistant's address (Wyoming has no password; with no address the Spark answers nobody). The field suggests the address from a profile's Home Assistant setup (home network addresses only; behind a reverse proxy enter the real HA address by hand) and shows addresses refused lately. In HA: integration "Wyoming Protocol", host = the Spark, port 31003; in the voice assistant pick "spark" for speech-to-text and text-to-speech. The service runs inside the panel and needs the full install. Status → Live shows every transcription and spoken answer for Home Assistant as a request of the device "Home Assistant" (way Hearing → text to Home Assistant, or Speaking → back), refused senders under Entrance; never the words. The Spark answers "describe" at once with the voice list from memory (read again in the background every 10 minutes), because HA waits only 1 second (else "Unable to get info").

**Calling the Spark (phone):** call the Spark from a phone in the house (DECT handset of the FRITZ!Box, IP phone) and talk to it like to an own speaker. The Spark signs in at the telephone system as an IP phone (`app/panel/telefon.py`, a small SIP part of its own in the panel, no foreign software): REGISTER with digest sign-in (MD5 or SHA-256), incoming calls with INVITE, ACK, BYE, CANCEL, OPTIONS and INFO; sound as RTP in G.722 (HD, 16 kHz) or G.711 (a-law, µ-law), own code in `app/panel/telefon_ton.py`; keys after RFC 4733. Admin: *Features → Phone* on; below, a wizard in four steps: 1. pick the system or "Search the home network" (asks `fritz.box`, `speedport.ip` and the gateway with SIP OPTIONS, home network only), 2. the clicks to add the Spark there as an IP phone, with a suggested user name and password to copy, 3. enter the access (address, port, user name, password in the vault; saving asks for the code, level "confirm") and "Check connection" with a plain answer ("User name or password wrong", "The system does not answer" …), 4. call the Spark's internal number from a handset (FRITZ!Box usually \*\*620): the calling number shows up and is assigned to a profile or as a "Shared phone". Templates (FRITZ!Box, Telekom Speedport, Gigaset, Asterisk/FreePBX, 3CX, UniFi Talk, other SIP system) are in `app/telefonanlagen.json`; a new system is a new entry. A call: "Hallo Anna, hier ist der Spark.", question, answer (Weiche and chat as at a speaker, short, without lists), listening again; after 20 seconds of silence "Noch etwas?", after another 10 seconds it hangs up; "Stopp" or # interrupts the answer; a goodbye ("Tschüss", "Danke, das war's", "Ich lege auf", also in the middle of an answer) and right after "Noch etwas?" a "Nein danke" end the call: "Tschüss!", then the Spark hangs up (fixed word lists, never the model). The phone's BYE ends the call at once; if none arrives, the Spark hangs up after 8 seconds without a sound packet, unless the phone holds the call (log `telefon: kein ton mehr vom telefon seit 8 s, aufgelegt`); at most 15 minutes. Against choppy sound a thread of its own sends at a 20 ms beat, also while the panel is busy; speech starts only once 200 ms are ready or the sentence is complete, after every gap it buffers more (at most 0.8 s). The log line `telefon: aufgelegt nach …` names gaps, late packets and `kodieren … ms je sekunde ton` at the end. Fixed rules decide who is calling: a profile's handset uses that profile, personal things only when the speaker recognition clearly knows the voice (as at a shared speaker); at the shared phone the voice picks the profile per question, else questions and answers as a guest; an unassigned handset only hears that it belongs to no profile yet (with *Phone: take guests* questions and answers as a guest, never a tool); calls from outside are refused, except to confirm a number (*Me → Phone*) and with *Features → Phone → Calls from outside*. One call at a time (up to 2), another caller hears "busy". Sound is never stored; questions and answers are in the profile's history ("Telefon DD.MM."). Status → Live shows the telephone system with signed in yes/no and today's numbers, Logs the area "telefon" (only internal numbers like \*\*611, never a number from outside, profiles through `profiles.log_name`), the Dienst-Wächter reports a lost sign-in. Self-test `test_telefon` with a made-up telephone system.

**Me → Phone:** each profile sets up under *Me → Phone* what belongs to it (`app/panel/telefon_ich.py`, file `users/<id>/telefon.json`, numbers and PIN in the vault). *My phone numbers*: mobile and landline (at most 5; adding asks for the code, level "confirm"). A number counts only once confirmed: the page shows a 4-digit code (valid 15 minutes), you call the Spark from that number and type it; after three wrong ones it is void. Only for this does the Spark take a call from outside. So nobody puts someone else's number on their profile, and a number belongs to at most one profile (`0170…` and `+49170…` count as the same). *Phone PIN*: 4 to 8 digits, not all the same, not in a row; changing only with a fresh code (level "change protection"). On the phone you type it and #: on the own handset it stands in for the voice for the rest of the call, at the shared phone it picks its profile. Three wrong ones in 15 minutes lock the PIN at that phone for 30 minutes, the handset's profile gets a notification. The PIN is a salted PBKDF2 hash, never spoken, shown, given to the language model or logged; when the Spark does not clearly know the voice at the own handset, it says on personal questions that the PIN helps. *My handsets* shows the handsets the admin assigned, *My calls* time, length, kind and number of questions of each call (no number, no words), deletable one by one or all. The page is in `features.ME_PAGES` and so also in the iPhone and Android app. Live counts right and wrong PINs and confirmed numbers, Logs only fixed words (`telefon: pin richtig …`, `telefon: nummer bestaetigt …`, never a number, code or PIN).

**Calls from outside:** with *Features → Phone → Calls from outside* (default off) the Spark takes calls the telephone system puts through from outside (in the FRITZ!Box choose the numbers the IP phone "Spark" rings for). Fixed rules in `telefon.Call.outside`: a number confirmed under *Me → Phone* picks its profile; because shown numbers can be forged, personal things only with a sure voice or after the phone PIN, and the greeting names nobody. Unknown or hidden numbers are refused; with *Unknown callers as guests* (the same switch as for unassigned handsets) they get questions and answers as a guest, never a tool, at most 20 a day; a PIN makes no profile out of them. If the number's profile is blocked, the call is refused. Logs only "telefon: anruf extern bekannt als …", "… unbekannt als gast" or "… abgelehnt", never the number; Live counts calls from outside and guests, *My calls* shows them as "From outside".

**Calls in Live, Summary and the control centre:** every phone call stands as one request under the devices from picking up to hanging up, as "Telefon" (with the handset's name when the profile allows names in Live), reason "Anruf" or "Spark ruft an"; never a number. The questions in the call are requests of their own but stand on their call's card (one card per call, e.g. "db · Anruf · 2 questions"), their ways start there; the list below shows them one by one.

**Troubleshooting the phone:** *A router between the Spark and the PBX:* if both are in different networks with NAT in between (e.g. FRITZ!Box → UniFi Dream Machine → home network), the Spark sees it in the answer to its sign-in (Via "received"), signs in again with the router's address and names it for the sound too (log `telefon: nat zwischen spark und anlage`). The wizard under Features → Phone then says what the router needs: forward UDP 31060 and 31062-31069 to the computer with the panel, best only from the PBX's address, and switch SIP ALG off; this means the second router (e.g. the UniFi box behind the FRITZ!Box), not the FRITZ!Box. Without these forwards no call from outside reaches the Spark, and a test call rings without sound. Simpler: put the Spark into the same network as the PBX. The line `telefon: nat zwischen spark und anlage, anlage sieht port 31060` is a hint, not an error, and comes once per address and panel start. Every call ends in the log with `telefon: aufgelegt nach … s, … fragen (reason), ton raus N pakete, davon M mit sprache, rein K pakete`. M zero: no speech output (before it stands `telefon: sprachausgabe` with the kind of error or `leer`); K zero: the PBX sends no sound. If the PBX refuses a call of the Spark, its answer code is logged (`telefon: anruf raus antwort 403`). If the PBX describes the sound only in the early answer (183), the Spark takes that one.

**The Spark calls:** with *Features → Phone → The Spark calls* (default off) the Spark places calls itself (`app/panel/telefon_anruf.py` decides with fixed rules, `telefon.Call.dial` dials: INVITE with digest sign-in, ringing up to 30 seconds, else CANCEL). *Wake-up call and reminder by call*, only ordered by the profile itself: "Call me tomorrow at 7" or "Remind me by call at 3 pm about the dentist" (tool `anruf_planen`, "Have the Spark call"; it only gets time, kind and text, never a number). The wake-up call says "Guten Morgen, hier ist der Spark. Es ist 7 Uhr.", then you can keep talking; a reminder is read only once the voice fits or the phone PIN was typed (if there is neither voice nor PIN to check, at once), else it comes as a note. *Call back*: whoever calls from on the go with an unsure voice can say "Ruf mich zurück" instead of the PIN; the Spark hangs up and calls the stored, confirmed number matching the call. A forged number thus never reaches the forger, and the call back counts like the PIN for that call. *Call on outages* (its own switch): if a service of the service watch is gone 10 minutes or longer, the Spark calls the admin besides the note, at most once an hour, never between 10 pm and 7 am (like "Only collect at night", always for calls), never in the admin's quiet hours or during "Do not disturb"; if the service is still gone afterwards, the call comes then (log `telefon: alarm in ruhezeit, nur push` and `telefon: alarm nach ruhezeit angerufen`). *Whom*: the profile's assigned handset; with *Whom the Spark calls → also confirmed own numbers* else the confirmed number (needed for the call back). Never a number from the conversation, a mail or the language model. *How often*: at most 5 calls per profile and day, never during "Do not disturb" (ordered calls also ring in the own quiet hours); not answered once more after 5 minutes, then a note. Every profile can switch off "The Spark may call me" under *Me → Phone → Calls from the Spark* and see and delete planned calls there; *My calls* shows them as "The Spark called". In wizard step 4 "Spark calls" calls an internal number with a test sentence. Live counts calls out, answered ones, call backs, alarm calls and planned ones; logs name kind, profile and result, never a number from outside or a reminder text. There is deliberately no confirmation call to an unconfirmed number (the Spark would otherwise call any number typed in); confirming works by a call from that number.

**Weather place:** postcode and place ("76307 Karlsbad") work; the name is searched in Germany and the postcode compared. When several places fit, the others can be picked; coordinates ("48.87, 8.50") work too.

**Web search:** switch on "Websuche" under Features and enter the address of your SearXNG instance below it. If SearXNG's `settings.yml` allows `json` under `search: formats:`, the panel uses the JSON API, otherwise the normal results page; "Test connection" shows whether it works. The LLM searches by itself when a question needs current information (the LLM server needs tool calling; the own language model has it on). The assistant says it is looking it up, reads the top pages and shows the sources under the answer. When the search is off or has no address, the assistant says so; the log has a line "chat: web search …" per question (offered, off, no address, locked). Asking in so many words ("such im Internet", "google mal", "recherchier", "schau online nach", "search the web") always goes through the search. After reading a mail the search is locked for the rest of that answer (a mail could otherwise carry content to the web). An answer from mail (also a proactive mail note) stays in the next turn only when the new question is about it ("what else does she write?", "read the first one"); then the search stays locked and the assistant says so. Any other question ("what is going on in politics today?") leaves the mail answer out and searches.

**Wake word "Hey Spark":** with the checkbox below the chat the assistant keeps listening. On "Hey Spark" it listens; "Hey Spark, what's the weather?" in one go is taken as the question.
