Then the narration may be up to 1,600 characters (`ERZ_MOMENT`, else 900, the opening of a round, chapter or act 1,500), with the fixed task to tell the moment in 3 to 4 paragraphs with every sense. **Way of telling per round:** brief, normal or rich (×0.7, ×1, ×1.6 on every limit, `WEISEN`), when creating under "The round" and for the host in the tab "Leiten" under "📜 Way of telling", from the next turn on. Endpoint `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/erzaehler` with `{weise}` (host, login, profile, CSRF, size and rate limit; an unknown word is refused). Longer narrations take a little more time; the turn's clock waits for the reading. Log `dungeon: laenge | ort | 1600` (or `normal`), `dungeon: erzaehlweise | knapp`. Self-test `Fight.test_longer_at_the_moments_and_the_rounds_way_of_telling`. **The encounter matches the text:** the model must name every chosen foe by its name from `angebot`; if one is missing it rewrites once with the note "gegner" (`dungeon_erzaehler.judge`), log `dungeon: nachbessern | gegner`. The encounter card wears the table theme's colours (light and dark), picture, name, count and values are always visible; "Stufe ¼" is the challenge rating from the bestiary, the tooltip explains it. **Tap on the picture:** in the hero sheet a tap on a tile's picture (What I can do, Bag, Equipment) opens its window too. Self-tests `Fight.test_the_narration_names_the_foes_of_the_card`, `Fight.test_a_picture_never_carries_a_buttons_attribute` and browser test `Windows.test_hero_sheet_merchant_and_item_windows`. **NPC lines once and without names:** when a side character's line already stands in the text, even split over two quotes ("You do not understand", he stammers, "…"), the panel reads it only there in the character's voice and does not show it again as a block (`dungeon_vorlesen.in_text`, `npc[].im_text`); a line not in the text the character speaks without its name in front. In the car every line comes once. Self-test `ReadAloud.test_an_npc_line_once_and_never_its_name`. **The voice fits "er" and "sie":** when "…", warnt er leise follows a quote, the panel takes as speaker only someone named before whose gender does not contradict it, and speaks a quote without a named speaker in the voice that fits the pronoun (`dungeon_vorlesen.pronoun`, `stimmen.sex`). Self-test `ReadAloud.test_the_pronoun_after_a_quote_picks_the_voices_gender`. **Scene pictures on their own:** after a turn the panel paints the scene picture by itself when the place changes, a fight or a new act begins, or 5 turns passed without a picture (`auto_scene`, `BILD_AUTO`); with the model's picture text, else from place and mood. The limits of Painting pictures hold (one picture every 3 turns, 12 per round, the day's limit, none in an admin's round). "🖼 Paint the scene now" stays. Log `dungeon: bild | auto | ort | ok` (or with a reason such as `abstand`). Self-test `Pictures.test_the_panel_paints_on_its_own`. **A find's name fits the thing:** the model may give a find its own name, the panel picks the thing from the catalogue and checks the name by fixed rules (`name_fits`): a proper name like "Glockenbrecher" for a sword stays; when a word for a find's place is in it (chest, crate, cache, hiding place, tomb … in `_FUNDORT`), it stays only when it names the thing too ("Truhenbrecher-Dolch"); otherwise the thing is called as in the catalogue and the model's name stands with it as "Found in: Versiegelte Truhe" (`own_name`, field `fund`). Finds in rounds, stored heroes and loot lines are repaired the same way when read. "rare" is the middle of the five rarities (common, uncommon, rare, epic, legendary). Self-test `Equipment.test_a_find_keeps_its_name_only_when_it_names_the_thing`.

**AI Dungeon Master: the voice only by fixed traits, also when someone goes on talking:** The gender of a quote's voice comes only from fixed traits: first the character's kept one (`npc_stimmen`), else the pronoun after the quote ("…", says he), else none; then the NPC voice speaks as a person (`offen`), never guessed from the name. A quote that only goes on (»Kein Fremder hier«, sagt er heiser. »Nur Schatten und Stille.«: no name, no pronoun, nobody named since the last quote) keeps the speaker and gender of the one before (`dungeon_vorlesen.rede`). Among several named before, the panel first takes the one whose kept gender fits the pronoun. Self-test `ReadAloud.test_he_speaks_with_a_mans_voice_also_when_he_goes_on`.

**AI Dungeon Master: reading aloud, table at small widths, prompt rules:** **Reading aloud:** the short silence with which "🔊 Sound on" unlocks the player is made on the tap as a `blob:` (`dmStill`), fitting the panel's security rule (CSP `media-src 'self' blob:`). Self-test `Fight.test_table_sound_only_from_sources_the_csp_allows` (no `data:audio` in the table's scripts). **Table at small widths:** the head wraps before the title gets narrower than 320 px; music, sound and buttons move into a second row and never lie over the title. The columns follow the table's own width, not the window's: up to 1060 px two columns with the group as a row on top, up to 720 px one column. Browser test at 1280, 1000 and 390 px. **Prompt:** the rules (Funktionen → KI-Dungeon-Master → Regeln anpassen) have no JSON rule of their own (the fixed schema rules that), and rule 7 says "Invent no values, items or foes outside the facts; the panel drops them." Armour works through the armour class.

**AI Dungeon Master: scene picture in the prologue, "🔊 Sound on":** Every prologue (new round, next chapter) paints its scene picture right away, within the limits of "Paint pictures" (`may_paint`): with the model's picture text, without one from the scene's place and mood (`scene_text`, the same source as "Paint scene picture now"). When the check refuses the model's picture text, the prologue paints nothing. Log `dungeon: bild | prolog | ok` or with the reason (`aus`, `malt`, `leer`, `person` …). "🔊 Sound on" unlocks the sound with a real 0.1 s silence and wakes the audio context; only a refusal of the browser (NotAllowedError) keeps it locked. Self-test `Pictures.test_every_opening_paints_its_scene`. **Quest done only at the end:** A goal the model reports (`erfolg` "ziel") ticks off the pinned quest only by a fixed rule (`quest_may_end`): with a story plan only in its last act or after it, without a plan after 8 turns at the earliest (`AUFTRAG_ZUEGE`). An earlier goal is a step on the way: its 100 XP count, the quest stays open and the model keeps getting it as a fact; a quest ticked off too early is open again after the next turn. Log `dungeon: auftrag | erfuellt` or `… | zu frueh`. Self-test `Fight.test_the_quest_is_not_done_after_one_goal_early_in_the_story`.

**AI Dungeon Master: fights by fixed rules:** **Encounter by budget:** The panel works out the experience budget of a medium D&D encounter from the level of every living hero (`dungeon_kampf.BUDGET`, half at the children's table) and, without a running fight, offers the model at most 5 foes of the round's world from the bestiary (fact `angebot`, drawn fixed per turn from the 12 nearest to a third of the budget). The model picks only from them (field `begegnung`: id and count); the panel takes only offered ids, at most 4 of a kind and 6 in all, and drops the strongest until the experience with the D&D multiplier is at most 1.5 times the budget. The card "⚔ Encounter" shows every foe with picture or sign, count and danger (easy, medium, hard); a tap shows text, level, life, armour class, attack, damage and experience. **The panel rolls the attacks:** The model gives an attack's check the target (`ziel`, the foe's name). The panel rolls d20 + the bonus of the weapon's value (with equipment and blessings) + the proficiency bonus by level (`prof`: +2, from level 5 +3 …) against the foe's armour class; a 20 always hits and rolls the damage twice, a 1 never hits. Damage: the weapon's die + the value's bonus + the rarity's bonus, with an attack spell its die by grade, without a weapon 1d2. Then every standing foe attacks the hero who last hit it, else a standing one: d20 + attack from the bestiary against the hero's **armour class** (10 + twice the body armour + helmet, shield, gloves, boots + Dexterity bonus, at most +2 in medium armour, none in heavy; shown on the hero sheet and the card), damage by the bestiary's dice. Only the panel changes the foes' life; damage the model reports does not count. A foe at a quarter of its life or less flees on a d20 of 9 or less, at the children's table it "gives up". The bar "⚔ Fight" above the story shows every foe with its life, defeated or fled. **End and experience:** The fight ends when no foe stands or the check "offer peace" or the group's flight succeeds (both only by the panel's check). The experience comes from the bestiary (fled ones half), divided among the living heroes and rounded down; only a won fight brings a find. The model reports only a goal reached (100 XP), no defeated foes or loot "foe"/"boss". Levels follow the D&D curve (level 5 from 6,500 XP). **Rooms of the map:** When the map puts `raum_gegner` (the room's tag and bestiary ids) into the round for a guardian or boss room, the panel offers only these foes, once each and without the budget; after the win `sieg` with the room's tag is in the round and the way is free. Self-tests `Fight` (seven cases). Live overview: running fights, standing foes, today encounters, fights, hits, misses, defeated, fled. Log: `dungeon: kampf | start | 2 gegner | mittel`, `dungeon: kampf | ende | sieg | 2 besiegt | 0 geflohen`.

**AI Dungeon Master: thing memory:** In every narration the model reports at most 3 important things that come up for the first time, with their features (field `dinge`: name, merkmale with material, colour, state). The panel checks them by fixed rules (short, the round's word list, no hero's name, none twice, none against one already kept) and keeps at most 12 per round with place and turn (`dungeon_erzaehler.DINGE_MAX`). Every narration gets the kept things at the group's place or from the last 10 turns as facts with the fixed rule never to change material, colour or state. **Fixed contradiction check:** when a narration names a kept thing with a feature from a fixed word list (material: wood, iron, silver, gold, stone, bronze, copper, glass, leather, bone, crystal; colour) that matches no kept feature of that kind ("wooden box" instead of "iron box"), the Dungeon Master writes it once more with a fixed hint naming the thing and its features; otherwise the first one stays. **For the host:** under "Truth of this chapter" stands "Kept things · only you see this" with name, features and place; ✏️ changes a thing, 🗑 forgets it. Players never see the list. Kept things go on into the next chapter. A line out of the game (ooc) that speaks for the panel ("the panel confirms …") is dropped. Endpoint `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/dinge` (host, login, profile, CSRF, size and rate limit). Live overview: kept things, contradictions rewritten today. Log: `dungeon: dinge | neu | 2`, `… | geaendert`, `… | weg`, `… | wortliste`, `… | nicht da`, `dungeon: nachbessern | widerspruch`. Self-tests `ThingMemory` (six cases) and `Windows.test_kept_things_for_the_host`.

**AI Dungeon Master: life 0 has consequences, powers work by fixed rules, experience only at the end:** At 0 life a hero is **down**: it does not act, the turn does not wait for it, bag, sheet and trading are locked (reason "boden"). A friend gives it one of their own healing potions with "🧪 Give a healing potion" on its card, a healing spell for the group gets it up, or the narration reports healing when someone helps it up. After 3 told turns without healing (`BODEN_ZUEGE`) it has **fallen, for good**: everything it wore and carried and its gold lie under "Lying here" for the others, the experience of this table is gone. A fallen hero stays so in the hero list, the guest account and a campaign; it sits at no table again (reason "tot"), gets no hero code and no consequences. For a new hero the host takes the seat back and invites again. When everyone is down, the Dungeon Master narrates on once the turn time is up. **At the children's table** (youngest tone) nobody dies: after 3 turns the hero is only unconscious and wakes at the next table (next chapter), with its things but without its gold. **Rest:** whoever sits down at a new table (new round, next chapter, seat) has full life and charges, only the living. The model gets every character's state ("down", "unconscious", "fallen") with the fixed rule that it does not act, and may never write "the panel confirms". **Powers by fixed rules:** abilities carry a `wirkung` (fixed words, checked by `check_rules`) and `immer` in `dungeon_regeln.json`. Iron skin (`weniger_schaden`) takes 1 off every hit, even below 1. Shield wall (`abfangen`), when the action names it with a word of using it, takes the first hit meant for a friend in that turn, once a scene (place); a second time at the same place a line for that seat only says "used already". Battle cry (`gruppe_probe`) gives the group +1 on that turn's checks, the roll shows "Battle cry +1". Lasting abilities (Iron skin, Steadfast, Weapon master, Master thief and others) show "always works" instead of "Use". **Experience at the end:** XP gather at the table ("+X XP still at the table" in the sheet, the hero list and the log) and are credited only at the regular end of the round (the host ends it, time is up, the chapter is closed), at most one level per game evening. Whoever leaves before (the host takes the seat back, a hero code mid-round) keeps the experience it came with; things and trades stay so nothing is doubled. A hero sits at one table only. Live overview: heroes down, today down, back up, potions for friends, fallen, unconscious, powers worked. Log: `dungeon: zustand | boden | noch 3`, `… | auf`, `… | tot | 5 dinge`, `… | ohnmacht`, `dungeon: kraft | abfangen`, `dungeon: ep | ende | 2 helden`, `dungeon: beitreten | tot`. Self-tests `Permadeath` (six cases).

**AI Dungeon Master: every character in its clone voice with a Base model:** when the speech output runs a Base model (Settings → Speech output, model "…-Base") and VoiceDesign is provided as well, VoiceDesign speaks a character's first sentence (narrator, NPC or hero with a described voice); if it is 3 to 15 seconds long it becomes the clone reference. The Base model speaks every later sentence from the reference: the same sound and faster than VoiceDesign. If that fails twice VoiceDesign steps in, then the fixed voice. The references live in the state folder `dungeon-klone` (at most 200, the longest unused go first) and count for all rounds. With the preset CustomVoice model this does not apply. Live overview: clone voices; log `dungeon: vorlesen | klon | neu | 5.0 s`. Self-test `ReadAloud.test_a_character_speaks_as_its_clone_with_a_base_model`.

**AI Dungeon Master: a bar of its own on the phone:** on the phone the game table shows one part, a bar of its own at the bottom picks it: 📖 Story (narration, clock and field), 👥 Group (the other players), 📜 Hero (own hero with the hero sheet, Threads and Date; the game master console under “⋯” in the head, the field to act in every pane) and 🗺 Map (once there is one). When the Dungeon Master tells on while another part is open, "Story" gets a red dot. The panel's own bar (Assistant, Today, Documents, Me, Manage) is hidden at the table and comes back with "← Rounds". A wide screen shows three columns. Self-test `Windows.test_phone_bar_one_pane_at_a_time`.

**AI Dungeon Master: a calm table without doubles:** the guide "How to play" is a window in the table's look with one page per part (map, writing, dice, life, merchants, reading aloud and more). "‹ Back", "Next ›", the dots and on a computer the arrow keys page through; Esc, ✕ or a tap beside it closes it. It appears once per device as a small card "How it works" with "More", then through "❔ How to play" in the head (on the phone under "⋯"). Music, sounds and volume sit in the table's head. Turn, clock and "have acted" stand only once: the turn in the head, clock and state above the field. Once you have acted the clock stands in your own card, your own extra minute there as "+1 minute for you". The own hero stands in the group as a short row (name, life, state), the large card with the hero sheet on the right. At the table the floating assistant face is hidden, on every other page it is shown. The tab "Host" uses the table's font, an odd last tile fills its row. Self-test `Windows.test_table_calm_guide_pages_and_no_doubles`.

**AI Dungeon Master: the word list also for what players write, the host hides and mutes:** A round's word list (its tone's words, the rules' list "never", its story's taboos) also checks everything players write: the table talk, every action, the new name when renaming and the name of a hero who sits down at the table (also a kept hero or a hero code from a rougher table). At a table "from 8" "There is blood!" does not go through; the page says "That does not work at this table: a word is on the round's word list … Put it another way.", and the text stays in the field. The list "never" holds for every new hero. The **host** has a small ⊘ "Hide for everyone" on every line of table talk and every action in the log: no seat sees it any more, but it stays in the round's file. A fellow player's "⋯" has **Mute in table talk** and "Let talk again"; a muted seat can still act and in the table talk only sees "The host muted you in the table talk". Endpoint `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/verbergen` {art: tisch|aktion, n, pid} (host only, sign-in, CSRF, size and rate limit), `PUT …/platz/{pid}` knows `stumm` and `laut`. Logs `dungeon: aktion | wortliste`, `dungeon: tisch | abgelehnt | ValueError`, `dungeon: platz | stumm`, `dungeon: verbergen | tisch` (or `aktion`, `… | nicht da`), `dungeon: beitreten | wortliste`. The live overview counts hidden lines, refused player texts and muted seats, never contents. Self-tests `Moderation` in `app/tests/test_dungeon.py` and `Windows.test_acting_shows_the_own_action`.

**AI Dungeon Master: values for items and foes:** Under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules every item row shows kind, rarity, the value it uses, damage, protection, healing, blessing with its value, tool bonus and price; foes show level, life, armour, attack, damage and EP. The detail has a list of values (`dungeon_regelseite.werte`): for items what it does at the table (the same lines as on the hero sheet), what it uses, whom it is for, spell grade, classes, weight and price; for foes life, armour, attack, damage with its average, EP, the six values, the conditions it can cause, and size. Conditions show effect and duration. Everything comes from fixed words and the catalogue's numbers.

**AI Dungeon Master: the player menu is no longer cut off:** A fellow player's "⋯" menu in the side column is exactly as wide as the hero's card and opens under its head; a browser test checks this at 1280 and 390 pixels wide. **Painting under the rules without a day limit:** only the admin reaches the "Malen" button under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules; it does not stop at the day limit of catalogue pictures (300 a day, `dungeon_dingbild.TAG_MAX`), but it counts. The run over the whole catalogue, at night or by button, ends at that limit.

**AI Dungeon Master: the die lands on the number that counts:** On the own throw the die slows down and its last face is always the panel's number. With advantage or disadvantage the panel rolls two dice; the throw and the log show both and say which one counts ("Disadvantage: 14 and 9, counts: 9"), before the throw it says "two dice, the lower counts" (or the higher). A natural 20 or 1 is named under the bar ("natural 20: always a success", "natural 1: always a miss"). Every wait has its own stamp (`warten.wt`), so the same check next turn is a new throw.

**AI Dungeon Master: the map follows when the narrator forgets the id:** When the model narrates the way to a neighbouring place but leaves `szene.ort_id` empty, the panel decides by a fixed rule (`dungeon_karte.named_way`): when the narration names exactly one neighbouring place and a player asked for that place in this turn, by its name (also inflected, "zur alten Bibliothek") or its direction ("nach Nordosten"), the group moves there. A place only told about, a far place or two named neighbours never move. Log `dungeon: karte | name statt id`. **Only who wants to go goes:** even a valid `ort_id` of the model moves the group only when an action of the turn wants to go: a word of going (gehen, weiter, folgen, betreten, verlassen, reisen, zurück … `GEHEN`, in English go, head, travel …), a neighbouring place or room, or a direction. When a hero only searches a letter pointing to Wolfshafen, the group stays where it is (log `dungeon: karte | verworfen | ungefragt`). A turn without an action never moves. **The narration names the new place:** when the `ort_id` points to a neighbouring place the narration does not name, `dungeon_karte.ort_fehlt` returns that name (for the narrator's "karte" fault, which has the turn rewritten), and the map keeps the group where it was at worst (log `dungeon: karte | verworfen | nicht erzaehlt`).

**AI Dungeon Master: ways in stages and the way to the nearest settlement:** The map only counts; the checks and events are rolled by the panel elsewhere. `dungeon_karte.weg(r, rd, "o3")` gives for a way from here its stages by its length (`ETAPPE` = 80 units, 1 to 3 stages, at least one) and the land it leads through (the id of `ideen.landschaften` whose place kinds hold the target, else the start, else "weg"; one source with the travel events). `LAND` only draws the ink signs around the places. `dungeon_karte.zur_siedlung(rd)` searches the ways for the nearest settlement (village, town, tavern, station, bazaar, harbour as for trading) and gives stages and steps, the name only when the players know the place; inside a settlement it is 0 stages.

**AI Dungeon Master: props by room and place:** `dungeon_karte.requisiten(r, rd, rng)` fixes 2 to 3 props of `ideen.requisiten` for the fight where the group stands: first those whose `arten` hold the room id (e.g. `speisesaal`) or room kind in a vault, or the place kind outside, filled up with those without `arten` (fit anywhere). They are rolled once per room or place (`karte.req`) and stay; `requisit_benutzt(rd, id)` takes a used one away (each only once). Effect, check and buttons are the fight's (switch `dungeonumgebung`). Logs `dungeon: karte | requisiten | N`, `requisit | benutzt`, `requisit | fehlt`.

**AI Dungeon Master: alarm and torches in a vault (rule depth "Full", Features → AI Dungeon Master → Rule depth):** The panel counts an alarm from 0 to 6 per vault (`dungeon_alarm.py`). Noise raises it, each by at most 3: a fight, a door, the price "noise" of a near miss, a fumbled sneak (the fight's events via `dungeon_karte.laerm(rd, n, grund)`) and a sprung trap. At 3 a bell tolls and the next chamber or passage entered holds a patrol with guardian foes; at 6 the master of the vault has one more follower. When new dwellers come it is calm again. On entering the panel lights a light of the group: first one that never burns out (light spell, Everburning torch with `licht` −1), else the first torch or glow stick in a pack (`licht` 4, used up). Every room entered burns one, then the next follows. Without light, whoever cannot see in the dark (not dwarf, elf, orc, gnome, half-elf or tiefling) rolls every check at a disadvantage; the receipt says "dunkel". Above the vault plan it says "🔔 Alarm 2/6 · 🔥 Torch: 3 rooms" or "🌑 Dark"; the view carries `karte.alarm` and `karte.fackel` (rooms left, −1 always burns, 0 dark, `null` outside), the model gets alarm and light only as data. Logs `dungeon: alarm | <grund> | <stufe>`, `alarm | streife`, `alarm | herr bereit`, `alarm | licht | <rooms>`, `alarm | verworfen`; the live overview counts noise, patrols, torches and darkness.

**AI Dungeon Master: the map does not blink:** The table writes itself anew on every refresh, but the map stays the same element while its drawing does not change (`dmkKeep` in `dungeonkarte.js`, called by `dmTable`), and a picture already loaded is in the drawing at once. It is drawn again only when something really changes, such as a way, a new place or the width.

**AI Dungeon Master: coming along only after the check and people on the move, the map's part:** In a round at the rule depth "Full" (part `dungeonmitkommen`) a word like "come with us" does not take a person along at once; the map only remembers which seat asked (`leute[name].fragt`). `dungeon_karte.fragen(rd)` tells before the narration whom the open actions ask so (read only, the same words), so the panel can roll the check first. The panel's check decides, `dungeon_karte.mitnehmen(rd, name, ja)` takes the person along or not and clears the question. Without the switch the word takes the person along directly. `dungeon_karte.wandern(r, rd, rng, anlass)` (occasion end of a chapter or a long rest, switch `dungeonwandern`) lets every known person not travelling along whose goal the panel knows (`dungeon_nsc.ziel_ort`) go one way of the map on towards it at 50 %; it returns a list of who went from where to where for rumours, the model only tells the meeting again. Logs `dungeon: karte | mitkommen | gefragt|ja|nein|ungefragt`, `karte | wandern | <anlass> | <anzahl>`.

**No daily limit also without the second step:** The admin ticks "frei" under People and devices, at the profile under Rights "No daily limit", for any profile and without the second login step (`PUT /api/admin/profiles/{uid}/tageslimit` with `{"frei": true}`, admin only, no manager, rate limit, CSRF). A profile with the admin role has the tick set for good. Stored in `state/ohne-tageslimit.json` (in the backup), changes in the admin log (`profile_daily_limit`) and in the log `coadmin: tageslimit | frei|begrenzt|kein profil`. The admin rights themselves need the second step. Me → Pictures and Me → Music show "no daily limit".

**No daily limits for admins, rarity under Regeln → Gegenstände:** A profile with the admin role (`coadmin.unbegrenzt`, the same rule as for the quota) has no daily limits for painting and changing pictures, songs, own music files and the pictures of an own Dungeon round; Me → Pictures and Me → Music show "no daily limit". Space, the queue of heavy jobs and everything that leaves the house (mails, calls, reMarkable) keep their limits. Under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules the item list stays put on a tap, only the mark and the detail change. Every item stands in its rarity's frame; most basic items roll it only when found, carry a dashed frame with a colour gradient, and their detail shows the ladder common to legendary with roll, bonus, price and minimum level. **Renaming a fellow player:** in a fellow player's "⋯" the host changes the hero's name (`PUT /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/platz/{pid}` with `{"was": "name"}`), only the name, checked as when creating (2 to 24 characters, at least one letter); values, level and bag stay. Log `dungeon: platz | name`.

**AI Dungeon Master: More time between turns:** After every narration a reading time runs first (15 s plus 1 s per 60 characters, at most 90 s, `dungeon.read_s`), only then the turn time; until then the clock shows "reading time, the clock starts in … s". New rounds have 300 s turn time (selectable 60, 120, 300). Once the last player acted, the turn closes only 10 s later (`GNADE_S`), the own card shows the seconds and "Change" still works. Below the input field and in the own card there is "+1 minute": once per turn and seat (`POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/mehrzeit`, guests `POST /api/dungeon/mehrzeit`; sign-in or seat code, CSRF, size and rate limit like the other game endpoints), everyone sees "more time for …". From 30 s left the clock turns red and a phone vibrates once. Logs: `dungeon: mehrzeit | ok`, `dungeon: mehrzeit | schon`, `dungeon: mehrzeit | nicht jetzt`.

**AI Dungeon Master: tab "Rules" with bestiary and balance:** On the **Dungeon Master** page the admin has a third tab *Rules* next to *Rounds* and *Heroes*; *Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules* opens it (without a profile as a card). On the left are the parts: stories, items, foes (bestiary), classes, peoples, backgrounds, worlds, tones, conditions, then voices, pictures, music, guests with e-mail, how it lands and *Advanced (JSON)*. Each part is a list with picture or sign, one line of values, origin (base, SRD, own, changed, hidden) and the balance mark, with search and filters (items by kind, foes by level, both by balance); beside it (below on a phone) the detail with *Edit* (the whole entry, checked like every rule), *Own copy*, *Hide* and *Back to shipped*. **Pictures:** foes get a painted picture in the style of their world like items (a fixed sentence of the panel, at night or by button, the same day limit). *🖌 Paint* paints a missing picture right away, *Paint again* paints a second one and shows both side by side: *Keep the new one* or *Keep the old one*; one nobody decides goes after a day. **Balance** (`dungeon_balance.py`): foes are compared with a fixed table per level (life, armour, attack, damage per round), items with a budget per rarity (damage, protection, healing, blessing, spell grade, price); the mark says *balanced*, *strong*, *too strong* or *weak*, the reason is in the detail. *Too strong* is only a hint, saving stays possible. A self-test checks the shipped catalogue; the list of entries still too strong may only shrink. Live counts own and hidden entries and pictures waiting.

**AI Dungeon Master: room for the SRD data:** Next to `dungeon_regeln.json` the panel reads the shipped file `dungeon_srd.json` (data from the D&D SRD 5.1/5.2, CC-BY-4.0) as a layer between the base rules and the admin's own: the same lists, the same id replaces the entry, `{"id": …, "dazu": {…}}` adds to the lists of an existing entry (such as a class's weapons, spells per grade, an item's classes; also in the admin's own rules). Without the file only base and own rules count; if it does not pass `check_rules`, the panel plays without it (log `dungeon: regeln verworfen | srd`). Checked are the lists **gegner** (sign, worlds, level 0 to 30, life, armour, attack, damage, experience, six values, size, conditions), **zustaende** (effect only from fixed words like `nachteil_angriff` or `kein_zug`, duration in rounds) and **hintergruende** (value bonuses 0 to 2, starting gear), the fixed difficulty table **sg** (very easy 5 to nearly impossible 30), **ladungen** per level and spell grade 1 to 5, on classes `start_faehigkeiten` and `zauber` per grade, on spells `grad` 0 to 5. Items may have a fixed `seltenheit` (loot and merchants offer them only when the roll reaches it), jewellery a fixed value (else drawn); the kind **treasure** (gems, art) is only for selling and stacks. Items with `welten` exist only in those worlds. Each merged list holds up to 500 entries; the admin's own file (*Features → AI Dungeon Master* "Adjust rules") at most 200 per list.

**AI Dungeon Master: spells and powers only when the hero has them:** As in D&D every class brings its **starting powers** (`start_faehigkeiten`, shown under the class as "can: …" when a hero is made), spell classes have their **spells per grade** (`zauber`); cantrips (grade 0) work any time, higher grades need a **charge** (`ladungen` per level). The charges fill up with every new round and chapter, a level brings new ones. The hero sheet shows **"What I can do"**: abilities, spells with "1 of 2 charges left" and spells or tools from the bag; a tap writes "I cast …" into the own action. **The panel checks** every action by fixed rules (`check_powers`): if, with a word like "cast", "use" or "wirke", it names a spell or ability of the catalogue the hero neither has nor carries, only that seat sees "(OOC: Bela cannot do that yet: Fireball.)", and the model gets `nicht_bekannt` and tells that it fails; without a charge it says "no charge left in this chapter". A known spell with a charge is cast when the turn is told (charge gone, healing and blessing by the catalogue, line "✦ … casts …"). The model gets the list `kann` per hero (names and charges only) and the fixed rule to cast only from it. Status → Live counts spells from charges and refused powers; Logs only `dungeon: kann | nicht | 1 namen` and `dungeon: kraft | grad 1`. *Features → AI Dungeon Master* "Adjust rules" changes starting powers, spells per class and charges (checked by `check_rules`).

**AI Dungeon Master: guests with e-mail:** Own admin switch *Features → AI Dungeon Master → Guests with e-mail* (`chat.dungeon_gast`, off by default; `dungeon_gast.py`). Under "Adjust rules", in the section "Guests with e-mail", the admin picks the mailbox with an outgoing server and sees only numbers (guest accounts, with turn mail, heroes, mails today). A guest (only with a seat link, so only by invitation) opens "Guest account with e-mail" in the invitation or at the table, types their address and gets a 6-digit code (valid 10 minutes, at most 5 wrong tries, at most 3 codes an hour per address, 40 game mails a day). With the code they have a guest account on this device (a key in the browser, the Spark knows only its hash, up to 5 devices); the hero at the seat goes in, "Keep this hero" adds one later, up to 8 heroes, written along after every turn and at the end of the round. A new invitation offers "Hero from your guest account". The Spark stores only an HMAC of the address (`dungeon-gaeste.json`, 0600). **"Your turn by mail"** is switched on by the guest with their address (it must match the account): then it lies sealed like a password on the Spark, switching it off deletes it. The mail comes when a turn has been open 10 minutes, the guest has written nothing and is not at the table, once per turn, at most 3 a day. All mails are fixed texts of the panel, never model or player text; the emergency stop holds them. "Delete guest account" removes heroes, devices and address. Live counts guest accounts, turn mails, codes and mails; Logs only `dungeon: gastmail | code gesendet`, `angemeldet`, `dran | gesendet` and the like, never an address. API (guest): `POST /api/dungeon/gast` (`{code, was: code|anmelden|konto|merken|zugmail|held_weg|loeschen, …}`, rate limit `dungeonkonto`), joining with `{code, konto, konto_held}`; admin `PUT /api/admin/dungeon/gastmail`.

**AI Dungeon Master: props in the scene picture:** While a fight goes on and "use the surroundings" is on, a scene picture (`dungeon.queue_scene`) takes the props still there (`dungeon_umgebung.here`, rows of `ideen.requisiten`) into the picture: at most `REQUISITEN_MAX` (3), by their English name from the catalog, never with the model's text (`dungeon_dingbild.props`). A name the picture rules refuse is left out; a used prop is missing from the next picture. No switch of its own, the one of "use the surroundings" counts. Self-test `test_the_fights_props_are_in_the_scene_picture`.

**AI Dungeon Master: faces for companions:** A hired companion (`rd["gefaehrten"]`, key `gf:…`) gets its portrait like a person with a card (`dungeon_gesicht`), but at once on hiring: from its fixed English line of the table (`GEFAEHRTE_EN` + `satz`, at the children's table the little animal as it is), never with its name, with the world's style, light and mood from fixed lists, checked by `dungeon.picture_text`, within the daily picture limit. The group shows the face instead of 🤝 (`gefaehrten[].bild`, `dmGefaehrten`); when the last narration names the companion, a scene picture takes the face as a reference ("the companion from reference image N with the same face"). It only holds while "Faces for NPCs", "Hire companions" and "Paint pictures" are on for the host; "NPC memory and chronicle" is not needed. The face goes on with the companion into the next chapter (`dungeon_gefaehrte.keep`), the sweep keeps it while a round or campaign has the companion, only a seat of that round may load it. Self-test `test_a_hired_companion_gets_its_face_at_once`. The settlement service "hire a companion" has its own picture (`DIENST_EN["gefaehrte"]`, a hiring corner by an inn's door, no person in front) and three lines of life in `dungeon_siedlung.json`.

**AI Dungeon Master: portraits in the NPC catalog:** Under *Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → "People (NPC)"* the list and the detail show each person's face (`dungeon_dingbild.person_row` in `dungeon_personen.view`): first the one the admin kept by hand, then the face from a round, else a catalog picture. A person without a face from a round is a catalog picture like items and foes (`dungeon_dingbild.people`): painted at night and with "Paint all pictures", "🖌 Paint", "Paint again" and "Keep the new one" go through the catalog picture door (`/api/admin/dungeon/katalog/personen/bild`). The picture's prompt is made of fixed parts only (`person_text`): its world's style, age and gender of its stored voice, the English column of its card (`dungeon_gesicht.katalog_text`), light and mood from fixed lists; never its name. When the person comes back in a round (`dungeon_personen.adopt`), it brings this face, not at the children's table. The page "People" of the monster album shows it too. The sweep keeps a face while a round or the catalog names it. Holds only while "Pictures for gear and spells", "Faces for NPCs" and "People catalog" are on. Self-test `test_a_catalog_person_gets_its_face`.

**AI Dungeon Master: innkeeper or landlady, names only as a whole word:** The settlement's lines of life (`dungeon_siedlung.json`) always name the innkeeper of one world the same, never once "Wirtin" and once "Wirt"; the line for "hire a companion" is neutral. A scene picture takes the face of a person or a companion only when the narration names it as a whole word (`dungeon_nsc.named` in `dungeon_gesicht.vorlagen`): "Perle" is not in "Perlen". Self-tests `test_one_innkeeper_per_world` and `test_a_hired_companion_gets_its_face_at_once`.

**AI Dungeon Master: what a foe is, companions with gender and age in the picture:** A foe's picture takes what it is from its field "wesen" in the Bestiarium (`dungeon_bestiarium.wesen`): humans, humanoids, giants, undead, constructs, fey, fiends, celestials and aberrations each have a fixed phrase (`WESEN_EN`), animals, dragons, elementals, plants, oozes and monsters already say it with their name. An old picture stays until the new one is painted (at night or with "Paint all pictures"). A companion's face puts gender and age from its data in front ("a middle aged woman, a companion of the heroes, …"), an animal or a machine gets "an animal/a machine companion"; nothing is guessed from the line. Self-tests `test_a_foe_is_painted_from_its_description_size_and_kind` and `test_a_hired_companion_gets_its_face_at_once`.

**AI Dungeon Master: three small night foes for science fiction:** The bestiary (`app/panel/dungeon_srd.json`, each with `"nacht": true`, the "new" mark and a saying) holds the glow moth (10 XP, an animal that nibbles at the light of lamps), the cable crawler (25 XP, a maintenance robot from the cable ducts, construct, immune to poison and psychic, weak to lightning) and the night flitter (50 XP, an animal that flits through the cargo holds in the dark). So every world has at least five night foes for one hero on levels 1 to 3 (one hero alone has a budget of about 60 XP on level 1).

**AI Dungeon Master: portrait by the gender on the card:** A person's portrait takes the gender written on its card (`rd["nsc"][k]["geschlecht"]`, set once when the card is made), through the same place as its voice (`dungeon_stimmen._of`); so does the age once the card has a field "alter", else from the fixed voice traits. A person with "divers" is painted as an androgynous person ("an old androgynous person", the word from `dungeon.GESCHLECHT`). None of it is guessed by the model; no switch, no endpoint of its own.

**AI Dungeon Master: hair, eyes, beard and build in the portrait:** A person's portrait adds the fixed looks of its card (`dungeon_nsc.MERKMALE`: hair, eyes, beard, build, rolled once by the panel), in the tables' English column, such as "grey hair, blue eyes, slender build". So the picture fits the card; the model guesses none of it. New portraits and catalog pictures take it at once, a portrait already painted stays until the host has it painted again.

**AI Dungeon Master: pictures for runes and glowing sockets:** With "Runes and sockets" on, each of the 13 runes gets a catalog picture per style, as its world's word (rune, chip, pearl, seal; `dungeon_runen.word`) and in its fixed colour by rank (`dungeon_dingbild.RUNE_FARBE`, `rune_text`); it is painted at night or with "Paint all pictures", none while the switch is off. The rune bag in the hero sheet and the trader's runes show the picture instead of "ᚱ" once it is there. Sockets are no picture of their own: `dungeonsockel.js` (`dmSockel`) lays them as dots over the picture or sign, a free socket dark, a set rune glowing in its colour (`ru_farbe` from `dungeon_dingbild.add`). A finished runeword gets a picture of its own (see below). The model decides none of it; no switch, no endpoint of its own.

**AI Dungeon Master: rune pictures on the rules page:** In the part "Runes" of the rules tab (Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules) every rune has its picture with "Paint", "Paint again" and "Keep new"/"Keep old" like items and foes. "Pictures in: …" above the list chooses the world; the rune shows as that world's word and in its style (rune, chip, pearl, seal; `dungeon_regelseite.WELT_WAHL`, `dungeon_dingbild._current` with `rune_text`). A rune picture stays while "Runes and sockets" is off: the sweep keeps it, only nothing is painted at night then.

**AI Dungeon Master: a picture of its own for an awakened runeword, pictures of the smith's rune services:** When a runeword wakes in a thing (`it["rw"]`), the panel calls `dungeon_dingbild.runenwort` once: like a named find the thing gets a picture of its own (`fund-<i>`) with the runes in their colours and the runeword's name, painted within a minute through the queue and counted to the day limit of the game's pictures. The picture of an earlier runeword of the same thing goes first; once the runeword is freed the thing shows the catalog picture. The smith's services (boring a socket, freeing a rune, fusing runes) have a picture per world without people and lines of life in the settlement (`DIENST_EN`, `dungeon_siedlung.json`). No switch, no endpoint of its own; the model decides none of it.

**AI Dungeon Master: one picture a house of the settlement:** Every house of the settlement view (`dungeon_gold.HAEUSER`: inn, smithy, healer, teacher) has a picture of its own per world, seen from the street, no people in the foreground (`dungeon_dingbild.HAUS_EN`, `house_text`). It is painted at night or with "Paint all pictures"; until then the view shows the first picture of its services or the place's. Each house has a line of life from its services; the pictures of the single services (the room inside) stay. No switch, no endpoint of its own; the model decides none of it.

**AI Dungeon Master: a house's picture whole instead of cut off:** In the settlement window the chosen house's picture stands whole as a square on the left beside name and line (up to 200 pixels on a computer, 96 on a phone); the offers below use the full width. Display only (`app.css`).

**AI Dungeon Master: "Paint all pictures · N" counts only what a run paints; a picture for the healer:** An entry whose picture the admin fixed under "Adjust rules" with "Keep new" (`fest`) is never painted again by a run, even when its prompt changes. One rule decides counting and painting (`dungeon_dingbild._stand`: there, fixed, refused, open): fixed counts as done, "Paint all pictures · N" and "left N" name only the open pictures (`status()["offen"]`, the same as `missing()`), refused ones stand beside as their own number; without open pictures both buttons are off. The settlement service "healer" (`dungeon_gold.HEILER`) has its picture per style (`DIENST_EN["heiler"]`, a calm room with herbs, bandages and small bottles, no blood) and three lines of life in `dungeon_siedlung.json`; until the first run the window shows 🌿. Self-tests `test_the_count_names_only_what_a_run_paints`, `test_every_service_painted_and_lined`.

**AI Dungeon Master: pictures for gear and spells:** Admin switch *Features → AI Dungeon Master → Pictures and hero song* (`chat.dungeon_bilder`, off by default); Features → Painting pictures must be on as well and the picture model installed. **Catalog:** every entry (weapons, armour, jewellery, potions, spells, scrolls, the admin's own too) gets one picture per world in the world's fixed style (`welten[].bildstil`), and in the picture book style while a round in the tone from 8 is open. The panel builds the text by a fixed rule from the style, the English name and a fixed phrase per kind (for spells also the spell's English sentence), followed by "no text, no letters, no watermark"; it is checked like a picture wish (word list, known people), a refused entry never goes to the picture service. It is painted at 512×512 one after the other through the queue "Heavy jobs" (the voice comes first, maintenance pause "bild") and kept at half size with the picture service's AI marks (`dungeon-bild/ding-<hash>.png`, the hash of the text; a changed style or name paints anew). When: at night from 01:00 to 05:00 once a night, and at once with "Create pictures now" under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules (section "Pictures for gear and spells" with the count, the last run and "Stop"); at most 300 pictures a day. **"Paint all pictures"** paints every missing picture without the day limit (items, spells, tools, foes, kinds of places and rooms, peoples, classes, abilities); the button shows how many are missing, the run "painting all, no day limit · painted · left", "Cancel" stops after the picture on its way; space, the queue, Notaus and the picture check hold, only the admin reaches it (like `coadmin.unbegrenzt`). Log: `dungeon: dingbild | lauf start | alle`. **Foe pictures** get besides the English name the English description (`info`, only when the word list of Painting pictures lets it through), the size (`groesse`: tiny to huge) and, from fixed lists of the panel, what they are (`MENSCH`: "an ordinary human person", `HUMANOID`: "a humanoid with two arms and two legs"), never from a choice of the model, plus "in a fitting place of its world". Every picture text goes through the word list of Painting pictures and the game's list "nie", in the picture book style through the youngest tone's words too. **Pictures stay:** the panel remembers the last picture painted per entry and style (`zuletzt`) and shows it until a new text's picture is there; a picture kept under Adjust rules is fixed (`fest`), never painted over and never swept. When the rules fall back to the state without the SRD data, it does not sweep (log `dungeon: dingbild | aufraeumen ausgelassen | regeln unvollstaendig`). The run ends on Notaus, "Stop", the switch off, in the morning (only the night's run), at the day limit, with a full queue and when the picture model or the image check is missing; a picture the image check holds back twice is not tried again. **Finds:** when the model names a find from "rare" on, the panel paints its own picture within a minute (the round's style, the English name from the catalog, the model's name and sentence, checked like a scene description with tone, "nie" and taboos); unknown magic only once it is known. It counts to the day limit of the game's pictures (`grenzen.bilder_tag`) and needs the host's "Painting pictures". **At the table:** every item in the bag, the equipment, loot, on the ground and at the merchant shows its picture in its rarity's frame (green, blue, purple, orange), until then the kind's sign (⚔️ 🛡️ 💍 🧪 ✨ 📜). Profiles load pictures with `GET /api/profile/dungeon/ding/{name}`, guests with their seat code (`POST /api/dungeon/ding`); rate limit `dungeonding`. The state lives in `dungeon-dingbilder.json`, hourly the panel removes pictures no entry and no hero needs any more. Status → Live counts item pictures, finds, painted, discarded and failed today on the Dungeon Master line; logs: `dungeon: dingbild | lauf start | knopf`, `dungeon: dingbild | ok | katalog | 3100 ms`, `dungeon: dingbild | ok | fund | 3300 ms`, `dungeon: dingbild | lauf ende | tag | 300 gemalt`, `dungeon: dingbild | abgelehnt | fund | tag`. **Peoples, classes, backgrounds and worlds on the rules tab:** under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules they show their picture with "Paint", "Paint again" and "Keep the new one"/"Keep the old one"; "Pictures in: …" chooses the world in whose style peoples, classes and backgrounds are shown and painted (the same picture as on that world's hero form); a kept picture is fixed. **Backgrounds** get one picture per world from the English name and description (only when the word list lets it through), at night and with "Paint all pictures"; they show only on the rules tab. **Every world** has a title picture in its own style ("landscape art, a wide view of the world …, no people", not in the picture book style) that the world cards under "New round" show instead of the sign; without a picture the sign stays. **Stage pictures per kind of place:** every kind of place (`ortsarten`) and of room (`raeume`) gets three wide pictures per world style in the format of the scene pictures (16:9, painted at 1024×576, kept at half size): by day, at night and in a fight (fixed text "scene art, a wide view of …" from the English name, for rooms with the sentence, without people, in a fight only distant silhouettes without faces). The panel picks by fixed rules: fight while one runs (`kampf`), night for `abend` or `nacht` (`tageszeit`, unknown values count as day), else day. It is painted at night and with "Paint all pictures", in the picture book style while a round in the youngest tone is open. `view()` names it as `buehne.ersatz`: outside the picture of the place's kind, in a vault the one of the room's kind (a deleted entry takes the first room of the same kind); the stage shows it only while the scene has no picture of its own. Without a map, a picture or with the pictures off it is empty; the stage lays weather and daytime over it. **Pictures with flair:** every picture takes from fixed lists a light (morning light, golden hour, moonlight, candle glow, storm light, light through a window, the glow of magic), an angle (close-up, low angle, from above, three-quarter view, through an archway) and a mood (calm, mysterious, menacing, festive, forgotten and dusty); which one is fixed by the entry's id with the style (sha256), never by the model. In the picture book style never menacing or storm light, in a dark world style (gothic, horror, grim, dark) never festive; places, worlds, heroes and the stage never close-up. Items, spells and tools lie in a place of their kind (a weapon on an anvil, a potion on an alchemist's table, a spell above an open spell book), sharp before a soft background; a fixed rarity shows (common plain and worn up to legendary with aura, runes and sparks), rolled finds keep their frame. Foes get a pose (lurking, attacking, roaring, stepping out of the dark, waiting for prey, circling; in the picture book only proud, curious or patient) and surroundings by size (large and huge in the open, in a hall, on a slope, the rest in a passage, a ruin, a thicket). Peoples, classes and backgrounds stand in their world with a pose, abilities as a moment in action; worlds and the map vignettes of the kinds of places and rooms get light and mood. When a picture text changes, the old picture stays visible until the new one is painted (at night 300 a day, "Paint all pictures" without a limit). **Settlement with life:** in a settlement's window the view names as `siedlung.kopf` the place's stage picture (by day or at night) and per service (`gasthaus`, `schmied`, `lehre`, `geruecht`, `verbessern`) a painted picture in the world's style (`bild`, fixed text from `DIENST_EN` with light, angle and mood, no people in the foreground, at night and with "Paint all pictures") and a line of life (`zeile`) from `dungeon_siedlung.json`: three lines per world and service in German and English, `alle` for any other world, another one per turn and place by a fixed rule, never from the model. Without a picture `bild` stays empty and the window shows the sign.

**AI Dungeon Master: map and random vaults (`dungeon_karte.py`):** the map belongs to the panel, not to the model. At the start of a round or a chapter the panel rolls 8 to 15 places in one of five landscapes (scattered, river valley, around a lake, two realms with one crossing, coast), at least 150 points apart, joined by a spanning tree and a few short extra ways that cross no other (at most 4 a place); the start in the west, the goal farthest away, a place on the way for every act of the story. Names come from the story's places (the first is the start), else from the world's kinds of places (`ortsarten`: 33 kinds with name lists) or from a stem (`silben`) and an ending of the kind, such as "Crow" and "Wood". The next chapter starts at the last place. **The model** gets only the current place, the ways from there with their direction and the direction of the next act's place (`karte` in the facts) and names one of those ways in `szene.ort_id`; anything else, a made-up name or a request in an action as well, leaves the group where it is (`dungeon: karte | verworfen`). The scene's place is always the map's name. **Fog:** the page shows only visited (solid) and known places (neighbours of visited ones, dashed, name in italics); a way into the unknown ends as a stub with "?", neither the page nor the model learns the name behind it. The view grows with what was discovered, "⤢" shows the map large. Text, circles and ways have on every width the screen size of the text beside them; each name finds a free spot around its place, long names take two lines, the place stands as "📍 You are at …" above the map. **Pictures instead of signs:** with Features → AI Dungeon Master → Pictures and hero song and Features → Painting pictures, the night run (or "Create pictures now") paints one picture per kind of place and of room in the style of every world it belongs to, from fixed phrases with the English name, within the same day limit. Every visited place of a running round whose host has "Painting pictures" gets its own picture from kind, name and style, checked like a scene picture (tone, "nie", taboos, word list, people) and within the game's day limit; a refused name gets none, until then the sign stays. An own place picture is shown only to seats of the same round (profile or guest link); the check reads only that round or the profile's rounds (`dungeon_ablage.runde`, `rids_von`), never all; any name outside the fixed pattern is refused; the sweep keeps what a map still names. Live counts the place pictures. Under "From here" a tap on a way writes "We go on to …" into the own field; it stays the player's text. The host may roll the map again before the first step. **Look:** parchment with grain, a dark edge, a double frame, a compass and the title plate; trees, mountains, hills, grass and marsh fit the nearest kind of place and stand the same on every repaint (fixed random), the river has a current, lake and coast waves, ways are bent ink lines, a small flag marks the group, the unknown lies under clouds of fog. Every world has its style (fantasy parchment, horror pale paper, pirates sea chart, sci-fi star chart). Motion: the flag walks, new places fade in, torches flicker; with "Reduce motion" on the device everything stands still. When the group enters a vault, the map switches to the tab "Vault". **Drawn plan:** the vault is a plan in the style of the One-Page Dungeons: black ink walls on paper, the rock shaded grey and crosshatched, a dotted grid, doors as bars (open where the group went), unknown neighbouring rooms dashed with "?". Every room entered carries its number and furniture after the room table (stairs at the entrance, a pool at the spring, a pit at the trap, a rune circle at the riddle, a chest and coins in the treasure, columns and a plinth at the guardian, a dais and throne at the master, shelves, cells, barrels, pods and so on; a room the admin adds gets the drawing of its kind). The numbers of unconquered rooms are red, a dashed red frame and a pawn show the group; below stands the key of the rooms entered. The paper takes the world's colour (sci-fi as a blueprint), dimmed at the dark table. The crosshatch follows benjcooley/dungeongen (MIT licence, © 2024 Benjamin Cooley): bundles of three parallel strokes, spread evenly, turned away from their neighbours and cut at their strokes; a fixed chance from the vault's name keeps it the same. 🔥 next to the tabs switches to the torchlight look with stone slabs, thick walls, doors, a colour per kind of room, painted room pictures and light around the group, 📜 back; the device remembers the choice. **The world goes on:** when the group has left a vault and gone at least 6 steps (`NEU_NACH`), the vault has new dwellers on the next entry: guardians, the master, traps and emptied chests come back, their rooms are unknown again, the panel picks the foes anew by the group's level; riddles, springs and altars stay. The table reads "Something new has moved into the vault …", log `dungeon: gewoelbe | neu belebt | N raeume`. When a faction's clock is full, it holds a place (`dungeon_fraktionen.occupied`): a dark banner with a golden crest (name on pointing), a ⚑ at the way under "From here", "⚑ Held by …" above the map while the group stands there. When "Arms for group and factions" is on, the banner carries that faction's arms (as an SVG image that runs nothing) and the line shows them instead of the ⚑. The model hears it as `besetzt_von`; arriving, the table reads once "… is held by …" (log `dungeon: karte | besetzt`). **Key doors:** while "Quest things" is allowed for the round, every second vault of 5 rooms and more (`SCHLOSS`) has a locked door on the way to the master. Its key is a quest thing with a fixed name per world (fantasy Eisenschlüssel/iron key, Bronzeschlüssel, Runensiegel; horror Knochenschlüssel …; pirates Messingschlüssel …; sci-fi Zugangskarte …, each the first the round does not have yet) and lies in a room reachable without that door, off the way when possible. When an action names the key and the group carries it, the panel opens the door, the key is used up and the group may go through in the same turn. The model never opens a door. On the plan the door carries a red lock, in torchlight 🔒, under "From here" "🔒 locked door"; above the map it says whether the key still lies in the vault or which name the action should name. With "Quest things" off no door is locked, and a locked one opens on the next turn. Log: `dungeon: gewoelbe | schloss`, `… | schloss | offen`, `… | schloss | ohne schluessel`, `dungeon: karte | verworfen | verschlossen`. **On the way:** a way gone for the first time brings an encounter from `begegnungen` 40 percent of the time (quiet, weather, a hint, a find from the catalog, a foe from the fight's offer, a merchant at the new place); the model tells it in the next turn. **Vaults:** a place of a vault kind (cave, ruin, crypt, tower, wreck, lab, manor, fortress …) or the host's button "🎲 Roll a vault" (it only lays an entrance below the place: ⚔ on the map and "into the vault" under "From here"; the group goes in by its action) leads into a grid of 5 to 12 rooms (6 to 9 columns, 4 to 6 rows) in one of five forms (branching, long corridor with side rooms, maze with loops, cross around a hall, winding cave), the entrance at the west edge, the master in the farthest room, a treasure next to it, about half of the other rooms special; rooms per world from `raeume` (42). **Trap:** on entering, the panel rolls d20 plus value for every hero against the trap's difficulty (`fallen`, 17, plus half the group's level) as a saving throw: failed the whole damage (at most 6), made half; armour does not help. **Riddle:** the door stays shut until an action holds one of the fixed answer words (`raetsel`, 40, case and umlauts do not count); after 3 turns the page shows the hint, solved it gives half the goal's EP to everyone. The model gets the question, never the answer. **Guardian and master** keep the ways shut until the fight is won: on entering, the panel picks the foes from the world's Bestiarium (without foes of its own the fantasy ones) by the group's level L within the fight's budget (guardian: kinds of the challenge nearest L/2; master: one nearest L+1 with helpers near L/4; the panel fills the count by experience) and offers only them; only the victory opens the room. **Treasure** gives the loot "truhe" once, a **spring** heals everyone by 2 to 6, an **altar** gives everyone +1 on one value for 3 turns. The way out is only at the entrance. Everything stays in the round (`dungeon-runden.json`); the admin changes the tables like the rest of the catalog (*Features → AI Dungeon Master*, button "Adjust rules"), the panel checks them. Live counts maps and rounds inside a vault; the journal writes fixed words and numbers only (`dungeon: karte`, `gewoelbe`, `falle`, `raetsel`, `quelle`, `altar`, `unterwegs`). **Places and people stay where they are:** back to the previous place only when an action names it, its direction or says "back"; otherwise the panel drops the way (`dungeon: karte | verworfen | rueckweg`). Whoever speaks in the narration stays where the group met them (up to 40 people); only someone an action takes along with fixed words ("come with", "follow us", "join us" …) travels with the group, "stay here", "wait here" or a farewell leaves them behind. The model gets here, elsewhere (with the place) and along. When an action and the narration name a neighbouring place but the map does not move, it shows "Place not changed" (`dungeon: karte | ort bleibt`). For the voice (CarPlay) `dungeon_karte.wege(rd, lang)` gives the same ways as "From here" with direction and goal, never a place in the fog. Endpoint `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/karte` (`{was: neu|gewoelbe}`, host only, login, profile binding, CSRF, size and rate limit).

**AI Dungeon Master: music, sounds, dice:** **Music** only when the admin switches on **Prepare music** under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → "Music at the table" (off by default) and the music model is installed; otherwise the table has sounds and dice only. The own music model (Making music, `musik.make`) builds one shared store for the whole Spark, one field per world and mood: exploring, tavern, tension, fight, finale, victory and vault, each holding as many pieces as the admin sets (default 12, 4 to 30). A piece runs 3 to 6 minutes, a victory 30 s, all instrumental, each with its own seed and a way of playing from fixed lists of the world. **Fitting the theme:** every world has its own scene and instruments per mood, for example a fantasy tavern with lute, fiddle, frame drum and upright bass, a fantasy fight with war drums, deep bass and horns, a pirate fight with barrel drums, horror tension with a heartbeat bass; drums and bass with measure, never hectic. Every field has a fixed tempo (exploring 75, tavern 96, tension 72, fight 100, finale 92, victory 96, vault 66 beats per minute) and every world a fixed key (fantasy D minor, horror C minor, sci-fi A minor, pirates E minor; tavern and victory in the relative major), so pieces following each other fit together harmonically. Every piece starts softly and fades out long. The storage defaults to 2400 MB; if it is too small for the number of pieces, the card "Music at the table" says so. Pieces of an older style keep playing until their field has new ones (the table is never silent). The description for the music model is made only of fixed words of `dungeon_musik.py`; actions, narration, scene, story or the name of an own world never go in. **The panel picks the mood:** fight (a fight at the table or fight words in the scene), then vault (the group stands in a random vault), then a merchant or a settlement (tavern), then the last act (finale), then tension words in the scene, else the act's mood (word list over title, content and turning point; act 1 exploring, then tension). **Ahead:** every running round asks for 2 pieces each for the mood now, the act's, the next act's, fight and victory. Every new act orders 2 new pieces for the act after it, which replace the most played ones. At night (1 to 6 o'clock, not during a Notaus) and with "Prepare music now" the Spark fills every field up to the number. Only one piece is made at a time, on the place of the heavy jobs (`schwer.run("musik")`), with speech first and only while no other heavy job waits. After 3 failures in a row the store rests for an hour. **Full:** per field the most played pieces go first (never the new one), the store stays within the set storage. **At the table** a bar sits above the story: mood, piece x of n, music on/off, sounds on/off, volume and ⏭; each device remembers its own choice. The music loops quietly (default 20 %) and fades over 12 s into the next piece of the same mood, never one of the last 3; a change of mood fades over at once. A new act or a new level plays a victory piece once, the music gets quieter while the narration is read aloud. **Sounds** are made in the browser (WebAudio, no files): dice, door (door or gate in the narration), hit, healing, coins (loot, trade), spell, level and, quietly in the background, rain, wind, fire or water (word list over place and mood, else the world's own). **Dice:** on every throw the d20 rolls for 0.8 s and always stops on the panel's number (display only), with a bright sound on 20 and a dull one on 1; with "reduce motion" the spin is left out. Guests fetch pieces with their seat code (`POST /api/dungeon/musik`), profiles with `GET /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/musik/{sid}`, only a seat of a running round, own rate limit `dungeonmusik`. Storage: `dungeon-musik.json` (setting, in the backup) and `dungeon-musik/` (pieces, stays local, can be made again). Live: pieces, MB, full fields, making now, waiting fields, made and failed today. Log: `dungeon: musik | …` with fixed words and numbers only.

**AI Dungeon Master: annoying piece:** Next to ⏭ the music bar has a **👎** button for the piece that is playing, for every seat of a running round, guests too. One mark is enough: the piece leaves every table's list at once, the next one fades in, its field no longer counts it and orders a new one. At night (with the fill) the Spark throws marked pieces away with their files and makes new ones; if a field fills up before, marked pieces go first. Each seat marks a piece only once; only the number of marks is kept, no name and no seat. Under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → Music at the table **"N marked"** shows each piece with world, mood and count; **Take back** lets it play again until the night. **▶ Listen** plays the piece right there (the button turns into "■ Stop", only one at a time); listening does not count as played and keeps the mark. Doors: `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/musik/{sid}/nervt` (a profile with a seat) and `POST /api/dungeon/musik/nervt` (a guest with a seat code), own rate limit `dungeonnervt`; `POST /api/admin/dungeon/musik/{sid}/zurueck` and `GET /api/admin/dungeon/musik/{sid}` (listening, marked pieces too) for the admin only. Live: marked pieces and today's marks, log `dungeon: musik | nervt …` with fixed words only.

**AI Dungeon Master: music with a fixed ground tone and a boss mood:** The caption for the music model is a short list of tags in the order ACE-Step 1.5 was trained on: genre (per world), instruments (one way of playing per piece), mood (per world and mood) and production (instrumental, background music, steady groove, soft start, long fade-out, seamless loop); no whole sentences or negations. **Tempo, key and time signature** go to the music engine as metadata (`bpm`, `keyscale`, `timesignature`, `dungeon_musik.meta`): exploring 75, tavern 96 (6/8), tension 72, fight 100, finale 92, victory 96, vault 66, campfire 70 (3/4), boss 104 beats per minute, else 4/4; every world has its minor key (fantasy D minor, horror C minor, sci-fi A minor, pirates E minor), tavern, victory and campfire the relative major, the boss the minor key a fifth up. With all metadata given, the engine's small planner model does not rewrite the caption, it reaches the sound model word for word. What a piece must not be (vocals, speech, hectic, chaotic, abrupt changes) goes along as a **negative prompt** (`lm_negative_prompt`). The music service takes these four fields only when given and checks them itself (`musik_server.metadata`): tempo a whole number from 40 to 220, key only "A" to "G" with "#" or "b" and "major" or "minor", time signature only 2, 3, 4 or 6, negative prompt only lowercase letters, commas and hyphens, at most 200 characters; else 400. Making music and editing music send none of them. The bard's song gets the same: tags and tempo per world, the world's minor key from the same table, 4/4 and its own negative prompt. **Boss:** a ninth mood, picked by the panel by a fixed rule, never the model: the fight at the table still has a foe whose bestiary entry carries the boss mark (`dungeon_musik.boss`). It comes before every other mood; a fallen boss gives the fight back, words in the scene or a name do not count. Every world has its own instruments for it (choir, war drums, low brass, deep bass) and mood (epic, menacing). Every running round asks for 2 boss pieces ahead; while the store has no boss piece, the table plays fight pieces and shows "Fight". The table under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → Music at the table has a column for it. No switch of its own: everything belongs to "Music at the table". Log `dungeon: musik | ok | boss | …`.

**AI Dungeon Master: quality of the music:** The card under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → Music at the table has the tabs **Store** and **Quality**. There the admin picks with segment buttons: **quality** Fast (default: the turbo model, 8 steps), Good (the same model, the more careful solver `dpm3m`, quality correction 0.1, a calmer planner with temperature 0.75) or Best (the SFT sound model with 50 steps). **Best** computes only at night (1 to 6 o'clock); by day the pieces are made with Good. Without the SFT model installed the music service makes Good and says so; the card shows "The finer sound model (SFT) is not installed". The SFT model is prepared (`musikmodell.SFT`, `sft_installed`) but cannot be installed yet: its file comes into the fixed list only with a checked SHA-256. **Length**: Short (1½ to 2 minutes), Medium (2 to 3) or Long (3 to 6, default); a victory stays 30 s. **Loudness**: Normal (default) or Louder (the engine clips the loudest 0.01 % instead of 0.001 % and so lifts quiet parts). All values are fixed in `musikmodell.QUALITAET` and `LAUT`; the panel sends only the names (`quality`, `loud`), the music service checks them (`musik_server.quality`, else 400). There is no guidance control on purpose: the turbo models hold it at 1.0. Making music and editing music send no level and stay at Fast. Changes apply to new pieces, old ones play on. **Test music** (State → Checks) also measures "2B SFT, 50 steps" with the same two songs and the same seed once the SFT model is installed; the samples are kept as `musik-test-2b-sft-1.mp3` and `-2.mp3`. Log: `dungeon: musik | ok | kampf | 240 s | gut | … ms` and `dungeon: musik | einstellung | … | gut | lang | normal | …`; the music service writes the level into `musik: gemacht …`. Storage: `dungeon-musik.json` (with quality, length, loudness; in the backup).

**AI Dungeon Master: sound template per world and mood:** The card under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → Music at the table has a third tab **Sound**. On the left are the moods of the chosen world (dot: changed or standard), on the right the field's template: **genre** (16 fixed genres or "As the world"), **key** (24 keys or the world key), **time** (4/4, 3/4, 6/8), **instruments** (at most 5 from a fixed list of 42), **character** (at most 3), **tempo** (50 to 140 bpm) and **singing** (none or wordless choir). A **package** (orchestral, medieval, Celtic, dark ambient, gothic, synth, epic, seafaring) fills every mood of the world fitting its role with "Apply to all moods"; "Standard" takes back every change of the world, "Back to standard" only the chosen field. There is **no free text field**: the panel takes only values from its lists (`dungeon_musik.pruefen`) and refuses anything else (400). Below it shows what goes to the music model (saved state, fixed words only). Only what differs from the standard is stored, in `dungeon-musik.json` (key `stil`); the template applies to every table. Every piece carries the **fingerprint** of its template: when the template changes, the field's pieces are "outdated" but play on until new ones are there; the night replaces them bit by bit. Endpoints: `PUT /api/admin/dungeon/musik/stil` and `POST /api/admin/dungeon/musik/paket`, admin only, with CSRF, size and rate limit. Live overview: number of outdated pieces and changed sound templates. The apps show the panel's rules page (Android in the panel view, iPhone through the jump link).

**AI Dungeon Master: hear a probe and like:** In the tab **Sound** under Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules → Music at the table the box **Hear a probe** sits below the template. "Make a probe" has the music model make a piece of the chosen field with its saved template, **30 seconds** or at **full length** (the length from the tab Quality). Probes run on the same place for heavy jobs after the voice, **only one at a time** and **at most 20 a day**; meanwhile the card looks again every 5 seconds. Per field the last **3 probes** stay, at night (or after a day) they are gone. Per probe: **▶ Listen**, **Into the store** (only a full-length probe with the current template; it goes into the field as a liked piece), **Variation** (the same planner number `lm_seed`, a new sound number `seed`: sounds related) and **Away**. Every piece and every probe keeps `seed` and `lm_seed`; the music service takes `lm_seed` as an optional field (`musik_server.request_body`, 0 to 2³¹−1). At the table the button **Liked** (heart) sits next to 👎 for the piece playing, for every seat of a running round, guests too, once per seat; only the number is kept. A liked piece stays when a full field is pruned and stands first in the table's list. Doors: `POST /api/admin/dungeon/musik/probe`, `POST /api/admin/dungeon/musik/probe/{pid}/behalten`, `DELETE /api/admin/dungeon/musik/probe/{pid}` (admin only, CSRF, size and rate limit), listening through `GET /api/admin/dungeon/musik/{id}`; `POST /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/musik/{sid}/gefaellt` and `POST /api/dungeon/musik/gefaellt` (a guest with a seat code), own rate limit `dungeongefaellt`. Probes live in `dungeon-musik/probe/`. Live: probes, whether one is being made, today's probes, today's likes and liked pieces; log `dungeon: musik | probe | …` and `dungeon: musik | gefaellt …` with fixed words and numbers only.

**AI Dungeon Master: bigger catalog from the SRD:** Nothing is taken from the DnDAppFiles repo (no licence, copied word for word from protected books). The data comes from the **System Reference Document 5.1 (SRD 5.1)** by Wizards of the Coast under **CC-BY-4.0**, shortened, converted to the game's values and translated into German, plus own entries for horror, sci-fi and pirates. Everything lives as the fixed file `app/panel/dungeon_srd.json` in the repo, nothing is loaded at runtime. The base rules stand on their own: what the five base classes and their items get from the SRD (weapons, spells per level, new classes) is a `dazu` entry in the SRD file. The panel puts it as a layer between `dungeon_regeln.json` and the admin's rules (Features → AI Dungeon Master → Adjust rules); the admin changes or removes any entry by its id (`"aus": true`). Contents: **212 foes** (142 from the SRD, 70 own) with level (challenge rating), life, armour class, attack bonus, damage dice, six values, experience, size, worlds and the conditions they cause; **254 items** (47 weapons, among them magic ones like Flame tongue or Vorpal sword with a fixed rarity, flintlock pistol and musket only for pirates, laser weapons only for sci-fi; 18 armours, 23 jewellery pieces with a fixed value like Gauntlets of ogre power, 19 potions, 85 spells from cantrip to level 5 with classes, 24 tools with keywords for the bonus on fitting checks, 38 gems and art objects to sell); **17 conditions** (blinded, charmed, deafened, frightened, grappled, incapacitated, invisible, paralysed, petrified, poisoned, prone, restrained, stunned, unconscious, exhausted, burning, bleeding) with fixed effects and rounds; the **difficulties** very easy 5, easy 10, medium 15, hard 20, very hard 25, nearly impossible 30; **15 backgrounds** (such as soldier, sage, sailor, smuggler, spacer, investigator) with a bonus and a starting item; **4 peoples** (dragonborn, gnome, half-elf, tiefling) and **7 classes** (barbarian, bard, druid, monk, paladin, sorcerer, warlock) with six abilities each. **Powers only when you have them:** each of the twelve classes has 1 to 2 starting abilities (`start_faehigkeiten`, e.g. rogue: ambush and picking locks, paladin: divine sense and lay on hands); the spellcasting classes (mage, sorcerer, warlock, healer, druid, bard, paladin, ranger) have a spell list per level (`zauber`, level 0 to 5, every spell carries its `grad`), warrior, rogue, barbarian and monk have none. Cantrips (level 0) work any number of times, from level 1 on charges per chapter by hero level count (`ladungen`, level 1: two charges of level 1, level 10: 4/3/3/3/2 as in the D&D table). The data is only data: rolls, damage and loot are decided by the panel by its rules, the model only narrates. Self-test `test_dungeon_srd`: attribution in the file and in the docs, no address except the licence, no id shared with the base rules, `check_rules` on the merged rules empty, every weapon belongs to a class, every condition and starting item exists, every world has at least 15 foes from weak to strong. Attribution: *This work includes material taken from the System Reference Document 5.1 ("SRD 5.1") by Wizards of the Coast LLC and available at https://dnd.wizards.com/resources/systems-reference-document. The SRD 5.1 is licensed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International License available at https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/legalcode.*

**AI Dungeon Master in the car:** everything for the car lives in `dungeon_auto.py`, endpoint `POST /api/profile/dungeon/auto` (`{text, nach, lang, rid, ok, neu, wahl}`, answer `{sagen, n, warten, aus, rid}`, plus `danach`, `ton`, `frage`, `weiter` when they fit). **Summary:** after the narration the panel says with fixed sentences what happened, from the log since the previous narration (rolls, hits, damage, healing, loot, level, down, unconscious, dead, up again, spells, victory; at most `EREIGNIS_MAX` = 8) and whose turn it is. **Asking back:** every action and every command that changes something (`TUN`) first comes back as "… Is that right?"
