(`frage`); only the same request with `ok` acts. **Commands** (`FRAGEN`, only up to 60 characters, else it is the action): fight status, equip, cast (only a spell from the own bag), pick up, potion to a friend, merchant with buy and sell, stat point, ability at a level up, the map's ways, the host's "Go on now". Every command goes through the same rules as at the table (`hero_act`, `act`, `maybe_close`); the panel finds names only among the own things, the wares of the open market and the seats of the round. **Choosing and creating a round:** `rid` picks an own round (only an own seat counts, else the newest), `neu` offers three stories (`WAHL`), `wahl` takes only stories the panel offered; after "yes" `make_round` creates the round with the first free hero, and the prologue starts. **Voices:** with reading aloud on in the round, the car waits up to `TON_WARTEN` = 90 seconds for the round's pieces (narrator and NPC voices from Voice Design) and plays them (`GET /api/profile/dungeon/{rid}/ton/{n}/{k}`, also for the iPhone app), else the Spark voice reads. **Waiting:** the iPhone app asks again without the 10-minute limit (4 seconds, after 2 minutes 10 seconds, up to 4 hours) and only while nothing else runs. Logs only fixed words: `dungeon: auto | anlegen | ok`, `dungeon: auto | kaufen | abgelehnt`, `dungeon: auto | neue runde | angeboten`, `dungeon: weiter | gastgeber | auto`. Status → Live counts the steps in the car. "Where can we go?" says the map's ways as "From here" at the table (`dungeon_karte.wege`, only what a seat may see). "Pause the round" does not exist in the car yet. Self-tests `test_dungeon_auto` (panel) and `test_carplay.DungeonInTheCar` (app).

**AI Dungeon Master: damage types and bosses in the SRD catalog:** Each of the 212 foes carries its damage type (`schaden_art`: slashing, piercing, bludgeoning, fire, cold, lightning, acid, poison, necrotic, radiant, psychic, thunder or force, taken from the strongest attack in the SRD), plus, where the SRD names them, `resistenz` (half damage), `immun` (no damage) and `schwaeche` (double damage), e.g. skeleton: vulnerable to bludgeoning, ghost: immune to cold and poison. The game knows no magic weapons, so immunity to nonmagical slashing, piercing and bludgeoning counts as resistance (stone golem, werewolf), which keeps every foe beatable. `boss` marks foes with legendary actions or from level 10 (aboleth, stone golem, young red dragon, unicorn, archmage) and the own leaders xeno queen, rogue AI, pirate captain and puppeteer; the 70 own foes are set by hand (ghost pirate: resistant to slashing, piercing and bludgeoning, vulnerable to radiant). Each of the 47 SRD weapons has its damage type and `eigenschaften` (light, finesse, reach, thrown, heavy, versatile); spell foci deal force, lasers and plasma fire, light weapons radiant. The panel computes what follows in combat by fixed rules. Self-test `test_dungeon_srd`: types and properties only from these lists, no type in two lists at once, every SRD weapon with a damage type.

The Spark makes music itself with ACE-Step 1.5 (MIT licence), without the internet. The model only comes with the installation on the model computer: `sudo ./install.sh --musik 2b` (2B turbo with the 1.7B planner, recommended), `2b-klein` (0.6B planner, less memory) or `xl` (XL turbo, sounds better, needs more); the first install asks, the default is none. The engine is acestep.cpp (`ace-server`, pinned to one commit, built for the GB10 with CUDA, no torch and no Docker); the GGUF files are pinned by revision and SHA-256 (`musikmodell.py`). The service `speech-spark-musik` (`musik_server.py`, only `127.0.0.1:31004`, with a split install through the TLS-Tor, API key) loads the engine only for a song (`127.0.0.1:31014`, after checking the free memory) and stops it after 10 minutes without a song; the picture and music services share `bedarf.py` for it. A song takes two steps: the small planner sets structure and audio codes, then the sound model turns them into an MP3 (192 kbit/s). The service sets every field itself, takes only an MP3 back and writes its own ID3 mark into it ("KI-erzeugt mit ACE-Step 1.5"). State → Checks → "Test music" makes two fixed samples for every installed model (30 s of piano, 60 s of pop with German lyrics) and shows done in, planning, sound and memory at the peak, plus both samples to listen to. When the engine stops a sample, it says "engine error"; the details are in the log (area "Music"). State → Live: target "Making music" with the last measurement (numbers); service guard part "Making music"; maintenance pause part "Making music"; logs (area "Musik"): `musik: test gestartet`, `musik: test xl 1 ok 12.5 s`, `musik: gemacht 30 s instrumental 12.5 s spitze 8.1 gib`, `musik: engine gestartet 2.0 s`, `musik: engine beendet leerlauf`, `musik: engine nicht gestartet speicher 6.2 gib frei`. API (admin): `GET|POST /api/admin/musik-test`, `GET /api/admin/musik-test/{2b|2b-klein|xl}/{1|2}.mp3`.

**Heavy jobs: one model at a time:** Painting pictures, changing pictures and making music share one place on the model computer: only the picture model or the music model is loaded, never both. A fixed rule without a switch, like Speech first. All jobs stand in one queue in the panel (`schwer.py`), "Bild testen" and "Test music" too. The order is by arrival, but while the loaded kind still waits it goes first, at most 3 times in a row while the other kind waits. When another kind is next, the panel first ends the loaded model (`/v1/bild/stop` or `/v1/musik/stop`), else it stays loaded until its idle time. The services hold the rule themselves: whoever starts its engine first takes the lock `state/bedarf.lock` on the model computer; when the other service holds it, it answers 503 "belegt" (counter `refused_place` in `/health`). With too little memory a job keeps its place, tries again every 30 seconds and only gives up after 10 minutes. Who waits behind others gets a fixed sentence, e.g. "Many requests are running right now, you are in the queue at place 2. It takes a moment, about a minute."; the panel estimates the time from the measured seconds per picture or song and the load time. The card in the chat and under *Me → Pictures* or *Me → Music* shows "Waiting · place 2 of 3 · about a minute" or "waiting for free memory"; when it is done the notice comes. At most 10 heavy jobs wait on the whole Spark, pictures and songs together. State → Live: under "In the background" every picture and song with "waiting for the place (one model at a time)" or "waiting for memory", at the targets "Painting pictures" and "Making music" "hat den Platz" or "wartet auf den Platz", the same in the control centre. Logs (area "Heavy jobs"): `schwer: wartet bild platz 2`, `schwer: dran musik nach 40 s`, `schwer: entladen bild wechsel`, `schwer: wartet musik memory naechster versuch in 30 s`, `schwer: abgesagt musik memory nach 600 s`; on the model computer `bild: engine nicht gestartet belegt`. The memory budget under State → Models counts pictures and music as one place.

**Messages in the conversation:** hovering over a message (on a phone: tapping it) shows a bin and, on answers, a speaker. The bin deletes the message, a question together with its answer; the speaker reads the answer out again.

**Timers and reminders:** "remind me about the oven in 10 minutes" or "remind me tomorrow at 8 about the appointment". Pending reminders show as chips below the toggles (✕ cancels); "which timers are running?" and "cancel the oven timer" work by voice too. When one is due, the page chimes, shows it in the conversation and speaks it; if the tab is in the background, a browser notification appears as well, while the page is open. Reminders need a profile and are kept on the Spark; guests have none. A reminder rings only once: the first device that plays it (an open page, the iPhone app or push) takes it on the Spark, all others stay silent. While a page of the profile is open, push waits 20 seconds so it does not arrive twice. A reminder more than 10 minutes overdue when the page opens shows quietly in the conversation ("Missed"). The iPhone app without Apple push rings with its own alarms; if it was closed, it reports that the next time it opens; together with an open page it can then ring twice, with Apple push it does not. Can be turned off under Features.

**Reminders as notifications** (profiles only): in the "Me" window under "Conversation", "Reminders on this device → Switch on" turns on push notifications; a reminder then arrives also when the page is closed, only on the devices of that profile. This needs https with a valid certificate (e.g. behind a reverse proxy); on an iPhone first add the page to the home screen ("Share → Add to Home Screen"). The Spark only sends to the browsers' push services (Apple, Google, Mozilla, Microsoft), encrypted. Every notification (reminder, "speaking up" note, morning briefing, agent report) arrives once: on the device the profile used last (an open page, the iPhone app or Telegram, at most 12 hours back), else the iPhone app, else Telegram, else the browser set up last. Private notes go over Telegram only when the profile allowed personal data there.

**Notes to the device in use (`zustellung.py`, admin switch Features → Timers and reminders → "Notes to the device in use", `chat.note_device`, off):** With the switch every device only tells the Spark how long ago its last input was: an open page and the Android app every minute in front, at once on the first input after a pause, and "away" in the background (`POST /api/zustellung/da`, only a number and a state); the iPhone app when opened, every minute in front and "away" in the background; the Spark companion with its poll every 30 seconds the computer's idle time (Windows `GetLastInputInfo`, Mac `CGEventSource`). A note then goes to the device with the last input within the last 2 minutes (the device's word no older than 90 seconds), else at once to the phone app (iPhone, else Android), without a phone app by the normal order. A way the profile chose for a kind of note (service watch) still holds. Kept in memory only, nothing is stored. Logs: `push: route aktiv|handy <way>`; Live: the connection "Gerade benutzte Geräte" with the count and the notes routed so today. Self-test `test_zustellung`.

**Tapping a note opens exactly that note (`mitteilungen.py`, no switch):** every note to the iPhone app, the Android app, the companion and the browser stays on the Spark for a day with a random number (in memory only, at most 30 per profile); only that number goes along (through Apple only "New message from the Spark"). Tapping opens the conversation and shows the note as a card above it: title, the whole text, the time and one button the Spark picks by fixed rules from the kind, never the model: about something open its card ("View and handle"), a chat "Open chat", a new mail "Read mail", a confirmation code "Copy code", a way in the text ("Me → Music") "Open: …" as a jump link; else only the text. The cross closes the card. Only the own profile gets its note back (`GET /api/profile/mitteilung`, the app `GET /api/iphone/note?tapped=1`, with a login and a rate limit); older than a day or after a restart of the Spark, the card says so. Browser through the service worker (`#meldung=<number>`), Android app from 1.4.4, the companion on the Mac on a click on the note (Windows shows it in the Meldungen tab). Logs: `push: mitteilung geoeffnet <kind> <way>` or `nicht mehr da`, never the text; Live under events "Mitteilung". Self-test `test_mitteilungen`.

**Following up and "read everywhere" (same switch):** `push.send` remembers every note (title and text in memory only, 6 hours at most). If it went to a device other than the phone app and the Spark chose the way itself, it comes once to the phone after 5 minutes (`zustellung.follow`, every minute), an urgent kind (`vorgaenge.ARTEN` "dringend", the one list; without a Vorgang a reminder counts as kind "erinnerung") after 2 minutes; never a third time. Not when the device was touched afterwards (its signal) or its Vorgang is no longer open. Notes about a Vorgang carry its number (`vg`, to Apple only the number): a tapped note says `gesehen` with the signal (5 numbers at most, `vorgaenge.seen`), the answer `zu` names the Vorgänge no longer open; pages close their browser notifications, the iPhone app takes them out of the notification centre, the companion and the Android app no longer fetch them. Logs: `push: nachgefasst <way> → <phone>`, `push: nachfassen nicht noetig, gesehen`, `push: nachfassen ohne handy`, `push: gesehen <n>`; Live: followed up and waiting as numbers.

**Daily briefing as a notification** (profiles only): below that, each profile sets a time under "Tagesbriefing als Mitteilung" (daily briefing as notification). Every morning at that time a short briefing (appointments, reminders, unread mail, your topics) goes to all devices of the profile with notifications on and is kept in the history; "Aus" (off) turns it off. **Parts at the same time:** calendar, web search topics, weather, the other small services and the mailboxes are read at the same time, each part at most 25 seconds (`chat.BRIEF_PART_S`, the small services 20 seconds each, `extras.PART_S`); a part that does not come in time is left out, the log names it with a fixed word (`briefing: part slow | kalender | 25 s`, also `thema`, `extras`, `mail`). The language model has 90 seconds (`chat.BRIEF_LLM_S`); if it is too slow or not there, a short version from what the panel read itself comes instead of an error (the day's appointments, reminders, which parts were missing; log `briefing: model slow, short version`). The Begleiter's Tagesstart waits at most 125 seconds (`chat.BRIEF_MOST`).

**Daily briefing and calendar:** say "good morning" or "what's on today?" and the assistant reads out today's and tomorrow's appointments, today's reminders and a few lines on your own topics (e.g. "weather Berlin", via your SearXNG); "What do I have on Friday?" works too. Each profile connects up to 8 calendars on the page Calendar, tab Settings; an appointment found in two calendars is read out once. Each one is a CalDAV address with user and app password (iCloud: https://caldav.icloud.com with the Apple ID and an app-specific password from appleid.apple.com; Nextcloud: …/remote.php/dav; Radicale …), or a subscription link with webcal://, webcals:// or https:// (Google: "secret address in iCal format", a public iCloud calendar). New appointments: "put dentist on Tuesday 10 am in my calendar" makes the assistant read out a proposal; only on a plain yes in the same conversation does the panel itself write it to the first CalDAV calendar (optionally with an alarm). Subscription links stay read only. The password stays on the Spark and is never shown again. Guests get no calendar. Can be turned off under Features.

**Changing appointments on the page:** on the Calendar page "+ Appointment" adds one (title, all day or from/to, place, with several calendars the choice), the details of an appointment hold "Change" and "Delete" (delete asks first). "Save" is the person's own yes; the panel checks as for the assistant: the Notaus, only own CalDAV calendars (never a subscription link), never a repeating appointment, and changing or deleting with the server's etag (If-Match), so a change made elsewhere is never overwritten. It finds exactly the appointment the page shows (calendar, title and start); if it is no longer there like that, the answer is "changed or repeating" (409). Into a calendar shared with "See and add" the page adds only when it is chosen; changing and deleting stay with the owner. Endpoints `POST` and `PUT /api/profile/calendar/event` (own browser login only, rate limit `kalenderaendern`); Live shows at the calendar only how many appointments were changed on the page today; log `calendar: appointment saved|changed|deleted` or `page change refused, not found`.

**Sharing calendars:** Features → Calendar and daily briefing → "Kalender teilen" (off by default; once released it applies to every profile at once, only the admin blocks single profiles; never guests). Under Calendar → Settings every connected calendar has "Share": for each other profile "Not shared", "See only" or "See and add", with the own code (level Confirm, `PUT /api/profile/calendar/{id}/share`, rate limit `kalenderteilen`); "Not shared" takes it back at once. The other profile sees the calendar on its Calendar page in its own colour as "Name · Calendar" and under Settings in "Shared with you"; its assistant reads it with `calendar_events` ("Was hat Sabine morgen?", "Hat Tom am Freitag Zeit?", the Weiche sends such questions to the calendar). It is read with the owner's access, address and password stay in the owner's file. A private appointment (CLASS PRIVATE or CONFIDENTIAL) shows at the other profile only as "belegt" with its time, without title and place. With "See and add" the other profile's assistant enters an appointment there only when the calendar is named ("put … in Sabine's calendar") and only after that profile's yes; changing, deleting and undo stay with the owner. The daily briefing, Proactive and Today show only the own calendars. Live shows at the calendar only how many shares there are from how many profiles. Log: `calendar: share set see|add`, `share taken back`, `share refused`.

**Page Calendar:** the calendar is a page of its own in the menu (on the left below "Assistant", on phones through the card "Appointments" under Today). Tab **Appointments**: day, week, month or list, paging with ‹ Today ›; all-day appointments on top, overlapping ones side by side; each calendar has its colour; a tap shows time, place and calendar. A calendar that does not answer is named on top as "Not reached"; one still loading after 8 seconds is named "Still loading" and appears by itself once it is there (see the cache below). Tab **Settings**: connect and remove calendars, topics for the briefing, "Show next appointments". Every way "Calendar → Settings → …" in help, hints and apps leads there; links to `#me=calbox` open the page. Read only through `GET /api/profile/calendar/view` (own profile, at most 42 days, rate limit); titles and places reach the page only as text. Appointments can be changed on the page (see above) and through the assistant ("Move the appointment …").

**Calendar fast: cache and background refresh:** The Spark keeps the appointments of each calendar month by month in the panel's memory (`calendars.py`), never on disk. Page, assistant, briefing and "On its own" read the same months; a month is fetched once even when several ask at the same time. Where an address's calendars are (CalDAV discovery, several requests at iCloud) is kept for 6 hours. The page waits at most 8 seconds: whatever is still loading keeps loading in the background and lands in the cache, and the page asks again by itself after 3 seconds (at most 5 times). A month older than 5 minutes is shown on the page at once and fetched anew behind it (up to 24 hours old); the assistant waits for a fresh one. Every 5 minutes the panel fetches this and the next month anew for every profile that read its appointments in the last 24 hours. When an appointment or a setting changes, the cache is dropped at once, and a fetch begun before stores nothing. A big calendar is parsed outside the event loop. No switch. Logs: `calendar: view loading | n of m calendars`, `calendar: kept fresh | n calendars of m profiles`, `calendar: refresh failed (error)`. The uConsole (`spark-tui kalender`) shows "Lädt noch: …".

**Calendar without panel stalls:** the Spark works out each appointment (expanding repetitions) once per calendar month and keeps the result in memory (at most 30,000 entries, never on disk); only a changed appointment or a new month is computed again. All of it runs in one background thread at low priority, never two at once, and lets the panel go after every appointment.

**Calendar renewed: full width, floating card, quick add, drag, share window:** the Calendar page uses the whole width. A tap on an appointment opens a card floating beside it over the view (on a phone a sheet from below): calendar, title, when, where and, for own CalDAV appointments, "Change", "+1 day", "+1 week" and "Delete". Esc closes it, E changes, Del deletes (after asking). Above the view sits "Quick add": the panel reads a line like "Zahnarzt morgen 10 Uhr bei Dr. Weber" by fixed rules, without the language model (`calendars.quick`, `GET /api/profile/calendar/quick`, reads only): day (heute, morgen, übermorgen, today, tomorrow, a weekday, "nächsten Montag", "in 3 Tagen", 24.12. or 24.12.2026), time (10 Uhr, 10:30, 14-15 Uhr, 3pm), place after "bei", "in" or "im", the rest is the title. Without a time the appointment is all day, without a day it is today or, when the time has passed, tomorrow; short weekdays (Mo, So) only count as "am So" or "So.". What was understood shows as chips below; it is saved only with "Add" or Enter, into the first own calendar, the same way as the form. "More …" opens the form already filled in. In week and day own appointments are dragged with the mouse to another time (quarter hours) or day; on a phone "+1 day" and "+1 week" move them. After every move "Undo" stays above the view for a minute. Under Calendar → Settings every calendar is a card with its kind (CalDAV, iCloud, subscription) and "Shared with …"; the address and "Remove" are under "⋯". "Share" opens a window with one row per profile and Not · See · See + add; the search filters while typing, those with access are on top, "Save" sends only the changes (each with the code). "Shared with you" is a section of its own. The briefing topics are chips with ✕, a new one is added with Enter and saved at once (at most three). No switch of its own.

**Sharing only the chosen calendar:** an address can hold several calendars (an iCloud or Nextcloud account); a share holds exactly one of them. With several, the share window asks "Which calendar of this account" (`GET /api/profile/calendar/{id}/parts`, reads only), the server asks for the calendars anew when saving and refuses a share without the choice. Per person `share_parts` is stored (profile → the calendar's address); the other person reads and adds only there (`calendars._only`). A share of such an account without that choice shows nothing until it is shared again; addresses with a single calendar need no choice.

**Page E-mail:** mail is a page of its own in the menu (on the left below "Calendar", only with reading e-mail; on phones through Today). Tab **Inbox**: the newest 50 messages of the last 14 days from every mailbox, unread ones bold with a dot, search by sender or subject, "Unread only"; a tap shows the message as plain text (HTML becomes text, pictures and links from outside stay off, long links become "[Link]"), attachments only by name. It is read with PEEK, so the message stays unread. Tab **For you** (only when "For you from mail" is released): what was found in the mails. Tab **Settings**: mailboxes, tidying, mail style, parcels. Every way "E-mail → Settings → …" jumps there; links to `#me=mailbox` open the page. Reading goes only through `GET /api/profile/mail/inbox` and `GET /api/profile/mail/message` (own profile, rate limit "post", log without contents). Replying, archiving and moving see below; anything else through the assistant.

**E-mail fast and replying below the mail:** All mailboxes of a profile are read at the same time. The list of the E-mail page lives in the panel's memory (`mail.py`, never on disk): the page shows the last state at once (up to 24 hours old) and fetches anew behind it when it is older than 2 minutes. Without a kept state it waits at most 8 seconds, then shows "The mailboxes are still loading …" and asks again by itself after 3 seconds (at most 5 times); the fetch goes on in the background. Requests for the same list at once start one fetch; the assistant's shorter list ("unread mail of the week") comes from the page's longer one when that holds all of it. Opened messages stay in memory for 30 minutes (at most 40 per profile), and the panel fetches the 10 newest ahead in the background (with PEEK, they stay unread). Every 5 minutes the panel fetches the list anew for every profile that read its mail on the page or through the assistant in the last 24 hours (tidying and "For you" do not count). Read/unread, archive and move change the kept list at once without a new fetch; a fetch begun before stores nothing. No switch. "Reply" opens the reply form right below the mail, as in a mail program; the mail stays above it, with preview and send. The tab "For you" reads all mailboxes at the same time, shows the last reading at once (up to 24 hours old) and reads one older than 10 minutes anew behind it; the first reading waits at most 8 seconds, then "Reading your mails …" and the page asks again by itself (log `fuerdich: laedt noch`). Logs: `mail: inbox loading | n mailboxes`, `mail: kept fresh | n profiles, m messages ahead`, `mail: refresh failed (error)`.

**E-mail like a mail client:** the E-mail page uses the whole width: the list on the left grouped by day (today, yesterday, weekday with date), each row with the sender's initials in their own colour; a wide reading pane on the right, on a phone the mail instead of the list. When "For you" found something (bill, contract, suspicious …), a chip says so in the row (only with "For you from mails" on). Keys on a computer: J/K go through the mails, Esc closes, R replies (with "Send mail"), E archives, U toggles read/unread (with "Order mail", with "Undo"). On a phone: swipe left archives, swipe right toggles read/unread (with "Order mail"). "As appointment" opens the calendar with the subject in "Quick add"; the panel reads it by fixed rules, it is added only with "Add". No switch and no endpoint of its own.

**Always a way back:** an open mail, an answer and a new mail have a button at every width: on the phone "‹ Back" or "‹ Discard", on the computer "✕ Close" or "✕ Discard". Esc closes the answer even from the text field. With text typed, the Spark asks "Discard the draft?".

**Suggested answers:** when answering on the E-mail page or in the iPhone app, three short answers in different directions are ready (e.g. accept, decline, ask back), in the mail's language and form of address, signed with the profile's name. The Spark prepares them while the mail is open and keeps them 15 minutes in memory (never on the disk). A tap puts a suggestion into the text, free to change; "shorter", "friendlier", "more formal" and "in English" rework it with the Schreibhilfe. It is sent only with "Send" in the preview. Part of "Send mail" (no own switch). Only the own language model reads the mail; it is data, never a command (between fixed marks, the answer taken only as text, at most three, cut). The suggestions ask the model directly, not through the background work line; speech and answers with priority go first (at most 20 seconds of waiting), during a maintenance pause a fixed sentence comes, at most two suggestions at the same time on the Spark. When the model is busy or too slow the Spark answers 503 with a sentence, and "Try again" stands below. Log: "mail: suggestions | 3 in 2.1 s", "mail: rewrite kuerzer", `mail: suggestions busy (belegt|gespraech|wartung)`, `mail: suggestions failed (TimeoutError) | 90 s`; Status → Live shows today's number at the E-mail box.

**Warming up:** when a profile signs in or opens the panel or the app, the Spark reads ahead in the background: this and next month from all its calendars (shared ones too), the e-mail listing with the newest messages, "For you" and the address book. Calendar, E-mail and Today show it at once; Today uses the same memory as the page Calendar. Afterwards the refreshes every 5 minutes keep it fresh. Rules: only functions the profile may use, never for guests; a profile at most every 10 minutes, at most two profiles at the same time; every step waits while somebody speaks. Everything stays in memory. Status → Live shows for Calendar and Mail only how many profiles are warm and how long ago; log (area Priority): "vorwaermen: done" with numbers.

**Per-profile store, stage A (`vorrat.py`, no switch):** when the person asks about appointments, weather or parcels, the tool takes the data from its service's memory while it is fresh (calendar 10 minutes, weather 30, parcels 15; `vorrat.DIENSTE`). When older (up to 24 hours, weather and parcels up to 6), the service asks anew and the answer waits at most 2 seconds; if nothing comes, the assistant names the older reading with its time ("as of 14:05"), and the new one is ready for the next question. "Look again", "refresh" or "reload" always ask the server. Mail follows the same rule with the encrypted mail store. Pages and background work keep their rules. Only for released functions, never for guests; it only changes when data is read. Measuring: every spoken answer keeps how long the tools waited and, per service, whether the data came from the store, as an older reading or after waiting (fixed words). Status → Checks shows it under "Per service: store, last 7 days" and in the column "Tools wait"; Status → Live shows for Calendar, E-mail, Weather and Parcels "today n questions, x % from the store". Log (area Priority): "vorrat: older reading" with service and age in minutes, never a profile or content.

**Per-profile store, stages B and C (`vorrat.py`, `halive.py`, no switch):** one round every minute (`vorrat.loop`) compares the calendars every 2 minutes and, for every profile active in the last 24 hours, refreshes what is older than its fresh age: weather at home, parcels and "For you" (as soon as a mail is newer than its last reading). At most two profiles at the same time, each step waits while somebody speaks, only released functions, never guests; mail has its own round, its times for the assistant (10 minutes fresh, 2 seconds wait) come from `vorrat.DIENSTE`. Weather (only at the own place) and parcels keep their last reading sealed in the profile folder (`wetter-vorrat.bin`, `pakete-vorrat.bin`, through `vorrat.Vorrat`, written at most every 30 or 5 minutes); after a restart the assistant answers the first question from it at once, with its time when older. Parcels only from mailboxes read completely; deleted with the last mailbox or the place, after 14 days without a reading and with the profile. Another place asked for once stays in memory only. Warming up also reads weather and parcels at sign-in; a device in use right now (signal of "Note to the device in use") warms too (at most every 10 minutes). The daily briefing is written 10 minutes before its time and sent on the minute (a text written ahead counts for 20 minutes); calendar, mail, weather and parcels are then already in memory. Home Assistant live: for active profiles with Home Assistant released the Spark keeps one WebSocket connection (at most 4 on the Spark), asks for all states once and then listens for changes (state_changed); questions, states, history and finding a device to switch use this copy. The connection only reads, goes through the guard (home network rules, checked address), uses TLS as set, ends with My kill switch and a block. At most 5000 devices and 8 MB per message, nothing on disk. A connection whose copy stood for 5 minutes counts as good: when it breaks the Spark reconnects after 10 seconds, otherwise the wait doubles (60 to 900 seconds). A WebSocket ping every 20 seconds (answer within 30) and TCP keepalive keep proxies and routers open. Status → Live: at Calendar "Vorrat: today n rounds, m services refreshed", at Home Assistant "live: n connections, today m changes", at Weather and Parcels "versiegelt bei n Profilen" (sealed for n profiles). Log: "vorrat: kept fresh", "homeassistant: live connected | n entities", `homeassistant: live lost (ConnectionClosedError) | code 1006 | up 812 s | retry in 10 s` (1006: cut without a goodbye, mostly a proxy; then add the custom header "WebSocket" in the Synology's reverse proxy), numbers and the profile as a short name only; Status → Logs shows a fixed hint for it.

**Up to date (`frisch.py`, no new switch):** fixed rules of the panel hold every source against today, never the model. A stand always names its day ("as of heute 06:40", "as of gestern 18:00", `frisch.stand`), for calendar, weather, parcels and mail. The weather works out "today" and "tomorrow" from the clock at the place; when today is missing from the forecast it says so, and "Jetzt …" (now) comes only from a reading of the last 2 hours. The morning briefing names every mail with day and time ("heute 06:12", "gestern 21:40") and only what was unread when read; a mail listing is at most 24 hours old. When the person asks for today's or the latest (fixed words like heute, aktuell, neueste, Nachrichten, today, latest, news), SearXNG searches only the last day (for "gestern", "diese Woche" the last week), for news also in its news category; every hit carries its date ("Datum: gestern 21:00", "Datum: unbekannt"), older ones are dropped. The briefing's topics always search the day's news. When the SearXNG knows no period it searches without and the dates decide; when nothing of today is found the result says so with the date of the newest hit. When a service came only with an older reading or a search found nothing of today, and the answer does not say so, the panel adds "Nicht ganz aktuell: Wetter: Stand gestern 23:10 Uhr." (not quite current; a rule of the answer check, off with it); the daily briefing's push too. A mail opened in the Spark counts as read at once (computer and iPhone, only when "Order mail" is released; it sets "read" on the mail server). Status → Live at Web search: "tagesaktuell: today n searches with a period, m without a hit of today, k old hits dropped, j stand notes"; log (area Chat): "frisch: search dropped", "frisch: search without a current hit", "frisch: hint said", "frisch: weather …", fixed words and numbers only.

**New mail at once (`mailidle.py`, admin switch Features → Reading e-mail → "Neue Mails sofort", `chat.mail_idle`, off):** for profiles that read their mail in the last two hours the Spark keeps a connection to the inbox open (IMAP IDLE). When the server reports a new (EXISTS) or moved mail (EXPUNGE), the profile's mail rules run (with "Mails ordnen"), the E-mail page's list is fetched anew with the newest mails, and "Für dich" hears of it (`mailidle.on_new`); at most every 20 seconds per profile, never while someone speaks. At most 4 connections on the Spark, the newest readers first; a server without IDLE stays with the fetch every five minutes (`mail.keep_fresh`, asked again after a day). The connection only reads (EXAMINE) and waits in `select()` on its socket without holding the loop or a lock; IDLE is renewed every 24 minutes, a lost connection is retried after 1 minute (up to 15 minutes). From the server only EXISTS, EXPUNGE, BYE and the answer to DONE count; over-long lines end the connection. My emergency stop, the admin's block and the switch close it. Live (node E-mail): open connections and new mails today, numbers only; the log only fixed words and the profile as a short name.

**Mail supply (`mailvorrat.py` on `vorrat.py`, part of "Read mail", no switch of its own):** the Spark keeps per mailbox the newest headers (sender, subject, time, read and flag marks; at most 300 from the last 30 days) and up to 40 mail texts per profile (fetched ahead and opened ones, each at most 4000 characters of plain text, never attachments, HTML or pictures) in the profile folder (`mail-vorrat.bin`), sealed with AES-GCM, bound to profile and name, written at most every 5 minutes; without a key in memory only. It is filled only from what the Spark reads anyway (the page's listing, a longer listing every 30 minutes, texts fetched ahead), never with a connection of its own. When a mailbox does not answer, the assistant, the briefing and the page answer from the supply with its time ("as read on 20.10. at 14:05"), and a mail opens from the kept text. After a restart the Mail page shows the listing at once from the supply and fetches it anew behind. For the assistant: a listing up to 10 minutes old counts, an older one is fetched anew for at most 2 seconds, otherwise it names the older state with its time. Ordering on the page follows in the supply. It is deleted with the mailbox, with all mailboxes, with the profile and after 14 days unread. `vorrat.py` is the one way to build such supplies. Live (node Mail): profiles with a supply and answers from it today, numbers only. **Folders at once and folder menu:** the folder list (names, what for, unread) is in the supply too; after a restart "Mailboxes" is there at once (at most 7 days old) and read anew behind; a new, renamed or removed folder drops it. The "⋯" at an own folder (or a right click) opens a menu beside it: Rename, New folder inside, Remove folder (empty ones only); on a phone as a sheet from below, Esc or a click beside closes it. Renaming and new folders are typed right in the row (Enter keeps, Esc drops). On the iPhone the same with a long press or a swipe. **Moving without reloading:** a moved, archived or deleted mail leaves the list at once, the folder list stays and recounts its unread numbers itself; the Spark rereads the folders quietly behind (a list shown is never replaced by an older one). When the Spark refuses, the mail comes back with the reason. The same on the iPhone.

**Load older mail (`mail.more`, part of "Read mail", no switch of its own):** at the end of the list the Spark fetches the next 50 older mails, in the inbox and every folder, down to the mailbox's first mail. A placeholder of fixed height keeps anything from jumping; at the end it says "No older mail". A search goes over the whole mailbox: the mail server searches sender, subject and text (umlauts too, `CHARSET UTF-8`), page by page; `has:attachment` is checked by the Spark on the header. The continuation mark `vor` (per mailbox the UID of the next page) names only own mailboxes, anything else is refused. Each mailbox goes on in its own order, the mailboxes merged by date; mails with a new number but an old date come with the second page. Fetching only reads (headers), nothing onto the disk or into the mail supply; the pages stay 10 minutes in memory, at most 10 per profile, and go as soon as a mail is ordered. Own rate limit (30 pages a minute). `spark-tui mails --anzahl N` shows up to 200 mails, `spark-tui mail N` opens any of them. Live (node Mail): older pages today, the number only.

**Spark calendar, stage 1: a copy on the Spark:** every CalDAV calendar (iCloud, Nextcloud, …) has a copy on the Spark. The first time the Spark fetches it whole, after that only the changes (WebDAV sync with a sync token; without it, compared by the tags of the appointments; a server that can do neither is read month by month). The Calendar page, Today, the assistant and the briefing read from the copy in milliseconds. For profiles active in the last 24 hours the Spark compares every 2 minutes, after an own change right at the next look. The copy is on the disk only encrypted (`kalender-spiegel/`, the same key as for the passwords), at most 5000 appointments per calendar, and is deleted with the calendar or the profile. Subscription links (`.ics`, `webcal://`) are read directly. Status → Live shows for Calendar the number of copies, appointments and the last comparison; log: "calendar: mirror full" or "changed" with numbers.

**Spark calendar, stage 2: changes at once:** what you add, change or delete on the Calendar page or in the iPhone app is in the copy at once and shows without waiting; it goes to the calendar server behind it. To change, the Spark takes the appointment and its tag from the copy. If the server refuses (changed elsewhere, not reachable), the Spark takes the change back, the server's version stands, and the page shows "Not saved, the calendar refused" with the title for 10 minutes. Changes by voice wait for the server, because a spoken confirmation cannot be taken back. Log: "calendar: saved behind" or "not saved behind" with the status.

**Spark calendar, stage 3: a calendar without an account:** under Calendar → Settings → "Add calendar" → provider "Spark calendar" (iPhone: Connect accounts → Calendar → "Create a Spark calendar") you create a calendar only on the Spark, without iCloud, Google or Nextcloud. Appointments come via the Calendar page, the iPhone app or voice ("Put dentist Tuesday 10 am into Zuhause"); Today, the briefing, changing, deleting, "undo that" and calendar sharing work as with any calendar. Technically it is a calendar entry with the address `https://spark.invalid/<id>/`, which never reaches the network: `sparkkalender.py` answers the CalDAV requests itself, bound to exactly this entry of this profile. Stored only encrypted in the profile folder (`users/<id>/spark-kalender.json`); without the Spark's key none is created. It moves with the profile and is part of its backup; removing it (after a question) deletes its appointments. Checked: iCalendar with an appointment, at most 256 KB per appointment and 5000 appointments per calendar. `spark.invalid` cannot be entered as an address. Logs: `calendar: spark calendar made`, `calendar: spark calendar deleted | count`; Live (Calendar) counts Spark calendars and appointments. Stage 4 makes it reachable for calendar apps.

**Spark calendar, stage 4: the Spark as a calendar server:** a profile's Spark calendars show in the iPhone's calendar (or in Thunderbird, DAVx5) and can be changed there; the Spark is a CalDAV server for them under `/caldav/` (`kalenderserver.py`). Admin switch Features → Calendar and daily briefing → "Calendar server for calendar apps" (`chat.calendar_server`, off). The profile makes an app password with its code under Calendar → Settings → "For calendar apps" (or on the iPhone under Connect accounts); it is shown once, the Spark keeps only a check value (`users/<id>/kalender-server.json`). On the iPhone under Settings, Apps, Calendar, Calendar accounts add a CalDAV account: server `https://<address>/caldav/`, user = the profile id shown, password = the app password; the app finds the rest itself (`/.well-known/caldav`). Works at home and through the proxy. Protection: only the own Spark calendars under the own path (anything else is 404), wrong passwords lock like a PIN, every request counts against the rate limit, requests from foreign web pages (Origin) are refused, Mein Notaus and the admin's block close the door, every appointment is checked as in stage 3 (iCalendar, size, count, etags). Name and colour stay on the Spark (PROPPATCH and MKCALENDAR refused). "Delete access" ends it at once, a new app password replaces the old one. Logs: `calendar: caldav saved|deleted|not saved (status)`, `calendar: caldav login refused`, `calendar: caldav password made|removed`; Live (Calendar): profiles with an app password and the day's requests, changes and refusals.

**Appointments: changeable or read only, with their text:** on the Calendar page an appointment the page cannot change carries a 🔒 in week, day and list; its card names the reason in fixed words (`calendars._why`): subscription link (iCal), shared calendar, repeating appointment (change the series in the calendar app) or a calendar that cannot be changed here. The card also shows the appointment's text (DESCRIPTION, "Note") and link (URL), as text only: at most 2000 characters, no control characters, links only with http or https, never opening by themselves; long notes show 8 lines and "Read on". A private appointment from a shared calendar stays "belegt" (busy), without text or link. The same in the iPhone app (lock, section "Note", reason in the card).

**Order mail (Features → Reading e-mail → "Mails ordnen", `chat.mail_manage`, off):** an opened mail shows "Read"/"Unread", "Archive" and a folder choice; after archiving or moving, "Undo" brings the mail straight back into the inbox (by its Message-ID). The button is the person's own yes. These actions in `postfach.py` set the `\Seen` flag, put a mail into the archive (folder flagged `\Archive`, else "Archive"/"Archiv") or an ordinary own folder, and back; never into trash, junk, sent or drafts, never deleted; without IMAP MOVE only COPY and then UID EXPUNGE of exactly the confirmed original. Notaus holds every action off. Endpoints `GET /api/profile/mail/folders` and `POST /api/profile/mail/act` (own browser login only, own mailboxes only, rate limit `postordnen`); Live shows only how many mails were ordered on the page today; log `mail: page <action> done|refused, not found|refused, notaus|failed`, never sender, subject or folder.

**E-mail as a full mail client, stage A: folders (part of "Mails ordnen", off):** above the list stands "📁 Inbox ▾"; it opens, in the list's place, every folder of every mailbox: inbox, drafts, sent, archive, own folders indented, junk and trash, each with its number of unread mails. A tap opens the folder (reading only: `EXAMINE`, `BODY.PEEK`, the newest 50; search and "Unread only" work on it). "+ New folder" makes a folder at the top of the mailbox, "⋯" on an own folder offers "Rename", "Folder inside" and "Remove (empty only)". Folders of the mail program (inbox, sent, drafts, trash, junk, archive) and those of "Tidy mails" are never renamed or removed; a folder holding mails or folders stays. Rename and remove ask for the code (`stufen.LIST`, confirm). On every mail: "★ Flag" (flag `\Flagged`, ★ in the list), from a folder also "Move to … Inbox"; mails are moved only out of the inbox, own folders and the archive, never out of sent, drafts, trash or junk. Multiple selection: a tick per row, above it "N selected" with Read, Unread, Flag, Archive, Move (into a folder of one mailbox) and "Clear selection", at most 50 at a time, "Undo" brings moved ones back. Endpoints `GET /api/profile/mail/tree` (kept 60 s), `GET /api/profile/mail/folder`, `POST /api/profile/mail/acts`, `POST /api/profile/mail/acts/back`, `POST /api/profile/mail/folder/new|rename|remove`; `GET /api/profile/mail/message` and `POST /api/profile/mail/act` take the folder (`folder`, `src`). All with the own login, own mailboxes only, size limit, rate limit `postordnen`/`post`, Notaus holds every change off. Live counts folder actions with the ordered mails (a number). Log `mail: tree shown | <folders>`, `mail: page many <action> done | <number>`, `mail: folder new|rename|remove done|refused|failed`, never a folder name, sender or subject. iPhone: folder button top left (pick, make, swipe to rename or remove), "Select" for the multiple selection, flag by swipe and in the mail.

**E-mail as a full mail client, stage B: rules (`mailregeln.py`, part of "Mails ordnen", off):** every mail has "⚙ Rule from this …": a form with the mail's sender and subject (subject without Re:/AW:/Fwd:, both changeable), "When" (sender as address or @domain, optionally a word in the subject, a recipient, only with attachment) and "Then" (into a folder of this mailbox, mark as read, ★ flag, not in "For you"). Trash, junk, sent and drafts are never a target, nothing is deleted. "Dry run" counts matching mails in the inbox of the last 90 days and changes nothing. After saving, the panel asks whether to sort existing mails; that asks for the code (`stufen.LIST`, confirm), takes at most 50 per mailbox, "Undo" brings them back. Otherwise a rule takes only newly arriving mails; the panel looks every 5 minutes (at most 200 mails per run). The first matching rule decides. The tab E-mail → Rules shows all rules with "This month N", Pause, Move up, Edit and Delete, below them the sender rules of "Tidy mails". At most 100 rules per profile, file `users/ID/mail-regeln.json`. A rule is fixed data, never a command; the language model never sees it. Endpoints `GET|POST /api/profile/mail/rules`, `POST /api/profile/mail/rules/test`, `POST /api/profile/mail/rules/order`, `POST /api/profile/mail/rules/{id}/apply`, `DELETE /api/profile/mail/rules/{id}`; own login, own rules and mailboxes only, size limit, rate limit `postordnen`, Notaus holds rules off. Live: "N rules at P profiles, today X mails sorted". Log `mail: rules run | N taken, E errors`, `mail: rule apply done|refused|failed`, never a sender, subject, folder or word of a rule. iPhone: "Rules" in E-mail (⋯) and "Rule from this …" in every mail, with dry run, sorting and "Undo".

**E-mail as a full mail client, stage C: search, forward, attachments, drag:** the search knows, besides words, `von:anna`, `betreff:rechnung`, `hat:anhang`, `ist:markiert` and `ist:ungelesen` (English `from:`, `subject:`, `has:attachment`, `is:flagged`, `is:unread`), plus the buttons "★ Flagged" and "📎 Attachment". The panel compares these words as text with sender, subject and header lines (`mail._words`, `mail.fits`), nothing is ever run; in the inbox the last 14 days, in a folder the newest 300 mails. Mails with an attachment show 📎. **Fetch an attachment** (with "Mails ordnen"): each attachment stands as "⬇ name (KB)"; a tap downloads it (`GET /api/profile/mail/file`, at most 25 MB, own mailboxes only), always as a file (`application/octet-stream`, `nosniff`, `sandbox`), never shown in the page. **Forward** (with "Mails senden"): opens the form with "Fwd: …"; below your lines (may stay empty) stands the mail with From, Date, Subject and To, attachments only with "Send the attachments along" and together at most 20 MB. It goes out only from the preview; from a folder only with "Mails ordnen". **Drag into a folder** (on a computer, with "Mails ordnen"): drag a row or all ticked ones onto the "Drop here" bar, the same as moving, with "Undo". Live counts fetched attachments. Log `mail: file shown | <KB>`, `mail: file refused|failed`, never a file name. iPhone: a search field with the same words as suggestions, attachments open by a tap in Quick Look, "Forward" in every mail; instead of dragging "Move to …".

**The bar of a mail:** the buttons of an open mail sit in one bar that stays on top while scrolling: "Reply" large with its word, beside it symbols in three groups: writing (forward, as appointment), ordering (read/unread, ★ flag, archive, move to … as a folder menu) and rule from this. Hovering a symbol shows its name and key: R reply, F forward, T as appointment, U read, S flag, E archive, V move, G rule, J and K browse, Esc closes (menu first, then the mail). On a phone the five most used sit at the thumb below (reply, forward, archive, folder, appointment), on top back, ✎ new mail, ★ and ⋯ (read, rule from this); the iPhone app the same in its bottom bar and top right. "✎ New mail" always stands above the list. A function that is off shows no button.

**Folders, dragging, deleting and the rule hint:** the Mail page shows the list, the mail and the folders as three equal boxes with a head line (the list with its name, count and new mails). The folders sit on the left (Apple style, see below); the "Folders" button or the key O shows and hides them, the browser remembers it. The folders of the mail program carry their own names and symbols (Inbox, Sent, Drafts, Archive, Trash, Junk). One or several mails can be dragged onto a folder; while dragging the folders open, and the archive and trash symbols of the open mail take it too; afterwards "Undo" is there. On a phone the folders come as a sheet, moving goes through the menu.

**Deleting** has its own admin switch under Functions → Read mail → Order mail → "Delete mail" (`chat.mail_delete`, off). Only the person deletes, on the page (trash symbol, the Delete key, dragging onto the trash, "Delete" for several): the mail goes into the mailbox's trash (the folder with `\Trash`), "Undo" brings it back. "Delete for good" exists only inside the trash, after its own question, and only for exactly that mail: the mailbox must know UIDPLUS, the Message-ID must match, then `UID STORE \Deleted` and `UID EXPUNGE` of that UID only, never a bare EXPUNGE. The model, rules, "For you" and the voice never delete; an ordinary move never goes into the trash. Live (node Mail): "of them N deleted", numbers only.

**Conversation:** under an opened mail stand the other mails of the same conversation, oldest first, one line each (who, when, first words; own ones as "You"), opened with their own text without quotes. The Spark finds them by Message-ID (References, In-Reply-To and replies to this mail) in the inbox, Sent and the mail's own folder, read only (EXAMINE, PEEK), at most 10, and checks every id exactly (`mail.thread`, `GET /api/profile/mail/thread`, loaded after the mail so opening never waits). No switch of its own, part of "Read e-mail". Live: "N conversations shown today". iPhone: the same section "Conversation".

**Only the own text:** `mail.plain` cuts quote heads (also those broken over two lines like "On Mon, Oct 20, 2026 at 10:00 AM Anna <" / "anna@x.de> wrote:"), the Outlook divider and lines like "Sent from my iPhone" or "Get Outlook for iOS"; summaries, "For you" and reply suggestions get only the sender's words (`test_mail_text`).

**Rule suggestions from your filing:** at night between 2 and 5 the Spark looks once per profile into the folders below the main folder of "Tidy mails" (the KI folder, `tidy` `root`), only when something changed there (`STATUS` MESSAGES and UIDNEXT; read only, headers only, per folder the newest 300 of the last 30 days). Only what the person filed counts: mails moved by "Tidy mails" (its log by Message-ID), senders with a rule and pairs on the never list are left out. Fixed rules, never the model: at least 5 mails of a sender in one folder and at least 80 % of its mails there (the inbox counted too); a domain (`@company.de`) only when several addresses all lie in the same folder and none in the inbox, never freemail (gmail, gmx, web.de, iCloud …); at most 3 new suggestions a night, the strongest first, open for 7 days at most. They stand on top of E-mail → Rules as "Suggestions from your filing" with "Make the rule and file" (the rule and the sender's mails in the inbox, with "Undo"), "Rule only" and "Do not ask again"; a rule comes only after the click. No switch of its own, part of "Order mails". Live: "N suggestions from filing"; the log has numbers only. iPhone: the same list on top of "Rules". **As a card:** each suggestion also stands in the morning as a card "Rule suggestion" in the assistant and under Open (`vorgaenge.QUELLEN["regel"]`) with "Make the rule and file them" (only while some of its mails lie in the inbox), "Make the rule", "Don't ask again"; the buttons run the same checks as the page (no buttons without "Mails ordnen"); taken on the page or after 7 days the card closes.

**Rule hint:** per profile the Spark counts how often mails of the same sender are moved into the same folder (`mailregeln.learn`, only address, folder, count, 30 days). The third time a hint below "Undo" offers "Make the rule and apply it now" (the rule and the fitting mails in the inbox, with "Undo"), "Only the rule" and "Don't ask again" (remembered for that pair). If the sender already has a rule, it does not ask. Live: "today N rule hints". iPhone app: delete on swipe, in the mail and for several, "Undo" and the rule hint above the list, in the trash "Delete for good" with a question, the mail program's folders with their own names.

**No ping-pong with mail rules:** like "Inbox tidying", the mail rules remember every mail they moved: Message-ID, mailbox, target folder, rule and time, 30 days, at most 3000, in `mail-regeln.json` (no sender, no subject). Both sorters ask one shared list (`mailregeln.spark_moved`, `mailregeln.user_moved`): did the Spark move this mail, and did the person put it elsewhere since? When someone puts a mail a rule moved back into the inbox in Apple Mail or on the page (new UID, same Message-ID), no rule touches it again, not "the existing ones too" and not "Inbox tidying". No switch. Log with numbers only (`mail: rule skip | user_moved | N`, `mail: tidy skip | user_moved | N`), Live: "N mails put back today, not sorted again".

**Learn sorting from corrections:** its own admin switch under Features → Reading e-mail → Order mail → "Learn sorting from corrections" (`chat.mail_learn`, off by default, no profile switch, never for guests). When the person puts a mail that a mail rule or "Inbox tidying" sorted (`mailregeln.spark_moved`) somewhere else, that is a **correction** (`mailregeln.correct`): on the E-mail page at once (`mailregeln.page_moved`), back in the inbox at the next run of the rules or the tidying, below the KI folder in the night look for rule suggestions (only when a folder changed). Dragging it into the fixed folder **"Falsch sortiert"** below the KI folder (`tidy.WRONG`, made once the switch is on; no folder of the tidying may have that name) means "was wrong" without a goal. Archiving is no correction. Consequences by fixed thresholds (`FIX_EXCEPT` = 1, `FIX_PAUSE` = 3, `FIX_DAYS` = 30, `FIX_WEIGHT` = 3), never the model: no sorter touches that mail again; one correction makes the sender address an **exception** of the rule (`except`, at most 50, `fits` skips it, for a domain rule only that address); 3 corrections of one rule in 30 days **pause** it (`on` off, `paused`, under E-mail → Rules "paused, often corrected") with a card "Rule paused" ("Switch on again", "Show the rule …"). "Inbox tidying" asks about a corrected sender instead of moving, until a newer rule of the person decides. A correction with a goal counts three times in the learning, so the card "Rule suggestion" comes at once: "Was in A, you put it into B" with "Change the rule" (the rule's folder becomes B, the exception goes), "Exception only" and "Don't ask again"; without a rule (tidying) "Make the rule". For a mail in "Falsch sortiert" the card asks **"Where does this mail belong?"** with the folders this sender usually goes to (at most 3), "Inbox", "Other folder …" (opens the mailbox's folders right at the card, "Move" puts the mail there; the iPhone opens E-mail → Inbox), "Just this mail" (the exception goes again), "Sender as exception" (for the tidying: stays in the inbox) and "Don't ask again". A folder button moves exactly this mail (Message-ID) out of "Falsch sortiert". Under Open the opened card shows the mail: from, subject, date, number of attachments and up to 2000 characters of text, as text only, read only (EXAMINE, PEEK, it stays unread in "Falsch sortiert"), through `GET /api/profile/vorgaenge/mail` (sign-in, own open card only, rate limit). The card's line names the folders first, then the sender. "Show in the conversation" is only on cards from a conversation and opens exactly that one. The buttons go through `vorgaenge.act` (sign-in, own card only, CSRF, size limit, rate limit, Notaus); the iPhone shows more than three buttons under "More". Kept in `mail-regeln.json` (corrections 30 days, at most 500: Message-ID, sender, folders). Logs with fixed words and numbers only (`mail: rule correction | folder|inbox|wrong|placed | N`, `mail: rule exception | N`, `mail: rule paused | N`, `mail: rule wrong folder | made`); Live: "Sortieren aus Korrekturen lernen an: heute N Korrekturen, M Ausnahmen, K Regeln pausiert".

**See the corrections:** only with "Learn sorting from corrections", E-mail → Rules shows the section **"Corrections (30 days)"** (`mailregeln.fix_list`, newest first, at most `FIX_SHOW` = 100): per correction the sender, from → to ("Falsch sortiert", inbox or folder), which rule or "Inbox tidying", the day and the state by fixed rules (`mailregeln.fix_state`: waiting, exception set, rule paused, not moved again, taken back). **"Undo"** (`POST /api/profile/mail/rules/fixes/{id}/undo`, `mailregeln.undo_fix`) takes back the exception and pause from this correction and counts it no more; the mail is not moved and stays untouched; the open card "Where does this mail belong?" or "Rule paused" closes. **"Change the rule"** opens the rule. Every rule shows "N taken (month) · M corrected (30 days)" with a bar (counters `month` and `fixed`), its exceptions to remove one by one (`POST /api/profile/mail/rules/{rid}/except`, `mailregeln.drop_except`) and, when paused, **"On again"** (the rule saved with `on`; only new corrections count for the next pause). Both endpoints: login, profile binding, CSRF, size limit, rate limit `postordnen`, the switches Order mail and "Learn sorting from corrections", never guests; journal lines `mail: rule correction undone | N |`, `mail: rule exception removed | 1 |` and one line per refusal, never sender or folder. Live counts "taken back". iPhone: the same list under E-mail → Rules with "Undo", "Change the rule", counters, exceptions and "On again" (swipe); Android through the panel view.

**The model only suggests:** no switch of its own, only with "Learn sorting from corrections". "Inbox tidying" notes in its log when the model filed a mail (`model`). When the person corrects such a filing, the correction carries the mark (`mailregeln.found`/`correct`, log `mail: tidy model fix | N`), and "Inbox tidying" asks about this sender for 30 days instead of moving by the model (`mailregeln.tidy_fixed`, already in the same run), until a rule decides: after **"Make the rule"** the correction is settled (`mailregeln.settle`), the sender's new mails are left to the mail rule (log `mail: tidy skip | rule decides | N`); if the rule is paused or deleted, it asks again. If the correction has a goal, the card "Rule suggestion" asks (kind `regel`, kind `fix` with `model`): **"Mails from <sender> always to "<folder>"?"** with **"Make the rule"** (a fixed mail rule sender → folder, only by this button), **"Just this mail"** and **"Never"** (never list), log `mail: rule card model | asked | N`. If such a mail lies in "Falsch sortiert", the model may **suggest an existing folder** in the background (`mailregeln.model_hint`, `tidy.suggest_folder` through `tidy._ask`, with maintenance pause and priority): it gets only the sender's domain and folder names (existing categories of "Inbox tidying" and the sender's usual folders, at most `tidy.FOLDER_MAX` = 30), never the address, subject or text. Its answer counts only if it is exactly one of these names (`tidy.parse_folder`), else it is dropped (`mail: rule card model | refused`). A valid suggestion becomes the first button of the card "Where does this mail belong?" marked "(the model's suggestion)"; only the person's tap files the mail, only then the card asks for a rule. The model moves nothing and never makes a rule. Under E-mail → Rules such corrections show "Inbox tidying (model)". Live: "N of them filings of the model, M folders suggested by the model". Self-tests `test_mail_lernen.ModelOnlySuggests`, `test_tidy`.

**Mail like Apple Mail, stage 1:** the Mail page follows the layout of Apple Mail, without a switch of its own (part of "Read mail").

**Calendar like Apple Calendar, stage 2:** no switch of its own (part of "Calendar"). A sidebar on the left: a small month (a tap jumps to the day), every calendar with its colour to show and hide (the browser remembers), shared ones and subscriptions marked; on a phone ☰ opens it as a sheet. View **Year** (twelve months, a tap opens the day). **Creating like Apple:** in week and day drag in free time (quarter hours) or double-click, double-click the all-day row for all day, long-press on a phone; the appointment shows at once as a draft and the **inspector** opens with the title to type. A tap on an own appointment opens the same inspector: title, place, all day, start and end, repeat (Never, Daily, Weekly, Every 2 weeks, Monthly, Yearly, with "Ends on"), calendar (only when creating), alert (5 min to 2 days before), people, note and link. Closing (Esc, a click beside it, "Done") saves; then "Undo" stays at the bottom for two minutes. Dragging the lower edge changes the length. On a phone the day shows a week strip on top; a swipe changes the day. **Repeating appointments** can be changed, moved and deleted: the panel asks "Only this appointment" or "All future ones" (with a changed rule only "All future ones"). "Only this" writes an exception (`RECURRENCE-ID`) or removes the day (`EXDATE`); "All future ones" changes the series itself from the first appointment, from a later one the old series ends before it (`UNTIL`, a `COUNT` is converted) and a new one begins. **People are only a list in the appointment** (`ATTENDEE` with `SCHEDULE-AGENT=CLIENT`): neither the calendar server nor the Spark sends an invitation through it. Technology: `kalserie.py` (pure iCal functions: reading and checking fields, changing a series), `calendars.page_series`, `change_series`, `keep_undo`, `page_undo`. Every change goes into the Spark's copy first and to the server behind, in order per profile, so "Undo" never arrives before the save. Endpoints: `PUT /api/profile/calendar/event` takes `uid`, `rid` (original start) and `scope` `one`|`future` for an appointment of a series; repeat, alert, people, note and link only when the form names them (otherwise they stay). `POST /api/profile/calendar/undo` `{"undo"}`: the token from the answer of every save, bound to the profile, valid 120 seconds, at most five, usable once (then 410); login, CSRF, size limit, rate limit "kalenderaendern". Logs: `calendar: series changed|deleted, this one|from this on`, `calendar: series not changed (…)`, `calendar: page undo done|gone|failed`, each with `… refused, notaus`. Live: "heute N auf der Seite geändert, N davon Serien, N zurückgenommen".
- **Mailboxes on the left:** with "Order mail" the column "Mailboxes" stands on the left: all inboxes, the smart mailboxes Unread, Flagged and With attachment, below them the folders per account. It stays open until you hide it (sidebar button or key O, the browser remembers it); dragging a mail opens it. Rows sit close like the side menu (30 px; in the panel on a phone 44 px). On top one quiet bar: search, "Select", reload, "New mail" (key N). Ticks only after "Select" or with ⌘/Ctrl-click.
- **List:** the sender with the time on the right (today the time, yesterday "Yesterday", this week the weekday, else the date), the subject below, 📎 and ★ as small marks.
- **Reading:** a head like Apple Mail (circle with initials, name, address, to, time on the right, subject big), attachments as tiles. HTML mails are shown formatted (paragraphs, bold, lists, tables, quotes): `mailansicht.py` cleans them on the Spark with a fixed list of allowed tags and attributes (no script, no style from outside, no `position`, no `on…` attributes, no SVG, no forms, no frames); the page shows them in a `sandbox` frame without script with its own CSP (`default-src 'none'; img-src 'self' data:`). Pictures from outside stay off so the sender does not learn that you read it; "Load pictures" fetches them for this mail through the Spark (`netguard`, only public addresses named in exactly this mail, only PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP by their first bytes, never SVG). Embedded pictures (`cid:`) come from the attachment. A link opens only after "Open the link? It leads to …" with its real address; when the link text differs from the target, the page warns (a phishing trick). `mailto:` opens a new mail. Mails without HTML stay text.
- **Phone:** the bar with reply, forward, archive, folder and delete stands fixed at the bottom above the tab bar.
- **Writing:** reply, forward and "New mail" open a window of their own (on a computer bottom right, movable by its head and to be made small, the mail stays readable; on a phone a sheet) with lines To, Copy, Subject, the text and the Spark's suggestions as ✨ chips. "Send" (or ⌘/Ctrl+Enter) has the Spark check the mail (like the preview sheet, `send/preview`), then it waits 10 seconds with "Undo" and "Send now"; "Undo" brings the window back with everything typed, nothing went out. When the mail holds text of the Spark (a ✨ suggestion, ✨ shorter/friendlier/more formal/in English, a draft from "For you"), the preview sheet comes before sending and only it sends. Mails by voice have their approval card with "yes".
- **iPhone app:** the same mail formatted in a web view without script that loads nothing from the network (a content rule blocks every http/https load, its own CSP, embedded pictures as `data:`), a head like Apple Mail, links only after the question with the address (then Safari), pictures from outside stay off. "Send" with 10 seconds "Undo", text of the Spark with the preview.
- **Live** (node Mail): "today N shown formatted, M pictures from outside loaded", numbers only. Logs: `mail: view html|text | N pictures outside`, `mail: picture loaded|shown|refused …`, never an address, subject or text.
- Not part of this stage: preview lines in the list, drafts in the folder "Drafts" and address suggestions from contacts.

**Contacts like Apple Contacts, stage 3a:** Contacts is a page of its own in the menu (`kontakte.js`), shown with "Contacts". On the left a search, the address books as groups (chips with counts), A to Z with a letter bar; on the right the card with initials, Message (`sms:`), Call (`tel:`), Mail (opens "New mail" on the page E-mail, without "Send mail" the mail program), Event (Quick add on the page Calendar) and Share (the card as `.vcf`), below phone, e-mail, address, website, birthday, note and "With Anna": the next appointment within 60 days (by mail address or name) and the latest mail from the mail supply, without asking a mailbox. Tapping the sender of a mail opens their card. The address books are under Contacts → Settings. Changing: admin switch Features → Contacts → "Edit contacts" (`chat.contacts_edit`, off). Then "Edit" like Apple (a minus per field, "+ add" per kind, "Done"), "New contact" (into the chosen address book or "On the Spark") and "Delete contact" after asking. `kontaktkarte.py` checks every field (numbers only digits and signs, mail form, websites only http/https, birthday as a date, maximum counts) and replaces only the page's lines in the vCard; photo, UID and everything else stay. `contacts.save` fetches the card fresh, compares the ETag, writes with `If-Match` (new ones with `If-None-Match: *`); if it was changed meanwhile, the Spark answers 409 and overwrites nothing. Cards "On the Spark" are kept sealed in the profile. Endpoints `/api/profile/contacts/page|with|vcf|card` with login, profile, CSRF, 64 KB, rate limit `kontakte`; Notaus blocks changing (423). The assistant and the voice never change contacts. Live: address books, cards on the Spark, changed on the page today (numbers only).


**Contacts like Apple Contacts, stage 3b: the Spark as a CardDAV server (`kontaktserver.py`):** admin switch Features → Contacts → "Contacts for contacts apps" (`chat.contacts_server`, off; needs "Contacts" and "Calendar server for calendar apps"). Then the address book "On the Spark" shows in the iPhone's Contacts app (on the iPhone: Settings → Apps → Contacts → Accounts → Other → CardDAV account: server = the Spark's address with `/carddav/`, user = profile id, password = the app password from Calendar → Settings → "For calendar apps"). The door is the calendar server's (Basic login, wrong guesses lock like a PIN, rate limit `caldav`, other sites' web pages refused, Mein Notaus). Only the own address book "On the Spark", never iCloud address books and never another profile. PROPFIND, REPORT (addressbook-query, addressbook-multiget), GET, PUT, DELETE with etags; per card exactly one vCard with a name or company, at most 256 KB, at most 2000 cards. Writing only with "Edit contacts", otherwise read only (403). A change on the page keeps the app's object name. Live: requests, changes, refusals today.

**Invitations from mail (`einladung.py`):** admin switch Features → Reading e-mail → "Invitations from mail" (`chat.mail_invites`, off; needs Reading e-mail and Calendar). If a mail carries an invitation (a `text/calendar` part or an `.ics` file; `mail.show` only reports `invite`), the E-mail page shows a bar under the subject (`einladung.js`): title, time, place, organiser, number of people, the own answer so far and whether the appointment is in a calendar already. "Accept" and "Maybe" put it in (into the chosen calendar, else the first writable one, CalDAV or Spark calendar), "Decline" takes it out; with `METHOD:PUBLISH` only "Add to calendar", a cancellation by the organiser (`CANCEL`) only "Remove from calendar" after asking. The invitation is someone else's text: at most 256 KB, one appointment (VEVENTs of one UID, at most 50), only REQUEST, PUBLISH and CANCEL. Into the calendar goes an appointment newly built by the Spark with only the fields needed (time, repetition, title, place, description up to 4000 characters, organiser, at most 100 people, time zones); the sender's alerts, attachments and links stay out. The place follows from the UID: the same invitation or its update replaces the appointment instead of adding it twice. With "Send mail" the answer (iMIP `METHOD:REPLY` with the own `PARTSTAT`) goes from the invitation's mailbox to the organiser (`senden.send_now`: day limit, Notaus, journal); it can be left out with the tick "Answer to … send". Endpoints `GET|POST /api/profile/mail/invite` with login, profile, CSRF, 4 KB, rate limit `einladung`; Notaus 423. The assistant does not answer invitations. Live (Calendar): shown, entered, answered today (numbers only). iPhone: the same section above the mail (`Einladung.swift`).

**Cards in the assistant:** when the assistant reads appointments (`calendar_events`), "Show in calendar" under the answer opens the Calendar page on that day; when it reads a mail (`mail_read`), "Open mail" opens exactly that mail on the E-mail page (event `goto`, at most three buttons, only a valid day or mail id, only with the page, never for another person's voice). The card is only a way there: the server decides what the page shows; adding, moving and deleting by voice have their approval card with yes/no.

**Send mail (Features → Reading e-mail → "Mails senden", `chat.mail_send`, off):** on the E-mail page "New mail", or "Reply" under a mail; "Preview" sends the form to `POST /api/profile/mail/send/preview`, the server checks every field (no line break in a field, so no header from the form; real addresses only; at most 10 recipients; subject at most 200, text at most 20,000 characters; no attachment, no HTML) and keeps the finished mail for 10 minutes under a random token bound to the profile. The sheet shows exactly that mail; only "Send" there (`POST /api/profile/mail/send` with the token) sends it, once. An answer takes subject ("Re: …"), In-Reply-To and References from the mail in the own inbox. `senden.py` sends through the mailbox's outgoing server (`SMTP_FOR`: iCloud smtp.mail.me.com 587, Gmail 465, GMX 465, web.de 587; with another IMAP server only the outgoing server typed in when connecting, never guessed, without one the mailbox only reads; the preview shows the server under "Via") with the same login, TLS only (465) or required STARTTLS (587), through `netguard.SMTP`/`SMTP_SSL` (checked address, USER rules). Except for iCloud and Gmail it puts a read copy into "Sent". At most 30 mails a day per profile, the Notaus holds sending off, a wrong password keeps the sheet for another try; if the server breaks off midway the sheet counts as used (never twice). Rate limit `mailsenden`; Live shows only how many mails were sent today; log `mail: send preview|sent|send refused|send failed|sent copy …` with numbers, never address, subject or text. **Sending by voice** (`chat.mail_send_voice`: off, answers only, all; default off): no tool for the model, fixed rules of the panel on the person's own words (`senden.answer`): "Antworte ihr per Mail: …" up to 15 minutes after the assistant read a mail (to its sender, `From` only, never `Reply-To`; the name only from the own contacts, never from the mail), with "all" also "Schreib eine Mail an Anna: …" (Anna only from the own contacts with exactly one address, or an address said in full; several fit → asked back). The Spark reads recipient and text back, approval card (kind "senden"), it is sent only after the plain "Ja" in the next message on the same device, through the same checks as the sheet. Never for guests, another voice or Telegram without the private release. Log `mail: voice proposed|sent|declined|refused, <reason>`.

**Send later (part of "Mails senden", no switch of its own):** the preview sheet and the "New mail" window offer "Later …" (iPhone: "Send later …" with date and time). `POST /api/profile/mail/send` with the token and `at` plans exactly the sheet's finished mail, at least 1 minute and at most 30 days ahead, at most 20 at once; until then it lies sealed in the profile (`mail-spaeter.bin`, `vorrat.seal`, bound to profile and file name). Above the list stand the planned mails (when, to whom, subject, never the text) with "Withdraw" (`DELETE /api/profile/mail/later/{id}`, also when "Mails senden" is off meanwhile). Each minute `senden.later_due_once` sends what is due, each mail exactly once, through the same checks as "Send" (30 a day, TLS, copy in "Sent"): the Notaus holds it back until it is over; with "Mails senden" off it shows "not sent"; if the outgoing server breaks off it shows "unclear, check Sent" and is never retried. With the sending the text goes, the line stays a day. Live shows only the number of planned mails; log `mail: send later planned|refused|dropped|unclear …` without address, subject or text.

**Confirmation codes (Features → Reading e-mail → New mail at once → "Bestätigungscodes", `chat.mail_code`, off):** when a mail with a code arrives ("482913 ist dein Bestätigungscode"), `mailcode.py` sends a note "Bestätigungscode · 482913 · GitHub" to the device in use, with "Copy code" (iPhone: the button or a tap puts it on the clipboard for 3 minutes, local only; Begleiter on Mac and Windows likewise). Fixed rules, never the model: unread, at most 10 minutes old, a word like code, Bestätigungscode, TAN, verification code close (80 characters) to exactly one code of 4 to 8 digits or capitals with digits; voucher, discount, order, tracking and customer numbers do not count, a year never. At most 10 notes an hour per profile, one per mail. Needs "New mail at once"; a profile with this function that used any device in the last two hours gets its IDLE connection for it. **Never through Telegram:** if Telegram is the device in use, it goes to the next other way (iPhone app, Android, Begleiter, browser); with none there is no note. Apple carries only "New message from the Spark", the app fetches the text from the Spark; the code lives only in memory with its note, never on disk, never in the log (`mail: code looked|no device|limit|too slow …` with numbers only); Live shows only the number of notes today. My emergency stop holds them.

**Mail statistics (part of "Mails ordnen", no switch of its own):** at the top of the tab Rules (panel and iPhone) "Who writes most" opens: the mails a week of the last four weeks and the 15 most frequent senders of the last 30 days with count and unread ones, plus "has rule" or "Rule …" (the form with that sender). `mailregeln.stats` counts in the panel from the listing of the Mail-Vorrat (300 headers a mailbox) and reads nothing again while it is fresh; nothing goes to the model. `GET /api/profile/mail/stats` (rate limit `postordnen`), log `mail: stats shown|too slow …` with numbers only, Live: how often looked at today.
