When an answer without a waiting proposal ends with a question, answers stand below as buttons (`ablauf.asks`, fixed rules on the last sentence, never made by the model): "Ja"/"Nein" for a fixed sentence start ("Soll ich", "Möchtest du", "Shall I" …), for "A or B?" up to 3 short parts of the question (each at most 30 characters and 4 words, no links) and "Something else …" (only places the cursor). After outside text never parts of the question on a button. A tap sends the word like a typed answer; only then is anything done, with all rules. "yes"/"no" (`ablauf.short_reply`) keeps the group of the question before (`intent.follow_up(…, answered=True)`, `ablauf.was_asked`: same device and conversation, ten minutes, once). Panel and Android (`choiceCard`), iPhone (`AnswersCard`, `choice`), Telegram buttons, Pebble; the speaker stays with speech. Only `rueckfrage-pending.json` is kept (ten minutes). Log `chat: rueckfrage-karte gezeigt | n`, `weiche: Antwort auf Rückfrage - group`. Tests `test_ablauf.Questions`, `test_ablauf.Chat`, `test_pebble`, `test_klaeren`.

**You can also say (basic set):** functions that are on but never used are shown with an example sentence; a tap only puts it into the input field, sent it goes through the Weiche and every rule. Fixed rules (`saetze.py`) choose, never the model; sentences are in German and English in `werkzeug_abdeckung.SENTENCES` (checked by `test_werkzeug_abdeckung`). Only what `features.allowed` permits, is set up and works on the device. Places: up to three follow-up sentences under the answer (only tools never used); "Try this" in an empty chat; "Did you mean …?" (only a suggestion); "What can you do?"; Me → What I can do (all sentences, search, "new", "never used", in the apps too); once "New for you: …"; while talking at most one sentence a day (switch "Tips while talking", each profile can switch it off under Me → Conversation); Telegram buttons, TUI `/tipps`; "sentence of the week" under On its own; for the admin Features → Routing "Nobody finds it" (unused for 30 days). At most one row per answer, one sentence per tool every 14 days, six a day; never after text from mail or web, in the emergency stop or for guests. Stored only tool use and shown sentences (`tipps.json`, never questions); Me → What I can do → "Delete my count". Switches: Features → below "Suggesting functions" "You can also say" and below it "Tips while talking". Log `tipps:`; Status → Live "Funktionen vorschlagen". Tests: `app/tests/test_saetze.py`.

**Cheat sheet to print:** Me → What I can do → "Deutsch" or "English" opens two A4 sheets: per area sentences to say with the trigger word marked (from `intent.classify`, including own words under Features → Routing), key words, the function's name, short commands, "Easily mixed up" and help when an answer goes wrong; only what is released and set up. Print, "Save as PDF" or "Download as file". Built from the code on every open (`spickzettel.py`, `werkzeug_abdeckung.SENTENCES`, `saetze.AREAS`, `features.py`, `app/VERSION`); `test_spickzettel` checks every card and sentence. Part of "You can also say"; never for guests; no scripts, strict CSP. Log `tipps: Spickzettel de|en <n> Werkzeuge`; Live "Spickzettel".

**Basic set for newcomers:** a **new installation** switches on a fixed list (`features.GRUNDAUSSTATTUNG`, each entry with its reason): targeted tool choice, fetch clear questions directly, instant acknowledgement, fetch ahead on a question back, ask instead of guessing with remember the choice, follow-up without wake word, undo by voice, learning from corrections, face shows what it does, help with the Spark with "Ask Spark on every page", suggesting functions, you can also say, lists for shopping and tasks, instant history sync. Only what needs no setup, nothing new to the outside, no extra model and no admin code; guests only ask and get answers. An **existing installation** never changes by itself: at the top of Features the card "Basic set for a good conversation" with "x of n on", "Take them all" and "What is off?" (filter "Recommended"); undo under Status → Logs → Admin-Protokoll. Once it asks **"May the Spark use the internet?"**: yes switches on the weather, Wikipedia straight away, the service watch and the web search (only with a SearXNG address); no keeps `chat.grund_internet`. Also in the iPhone app under Manage Spark → Funktionen. Endpoint `GET`/`POST /api/admin/grundausstattung` (admin only, never the Verwalter, rate limited, 256 bytes), every take-over in the admin log. Tests: `app/tests/test_grundausstattung.py`.

**Tool coverage:** every function stands in `werkzeug_abdeckung.py` with tools, a fixed rule or the reason there is nothing to ask for; a self-test keeps it complete. Tools named by the person ("… onto the reMarkable", an own MCP service) stay whatever the group. The switch knows e.g. doors, windows, household appliances, power, battery, camera, "Call Mum", "Navigate home", "tick off milk", "Postfach", "Bestellung". "Turn on web search" goes to the help, never to the web search.

**More to read:** "Which documents do I have?" (`document_list`) names the document cards by kind or tags. "What is on my reMarkable?" (`remarkable_library`, `rmliste.py`) reads list and tasks of the last comparison, never the cloud itself. "What does the front door camera see?" (`camera_describe`) describes the still picture of a Home Assistant camera (switches Features → Describe a camera picture and "Camera picture" under Me → By itself, both off; at most 30 pictures per hour; never kept). "Which smart home rules do I have?" (`home_rules_list`). All four results are outside text: nothing is switched or changed after them, nothing over MCP.

**Changing by voice (`aendern.py`):** what was created can also be changed. Everything except switching waits for the yes in the next message, goes through the emergency stop and is locked after outside text; none of it goes over MCP.
- **Appointments** (`calendar_change`): "Move the dentist to Friday 15:30", "Delete tomorrow's appointment with Anna". It searches writable CalDAV calendars by title and day; repeating appointments are never changed (only in the calendar app), and when several fit the Spark asks. The server's etag guards against changes made meanwhile. To move one the model never uses `calendar_add`.
- **Scheduled jobs** (`agent_change`): "Pause the weekly overview", "Resume the weather plan", "Delete the scheduled job …".
- **Smart home rules** (`home_rule_delete`): "Delete the rule for the front door".
- **Notes by itself** (`proactive_pause`): "Don't speak up any more today" (until midnight or some hours, at most 24), "You may speak up again"; at once, like "not now".
- **Volume of own speakers** (`speaker_volume`): "Make the kitchen speaker quieter", "Volume to 40"; own devices only, at once like switching.
- **Repeating reminders** (`reminder_set` with `repeat`): "Remind me every Monday at 7 about the bins": daily, weekdays, weekly or monthly (at most the 28th), always at the same clock time, also across the clock change.
- **A message into an own group** (`message_send`): "Write into the family group that I'll be late"; only the profile's groups, never private chats.
- **Undo** (`undo_last`, Features → "Undo by voice", off; once on for every profile): the Spark keeps its last five deeds for 30 minutes in memory only (reminder, list entry, note, appointment, device on/off, volume, pause, job, rule) and proposes to take back the last one; only the yes does it. For devices behind the code word, say "yes" with the code word.

**Status by voice (`zustand_wz.py`, tool `spark_zustand`):** "Is everything running?", "When was the last backup?", "Is the emergency stop on?": every profile reads its part of Me → My status and its own emergency stop, an admin also the service watch, backup, version and last update check (never GitHub itself). Fixed words and numbers only, no error texts or addresses; nothing is switched. Only for switch group "zustand". Switch: Features → Status by voice (`chat.status_voice`), off; never guests, never MCP.

**Exact calculations (`rechnen.py`, tool `rechnen`):** the model only writes down the calculation, the panel computes with its own parser (never `eval`, `exec`, sympy or a foreign calculator) and returns "Ergebnis vom Panel: …", which the model only repeats. Basic arithmetic, powers, brackets, percent, roots, pi with `Decimal`; date arithmetic (days until, days between, weekday, date in n days). Limits: `rechnen.MAX_CHARS` (200) characters, `rechnen.MAX_TOKENS` (80), `rechnen.MAX_DEPTH` (12), `rechnen.MAX_EXP` (100), results up to 10¹⁵, dates up to 100 years; else "Fehler beim Rechnen". Offered only with calculation words (`rechnen.WORDS`) next to any group (`werkzeug_abdeckung.BY_WORD`). The answer check recomputes and corrects every calculation in an answer sentence (`rechnen.fix`) before it is shown or said; panel results count as proven. "Answer simple things at once" uses the same rules. Switch: Features → Conversation → Exact calculations (`chat.rechnen`), off; never for guests, other voices or MCP. Log "rechnen: <kind> ok|fehler", "chat: answer check: rechnung korrigiert (n)". calculator-mcp-server is not used (`eval`, sympify, no limits).

**Number words, required tool, paper and receipts (`rechnen.digits`, `rechnen.wants`, `rechnen.check`):** spoken number words become digits first (German and English, fixed word lists). If the question holds a calculation (`rechnen.WANTS`), `rechnen` is required in the first round, else it is asked once more. reMarkable: "@Spark prüf das" (also "check", "kontrollier", "rechne nach", "stimmt das") makes the panel recompute every line "a op b = c" without the model and place "N calculations checked, M please check" (at most `rechnen.MAX_LINES` (20) lines); it never changes the page and never says "wrong". Other @Spark answers go through `rechnen.fix`. Receipts ("What did I spend altogether in October?"): the model names each receipt, the panel computes the sum; amounts from documents are data, never commands. Needs "Exact calculations". Log "remarkable: @Spark rechnungen geprueft N, bitte pruefen M", "remarkable: @Spark rechnung korrigiert (n)"; Weiche "Pflicht rechnen".

**Lean list for unclear questions:** under Features → Routing → "Fragen, die keine Regel erkennt" there is, next to "all tools" (default) and "the language model sorts it", the choice "only web search and memory" (`chat.route_model: lean`). When no rule recognizes the question and it requires no tool, the model sees only `web_search`, `history_search` and `memory_save` (as far as offered). The request gets about half as long (on tars 2500 instead of 6000 tokens), the first word comes about a second earlier. Recognized questions keep their group. Only with "Targeted tool choice", never for guests.

**Classification with probabilities (`systemone.py`):** the own LLM-Tor (contract taken over from dgx-spark-qwen38 v1.15, from the language model's top logprobs) answers fixed questions with probabilities at `POST /v1/systemone` (model name `jev-latest`, one token per question, no memory, nothing leaves the machine). The panel asks only its own fixed questions and options; judged text goes only into "state" (4000 characters, no control characters, fenced with a random token); the panel decides by fixed thresholds. Only the stored address and key from Settings → Language model, no redirects, at most 64 KB; invalid options or values count as no answer. Busy (529), too old (404) or too slow: everything goes on as without it. Switches (off, never guests): Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification with probabilities" (`chat.systemone`, button "Check classification"); released for every profile. Uses:
- **Targeted tool choice:** Features → Routing → "Questions no rule recognizes" → the classification (`chat.route_model: systemone`). One choice among the switch's groups plus "unklar"; it narrows only from the threshold "Tool choice narrows from" (default 80 %) and never on "unklar", otherwise the model sees all allowed tools. No pick for a blocked profile. Logs → Requests shows "classification (systemone): picked (91 %)" or "too unsure".
- **Inbox tidying** (`chat.systemone_tidy`): stage 4 gets its category with a probability; in "automatic" mode the Spark moves only from 90 % (else it asks), reason "systemone, 93 % sicher". Without systemone, stage 4 asks the language model for JSON.
- **Recognize important mail** (`chat.systemone_mail` below speaking up by itself, profile E-mail → Settings → Important mail → `pro_mail_smart`): also senders off the list when a yes/no on sender and subject (never the mail text) gives at least 80 %; only new mail, at most 5 questions per check. Only what stays in the inbox after mail rules and "Tidy the inbox" is reported (waiting at most 2 hours). Automatic senders, lists, social networks, bills, ads, login codes, mails moved by rules and senders on "Not important" are never important, without asking the model; the own list always counts. The card's "Not important" adds the sender (`pro_mail_not`, view and delete under E-mail → Settings and on the iPhone). Live: "Wichtige Mail: heute N gemeldet, M aussortiert"; logs `proactive: mail sorted | … new, … not important, … asked`, `proactive: mail cards tidied | n`, `proactive: mail sender not important | added|kept`.
- **Quality test** (`chat.systemone_quality`): a second opinion per question, "answers" 0 to 2 and "made up" in percent. It never counts for passing; a passed question with doubt (below 1 or 50 % made up and more) stays open to look at.
- **Classification in the settings assistant** (`chat.systemone_berater`, admin only): picking the catalog proposal for a case (`berater.pick`) as a choice with a probability (options `o0`, `o1` … plus "none"); proposed only from 60 % (`systemone.BERATER_MIN`), otherwise the catalog order holds. Reason: "Einordnung, 74 % sicher". The person's words go only into "state". Language model busy or missing: the JSON question. `_cand` and the check stay the bolt.
- **Check answers in the background** (`chat.systemone_check`, needs the settings assistant): after an answer with tools the panel asks the quality test's two questions in the background (question, up to four tool results, answer). From 90 % "made up" (`systemone.CHECK_MIN`) a case "Made-up suspected" is made (`faelle.KINDS["erfunden"]`, proposals answer check, temperature, quality test). Runs through `vorrang` ("Antwort prüfen", waits while somebody speaks), at most 50 times a day, never before speaking; never for sentences with the code word.
- **Recognize complaints** (`chat.systemone_klage`, needs "Stop error loops"): the deeds of the last answer stay in memory for 10 minutes (`taten.note_last`). When the next message says "still does not work" (`taten.COMPLAINT`) or the classification finds a complaint from 80 % (`systemone.KLAGE_MIN`, 1.5 s), the model has to look first and answer honestly; tools of that kind (`taten.locked`) are locked unless the message itself asks for it (`taten.RETRY`). Logs → Requests "Beschwerde (Panel)"; Live "n Beschwerden erkannt".
- **Narrator bench** (under *Test benches*, `chat.dungeon_pruefstand`, admin only, on the button): per fixed scene the model's view, "vivid" and "follows the actions" in per cent (use `erzaehler`); only the fixed rules give points.
- **Label mass**: `x-systemone-label-mass` says how much probability fell on the options; below 50 % (`systemone.MIN_MASS`) the answer counts as unsure and the caller goes on without it (Live counts "unsure"). `x-systemone-branches` gives the number of questions asked; \"Check classification\" and Live show it.
- **Measure classification** (button under Features → Routing at "Questions no rule recognizes", `POST`/`GET /api/admin/systemone/messen`, admin only): up to 200 test sentences of the switch (`intent_bench.BENCH` without two-group sentences, plus own ones) run as background work ("Einordnung messen") through the tool choice. Per threshold 60/70/80/90 %: narrowings, errors, label mass, time, mix-ups. Suggestion: the lowest threshold with at least 10 narrowings and at most 3 % errors; "Take the suggestion" sets `chat.systemone_min` (0.6/0.7/0.8/0.9, default 0.8). Memory only.
Journal: `systemone:` with use and milliseconds, `weiche: systemone wetter 0.91`; never text. Status → Live: "⚖ n" at the language model, failures under events ("Einordnung"). Self-tests: `app/tests/test_systemone.py`.

**First sentence sooner:** a long first sentence goes to speech output at its last comma from 50 characters on, if that comma comes after at least 20 characters; the first sound comes earlier.

**Formatted answers:** the panel's chat (browser, phone view, Android app) shows Markdown formatted (headings, bold, italic, lists, quotes, code, tables, links), also while it comes in. Safe (`md.js`): model text never becomes HTML, elements only from a fixed list via `createElement`/`textContent`, pictures only linked, links only http(s) to public addresses (no localhost, no home network, no `name:password@` addresses), in a new tab without referrer, naming the host when the link text differs; over 60,000 characters plain text. The signs are never spoken (`textnorm.speech_markdown`, whatever "do not read out emojis, code and Markdown" says; `code` as a word in quotes, links only as text, code blocks not at all), and this runs before "never say passwords". Without formatting, text without signs arrives (`textnorm.plain_markdown`): Telegram, Siri, companion, reMarkable, watch, speaker display; the history keeps the original. The iPhone app shows bold, italic, code and links (http(s) only); copy and share without signs. Only the panel's chat gives the model the hint `chat.WEB_HINT` (spoken without tables and code blocks). No switch, nothing leaves the house. Test: `test_markdown`. The automatic daily briefing (Calendar → Settings, push "Your day") is kept sorted in the history (`chat.BRIEF_MD_SYSTEM`), the push without signs, the reMarkable morning sheet as prose. **Companion:** "Ask Spark" shows formatted (Windows: bold, lists, monospace; Mac: bold, italic, code, public http(s) links); `/api/desk/ask` sends next to `answer` (plain text) the field `md`, passwords hidden in both, `md` left out when the plain text hides more. Nothing is shown as HTML or run (`MdText.cs`, `MdAnswer` in `AskView.swift`). The morning start (`/api/desk/daily?kind=morning`) comes as `md` for the window and `text` for the notification; the evening stays a fixed list without the model. Test: `test_begleiter`.

### More precise and faster

Package A:

- **Measuring per kind of question:** every spoken answer keeps the time to the first sound, switch group, number of tools offered, language model rounds, share read from the cache, seconds to the first character and to the end, and the version (`latency.MEASURE`, fixed words and numbers only, two weeks in `state/latency.json`). *Status → Checks* shows, folded, "Per kind of question, last 7 days" (slowest first, median and 9 of 10) and "Per version" (the last four). The language model always reports how much it read (`stream_options.include_usage`).
- **Short answers without a screen:** speakers, phone, watch, Siri, the Android assistant and CarPlay get at most `chat.SPOKEN_MAX_TOKENS` (700) tokens; their hints ask for one to three sentences. Anything longer ends at its last full sentence.
- **Names for listening** (*Features → Names for listening*, `chat.hoer_namen`, off): the speech recognition gets profile names, contacts and the profile's Home Assistant rooms and devices as context (`hoerwoerter.py`, at most 100 names, 700 characters, before the context under *Settings → Speech recognition*); contacts and Home Assistant only when released. Shared speakers and the phone only profile names, guests nothing. Home Assistant at most every 30 minutes, memory only; log `hoeren:` numbers only.
- **Complete spoken text:** units after a number (km/h, km, kWh, kW, kg, g, l, GB, MB, min, h, W …) and German abbreviations ("z. B.", "ca.", "bzw.", "usw.", "Nr.", "Str." …) are spoken in full, "30 €/Monat" becomes "dreißig Euro pro Monat" (`textnorm.UNITS`, `speak_abbreviations`, in both speech services).

Package B:

- **A fixed tool list per group** (with *Fast answer start*, `chat.prompt_cache`): when the switch recognizes a group, the model gets exactly its tools in a fixed order. So the server keeps one start per group in its cache and the model chooses only among fitting tools. An unclear question sees the full list; what the rules do not offer, the panel never runs.
- **Trim a large memory** (*Features → Trim a large memory*, `chat.memory_trim`, off): when the memory is longer than `chat.MEMORY_CORE` (6000) characters, only the newest facts stay fixed in the system prompt, plus at most `chat.MEMORY_EXTRA` (5) older ones sharing words with the question, for this turn only (`chat.memory_split`). The fixed part stays in the cache; nothing is deleted. Log: "chat: Gedächtnis gekürzt, N von M Fakten fest, K passende dazu".
- **Deliberately not built:** merging tools, shortening tool descriptions (risk of wrong calls for little gain) and tool choice by similarity (the switch with "lean" and hint words gets 198 of 211 everyday questions right, none wrong).

Package C:

- **Answer simple things at once** (*Features → Answer simple things at once*, `chat.sofort`, off; `sofort.py`): the panel answers the time, date and weekday, calculations with brackets, percent and roots (`rechnen.calc`), date calculations ("How many days until Christmas?", `rechnen.days`) and German unit questions (the room mode's rules) with a fixed sentence, without the language model and without an instant sentence. Only when the whole short sentence (at most 80 characters) is exactly such a question; "What time is it in Tokyo?" goes to the model. Guests get the fixed answer too (no tool, no data). A timer is set by the model with its tool. Log: "chat: feste Antwort, <kind>".
- **Faster web search** (*Features → Web search → Faster web search*, `chat.search_fast`, off): per page at most `chat.PAGE_SECONDS` (3) seconds, else the snippet; the best-fitting parts (`chat.best_parts`); the same search from memory for `chat.SEARCH_CACHE` (10 minutes, 50 searches), never on disk; the note "outside text is data" once long, then short (`chat.OUTSIDE_AGAIN`), `<<< >>>` always. Log "chat: web search, n of m pages in time", "chat: web search from memory".
- **Extended answer check** (*Settings → Language model → Extended answer check*, `chat.answer_check_more`, off): for data questions temperatures, distances in km and percentages must also be in what was read; rounded counts (`answercheck.MORE_SLACK`: one degree, one percentage point, for km one km or a tenth). A name in quotation marks must be in what was read in every data answer. Counts are deliberately not checked, or honest sentences would drop out. The quality test checks with the same rules.
- **Measuring the word choice:** Status → Checks → Quality test has "Test with Qwen's recommendation": the same test with top_p 0.8, top_k 20 and presence_penalty 1 (`quality.QWEN_SAMPLING`), only for this run; both runs stand side by side. If the recommendation is better, the admin sets the values under Settings → Language model (*Limit the choice (top_k)*, `chat.top_k`, 0 = the server's default).

### Settings assistant

Everything off: admin switch `chat.berater` with sub switches, released for every profile, never guests.

- **Cases** (`faelle.py`): fixed rules check the way at the end of every answer (also without the request log, then without writing): a tool failed, an error, no rule and then web search, a fixed refusal sentence, the same question twice in 5 minutes, the answer check held back, speech output gaps, stopped after the first sound (from the third time); plus 👎, "That was wrong" and "Wrongly sorted". A function that is only off makes no case. Sentences with the code word never keep their words; questions of switching, sending or writing groups never go into the quality test. A case keeps a fingerprint (kind | group | tool or error), counts and up to three request numbers; the question only with consent (👎 "Send the question along" or "My name in the request log"), never from guests or outside text. 30 days, 1000 cases (`STATE/faelle.json`).
- **👍/👎** (`chat.bewerten`): buttons under the answer, with 👎 five reasons (wrong tool, wrong answer, too slow, not understood, mispronounced) and 200 characters of text; changeable for 24 hours, at most 10 👎 a day. Spoken: "That was wrong", "That was good" (fixed sentence, no tools). 👍 is only counted (`STATE/bewertungen.json`); with consent the question becomes a **confirmed sentence** (`STATE/gut-saetze.json`, 200) no proposal may sort differently.
- **Detectors** (every 30 minutes, each can be switched off under Status → Improve → Catalog): many unclear questions (over 30 % of at least 30 in 7 days), often slow (over 30 % of at least 10 in 24 hours), errors pile up (5 in 24 hours), quality test newly red, a service was gone, memory below the reserve, few 👍 in a group (under 75 % of at least 20 in 7 days).
- **Catalog** (`berater_katalog.py`): stage A Weiche (own word, "not with", test sentence), must-word, pronunciation, word for the speech recognition, question into the quality test; stage B fine values in narrow ranges, one step (web search, lookups, wait for own sources, history, temperatures, first sound); stage C switches with a second step (a code on the page, 2 minutes); P a profile's own settings. Stage D never: passwords and keys, the language model's address, Notaus and Wächter, second sign-in step, roles, ports, guest access, sending mail, MCP to the outside, reporting problems, updates, backups.
- **Proposal**: fixed rules build the fitting candidates from the catalog. Only when the language model runs at home (loopback or private address) it picks one (JSON schema, only candidate numbers, one sentence of reason shown as data); at most `chat.berater_calls` (20) a day, through `vorrang` and only after a minute without speech. Otherwise the rule takes the first.
- **Test bench**: the same check as saving (`admin.validate`), every test sentence of the Weiche, the confirmed sentences, the questions of the last 7 days (how many land differently), ranges and step. Red cannot be taken; "Other" shows the remaining candidates.
- **Take** writes through the panel's own ways (the Weiche with its history, else `admin._save` with undo and service restart, the quality test through its list) and keeps the value before; **Undo** puts exactly that back. **Re-check** after `chat.berater_check_h` (24) hours: the case did not come back "helps", once "unchanged", twice or a test sentence red "worse".
- **Routes of a case**: local (catalog, test bench green), **report** (`chat.berater_issue`, needs Reporting problems): a draft in fixed words (kind, group, tool, numbers, never a question), sent only after its preview; **environment** (`chat.berater_env`): steps to copy for services outside (e.g. `speech-spark status`, `sudo speech-spark logs tts -n 100`), nothing is run; "Done" starts the re-check.
- **Ideas outside the catalog** (`chat.berater_ideas`, admins only, off): when only a report or the environment remains, the language model in the LAN writes an idea (at most 400 characters, also on "Get idea"), marked "Idea, not checked" and never applied. Named settings only without secrets, addresses and access (never stage D), shown as a name with a jump link; the admin changes it alone. Keys and tool names become names before they are shown (`klartext.readable`). Environment steps (only with `chat.berater_env`) are to copy; chains, redirection, deleting, downloading and rights are filtered out. From two "Good idea" on, "Report as catalog wish" is offered (needs `chat.berater_issue`). Counts against the model calls of the day.
- **Finding before every proposal** (`befund.py`): fixed rules work out the cause from tool names and yes/no marks of the request, never from words of an answer (e.g. wrong group, tool not called, missing, failed, service error, found nothing, answer contradicts, refusal, gaps, cause unclear). Proposals only fit the cause; temperature and "Check answers" only for "answer contradicts". A 👎 with an unclear cause gets, instead of a proposal, the question "What would have been right?" (expected tool, then "Re-assess").
- **Plain words** (`klartext.py`): every case and proposal shows a card in fixed sentences: *What happened*, *Where from* (with "View request" to Status → Logs → Requests), *Why*, *What helps* (with jump links); never words of a question. The error code sentences exist once (`klartext.CODES`). The same change for several cases is one proposal with "N cases". "Why this proposal" names cause, risk and how to undo. "★ best pick" is set by the panel by fixed rules (test bench green, replay not red, more cases, rule before fine value before switch), never by the model.
- **What needs you, whole way, check yourself:** the top says what waits for the admin (proposals, checks to do yourself, reports with their cases), the best pick one tap away, below it the 👍 share of the last 7 days as a light (green from 85 %, yellow from 75 %, else red; a colour only from 20 ratings). "Whole way" shows the request's way in fixed sentences (with Explain) and its log lines on the same page. "Check yourself" shows the service as the service watch sees it, with "Check now"; the steps are a checklist on the page only (nothing stored or run).
- **"Should have looked in …"** with 👎 (panel and iPhone app, optional): documents, documents the Spark made, calendar, mails, devices, reminders, web, earlier conversations. Only the choice is kept.
- **Tool map:** every tool belongs to a group of the Weiche or to the general ones; a self-test keeps it so.
- **Gaps** (Status → Improve → Gaps): cases with no fitting tool offered, counted per tool (cases, reports, people); the Live overview shows the number.
- **"Not helpful"** on a proposal: like Reject (30 days), but the case gets the next proposal; after three times for the same cause and change that change comes last.
- **Must rules for every tool** (`chat.tool_words`, Features → Routing → must words): a line `tool: word, word` works for every tool of the Weiche; when exactly one is a must, the first round has to call exactly that one.
- **Tool hints** (`chat.tool_hints`, Settings → Language model → Advanced): one line `tool: sentence` (4 to 200 characters, no angle or curly brackets, at most 20); the model gets the sentence only in answers that offer the tool, also in the quality test. The settings assistant only proposes fixed sentences.
- **Extra tool for a group** as a proposal: when the expected tool is missing because the Weiche left it out, the assistant proposes `chat.route_extra` (Features → Routing, with history and undo); it is only added when the answer may offer it anyway.
- **Quality test with an expected tool:** a question from a case can go into the quality test with the tool it has to call; passed only when exactly that one was called.
- **Replay:** every proposal for the Weiche, a must rule, a hint, an extra tool or a test sentence runs the case's last question through the fixed rules before and after (group, offered tools, must, called first) and shows "fits" or "does not fit" without naming the question; when the answer is already right without the change, it says so. With "Analyse case" the local model then calls the offered tools once, without running them.
- **Analyse case** (admin switch below the settings assistant, off): the language model in the LAN picks a rule from templates by JSON schema (kind, tool, group, template, one word of the question). The panel turns it into a catalog proposal by fixed rules, nothing else; the word has to be a whole word of the question. Test bench and replay run as always, only the admin can take it; every analysis counts against the calls of the day.
- **Rules** (Status → Improve → Rules): every own rule with its source and "Remove"; Live counts them. **Built in** lists the fixed answer rules "Check deeds" (a claimed deed without a successful tool is not said) and "Stop error loops", only switched off by their switches. "Never say no … without" exists only for tools that know the Spark's earlier doing.
- **Rule versions, export and import:** before every change to the own rules the Spark keeps the six rule settings (the last 30, `STATE/regel-versionen.json`, in the backup); "Earlier versions" restores one. "Export" saves the rules as JSON, "Import" checks them like a save and first shows which test sentences of the Weiche would come out differently.
- **Cleanup:** the Rules tab marks rules that are built in already, contradict "not with", are in the group already or were not used for 30 days. Only which rule applied is counted, never a word of the question. Removing stays with the admin.
- **Re-check after an update or a model change:** when the version or the language model's name changes, the Spark replays every taken rule and writes "Still works" or "No longer works" on the proposal.
- **Undo automatically** (admin switch, off): the re-check also counts the group's 👍 share (7 days before against after, from 10 ratings); a fall of 15 points means "worse". With the switch the Spark then undoes the admin's takes too, otherwise only the autopilot's, and tells the admin profiles.
- **Guard against manipulation:** the autopilot only takes cases from at least two profiles or an admin; one person alone cannot change anything by itself with many 👎, and the proposal shows that. Rule texts only come from fixed templates.- **English form** (with "Analyse case"): for a must word or a Weiche word the language model in the LAN fetches the English form (one word, schema with a pattern, checked by the panel); it is the first choice under "Other", with its own test bench.
- **Monthly report** (Status → Improve → Report): 👍 share per group against the 30 days before, rules taken, helpful, worse and undone, open proposals, gaps and own rules, numbers only. On the 1st of a month from 8 am it goes once to the admin profiles with "Note on a new proposal".
- **"Why?" for every profile** (admin switch, off; released for every profile, never guests): the Spark answers "Why did you just search the web?" in fixed plain sentences: how it understood the question and where it looked, without tool names, rules or settings. Admin profiles with "Ask for proposals in the chat" get the detailed version.

**Spark diary** (Features → "Spark diary", off; never guests): the Spark notes what it did for a profile (reminder, mail draft, document, reMarkable, message, device only with name and on/off, never the code-word sentence). 7 days, at most 200 entries, `users/<id>/tagebuch.json`. "What did you do for me today?" has to call `spark_tagebuch`. The model never gets titles of web pages and mails, only the kind. Journal "tagebuch: <kind> notiert".
- **Questions about the Spark's own doing** ("Was a document made for the reMarkable?", "Is the letter ready?"): a fixed rule (`chat.DONE_WORDS`) makes the first round call `document_status` (time, where it was sent, what went to the reMarkable in the last 7 days), with the reMarkable and without "Making documents" `remarkable_sent`; never the uploaded documents. Such a question never sends the answer to the reMarkable.
- **Explain** (`chat.berater_explain`): Status → Logs → Requests → "Explain" describes a request in fixed sentences and names what could help; "Take as a case". Likewise under Status → Logs → Diagnosis at the newest error (only the area, never the log line).
- **In the chat** (`chat.berater_chat`, admin profiles only): "What does the settings assistant propose?" names open proposals; "Why did you just search the web?" (Warum … eben/gerade …) explains the way of the last answer (switch, tools, first sound, errors; from the memory of the last 24 hours); "How do the answers get faster?" names the catalog's steps. A "yes" never takes anything; for everybody else these are normal questions. **Note** (`chat.berater_notify`, profile `berater_note`): a new proposal to admin profiles, at most 3 a day.
- **Feedback "fixed"** (`chat.bewerten_back`, profile `rate_back`): when a change after a 👎 helps, the person gets one note.
- **Proposals for a profile** (`chat.berater_me`): from the own 👎 of the last two weeks (e.g. twice "too slow": shorter answers, instant reply), at the top of Me → Conversation; only the profile takes them, "No thanks" hides one.
- **Autopilot** (`chat.berater_auto`): stages A and B with a green test bench and at least two reports, at most one change a day, at most three steps from the admin's value; it undoes on "worse". Never 22 to 7 o'clock, never during a Notaus; every change goes as a note to the admin profiles. Rejected proposals do not come back for 30 days. A report counts as done once the issue is closed on GitHub.
- **Display**: Status → Improve (inbox, proposals, re-check, done, catalog with detectors; above them the 👍 share per group of the last 7 days), on top of the admin pages and in the iPhone app under "Needs you" "N proposals to improve wait for you" (without changing the status colour), Live node "Improve" with numbers, events "Verbessern". Journal: `faelle:` (kind, group, tool, number) and `berater:`.

## The panel

On the first admin login a **setup wizard** walks through password, language model (with a connection test), voice (to listen to), profiles, access and features, and ends with the live check. Start it again: *Status → Checks → Run setup again*.

**Ease of use:** the search also finds description words and everyday words (alarm clock, light, backup, PIN, delete); without a hit it offers "Ask Spark". When the microphone fails, a fixed sentence names reason and way. The head under Status → Summary is red only when something really is not running; advice stands as "Open" below. Pills and marks have at least 4.5:1 contrast; on a phone small buttons and links have a larger hit area. New profiles get "Los geht's" at their first sign-in; the sign-in page says "Forgot the PIN? An admin sets a new one.". A new PIN goes into two hidden fields. "Still switch on yourself: Features → …" is a jump link. Notes only to know stand only under Status.

The menu is on the left on computers and at the bottom on phones (Assistant, Me, Status, Manage). **Search** (Ctrl K or ⌘ K; on phones a magnifier at the top of the assistant) finds pages, switches, settings, Me, checks, logs and guides and marks the hit. A way like "Settings → Spark → Operation" or "Me → Documents" in a hint or answer is a link there. Below are **Assistant** and **Me**, under *Manage Spark* the admin pages **Status**, **Features** (with *Routing*), **People and devices** (with *Apps and interfaces*) and **Settings**, each with sub-pages; a number at "Settings" counts unsaved pages. On a computer "Me" opens as a page right of the sidebar.

- **Assistent** (assistant): voice chat with the language model (the own one behind the LLM-Tor); the answer as text and streamed speech sentence by sentence. Hands-free mode listens again after each answer; the space bar or "Speak" interrupts; after 8 seconds of silence the microphone goes off ("Heard nothing, microphone off"), only "Hey Spark" listens all the time. **Follow-up without wake word** (admin: Features → Follow-up without wake word, off by default; profile: Me → Conversation, off/4/6/8/10 seconds): after a spoken question the microphone stays open that long; never after typed questions, never for guests, never in room mode, not on ESP32 speakers ("Jarvis" on the chip). When iOS ends the microphone (lock, background, call, Siri), the panel says so and gets a fresh one next time; muted time does not count as silence.
- **Silence never becomes words** (fixed rule, `stille.py`): at `/api/test/asr` (app, browser, guests) the panel measures in 30 ms pieces how long the recording is clearly above the quietest part of the room (three times the 20th percentile, about −46 to −30 dBFS). Under 0.1 s of voice it is silence; typical silence sentences ("Untertitel im Auftrag des ZDF", "Thanks for watching", "[Musik]") never count; a lone short word ("Ja", "Okay") only from 0.2 s of voice. A text made mostly of the name list of "Names for listening" (at least 3 names and 60 %, or without commas at least 8 words nearly all from the list) is never a question (`stille.context_echo`), also for speakers, phone, voice messages and the Dungeon table; a sentence with one or two device names goes through. Reasons `silence`, `silence phrase`, `too short`, `context echo`; browser "Heard nothing.", log `chat: ignored (silence)`, Live "Stille überhört", never text; no sound is kept. The iPhone app leaves out its chime plus 0.25 s and sends only from 0.15 s of voice.
- **TV and other voices never become a question** (fixed rule, `fremdstimme.py`): when the browser opens the microphone by itself, the page reports `opened` = `barge`, `follow` or `hands`; "Speak" and "Hey Spark" are never checked. (1) With a taught voiceprint and speaker recognition, the recording counts only for the profile's voice, clearly another household profile's, or when it is too short to compare; else no question, browser "Ignored a voice that is not yours, for example from the TV." (2) Without a voiceprint the page learns the room level while the Spark is quiet; barge-in only clearly above it (3.5, 2.5 or 2 times by sensitivity), follow-up and hands-free window only above twice that. Log `chat: ignored (foreign voice, barge-in) from web | match 0.48 of 0.75 needed, …` or `chat: open microphone voice checked … | known voice`, Live "Fremde Stimme überhört", never text or name. The iPhone app checks `hands` and `barge` the same way.
- **Barge-in: pause first** (admin: Features → Barge-in: pause first, off by default; `zwischenruf.py`): without it a quarter of a second of voice ends the answer. With it the browser, Android and iPhone app only pause (fade out in 15 ms, the model keeps writing) and send the recording to `/api/test/asr` (`opened=barge`, `hold=1`, maybe `asked=1`). The panel decides with fixed words, never the model (`barge`): **weiter** (nothing, own or foreign voice, silence, listening words like "mhm", "ja", "okay", "genau"): the answer plays on. **stopp** (only stop words like "Stopp", "Halt", "Sei still", "stop"): the answer ends. **frage** (anything else, more than four words always): a new question; after an answer ending on a question even "ja" is an answer. Without a decision after 4 s the answer plays on; a tapped "Stop" always ends it. Only signed-in profiles with "Barge in" (Me → Conversation), never guests; the admin can block. Log `hoeren: zwischenruf weiter | listening words | from web`, Live "Ins Wort fallen: Angehalten, läuft weiter", never words. iPhone: `/api/iphone/hello` with `barge_hold`; off in the car.
- **Turn end by tone** (admin: Features → Turn end by tone, off by default; `satzende.py`, `smartturn.py` on the model computer): the turn end is decided by the word list of natural turn-taking at each pause (250 ms): "fertig" at once, "offen" ("und", "weil", "ähm", a comma) up to 2 s, otherwise after 800 ms. Smart Turn v3.2 (pipecat-ai/smart-turn, BSD-2-Clause, 8 MB int8 ONNX) estimates a number 0 to 1 from the tone of the last 8 s. The switch **only measures**: the pause goes to `/api/test/asr` with `turn=1`, the last 8 s as a 16 kHz WAV to `POST /v1/turn` (port 31001), the answer waits at most 0.3 s; the next recording reports `turns=<number>:<fertig|offen|neutral>:<0|1>`. Log `hoeren: satzende p=0.82 regel=neutral weiter=nein ms=31`, `state/satzende.json` only four numbers, never sound, words or profile. Ends after 200 pauses or 7 days; the summary below the switch shows hits, false alarms (threshold 0.8) and possible savings (0.55 s each). Nothing is decided with it. `install.sh` fetches the model from the pinned PyPI package pipecat-ai 1.12.0, checks with SHA-256, keeps only the model (`/opt/speech-spark/smartturn/`, owned by root), the service checks before every load; no pickle, no foreign code, no transformers. Only signed-in profiles with natural turn-taking or live text (Me → Conversation) in the browser and Android app, never guests; the admin can block profiles.
- **Ignore my own voice** (admin: Features → Ignore my own voice, off by default, with "What it ignores" below; profile: Me → Conversation, on once allowed): the Spark remembers in memory for 60 seconds what it said on which device (at most 20 pieces, never on disk). A recording whose word pairs come at least 60 % from that counts as silence (browser "Ignored my own voice.", iPhone "Nichts verstanden.", speaker diagnosis "Eigene Stimme überhört"). With "also everything while another device is speaking" (recommended), a recording falling at least 70 % into another device's speaking time of the same profile (plus 1 second) does not count either; the speaking device is never held back. Sentences under three words and those starting with "Spark", "Jarvis" or "Computer" always count. Only signed-in profiles, never guests. Log `chat: ignored (own voice)` or `(speaking pause)`, never text. Home Assistant via Wyoming is not covered.
- **Design own voices** (since V01.1.374, Dominik 2026-11-11; admin: Features → Design own voices, off by default; needs Settings → Speech output → "Also provide VoiceDesign" and "Also provide own voices"; profile: Me → Conversation → Own voice → "Voices …"): a box with three tabs opens below the row. *Ready voices*: 20 voices with more variety (calm and warm, factual, narrator, storyteller, butler, Bavarian, North German …), filter Woman, Man, Neutral, "▶" plays the sample, a click chooses it. *Design your own*: gender, age, pitch, pace and up to two kinds from fixed lists, plus up to 120 characters of own words (only letters, comma and dot); "▶ Sample" speaks the fixed example sentence, "Roll again" makes a new variant, "Take it" keeps exactly the sample heard under a name and chooses it. *My voices*: up to three of your own, listen, choose, delete (tap twice). VoiceDesign makes each voice only once as a short sample (3 to 15 seconds); after that the third engine "Own voices" (Base, `speech-spark-tts-klon`) speaks it the same in every answer and as fast as a fixed voice. If the main voice is a Base model itself, it speaks the own voices and the third engine does not run (never a weaker model beside a better one); under Status → Models VoiceDesign then shows as "ready, loads on demand". VoiceDesign itself loads only when needed and is unloaded again after `tts.voicedesign_leerlauf_min` minutes without a request (default 5); while it loads the page says "VoiceDesign is loading …" and the sample comes by itself. The Spark makes the 20 ready voices after the release in quiet minutes, one after the other (priority "Stimmen gestalten"). 10 samples per day and profile (the admin lifts that under People and devices, at the profile under Rights, with "Ohne Tageslimit"), never for guests. If the engine for own voices is not running, the standard voice speaks. The choice counts on all devices.
- **Own voices: the lock holds on every way** (code review 2026-11-11, point 4): whether a profile may speak with a designed voice is decided by `stimmdesign.stimme_ok` alone (function released, one of the 20 fixed ones or its own). Speaker, Telegram and the MCP server take the profile's voice only through `profiles.voice_for` (before, unchecked from the settings), the chat takes a voice from the request only when the profile may speak with it, and the MCP tool `speak` refuses another profile's. A voice stored earlier that is no longer allowed (function off, another profile's) counts as the standard voice everywhere. `GET /v1/audio/voices` of the speech output names no designed voices any more (`tts_proxy.ohne_eigene`). When a deletion leaves the voice sample on the model computer because it does not answer (`stimmdesign: loeschen ablage fehler`), the panel takes it away the next time Me → Conversation → Voice is opened (`stimmdesign.reste_weg`: every sample `eigen-…` that belongs to no profile, at most 10 each time; `stimmdesign: rest geloescht ok`). A second "Übernehmen" while the first still stores is refused (409), so a fourth voice never appears. (🧪 test_stimmdesign)
- **Zustand** (status): on top one sentence on whether everything runs, below it "Braucht dich" (needs you: update ready, unsaved settings, a service off or failing) with a button to each place; the dot next to "Status" in the menu shows the same in green, yellow or red. Sub-pages *Summary*, *Checks* (live check, quality test, benchmark, try speech recognition and speech output) and *Logs*.
  Under the sentence chips with version, last backup and last check and a button *Zum offenen Punkt* (to the open point); *Braucht dich* right below. A wide tile *Antworten pro Stunde* (answers per hour) shows the last 24 hours as bars (counts only, from `latency.json`; tapping opens Status → Logs → Requests; without any answer it is not shown; the iPhone app shows it under "Heute"). *Jetzt* (now) shows four tiles *Zuhören* (listening), *Sprechen* (speaking: running, off, no answer, error; model and average time), *Sprachmodell* (language model, per the last check) and *Speicher* (memory: free, with the 8 GiB line below which DGX OS ends processes). *Heute* (today, last 24 h) has tiles for answers, median first sound (yellow when clearly slower than the week before), speakers online (yellow when not all are reachable) and background waiting; every tile jumps to its place. The other cards stand under *Bereiche* (areas). GPU, CPU, the service table with start/stop are under *Technische Details* (folded).
- **Einstellungen** (settings): sub-pages on the left, starting with *At a glance* (each page's state in one line, on top what a switched-on feature is missing), then *Assistant* (Features, Defaults, Language model), *Speech* (Speech recognition, Speech output, Voices) and *Spark* (Security, Operation, Update, Backup). Every page: title with a sentence, sections, rows with the switch or value on the right, one sentence of help, the rest behind "More", "Advanced" folded; a search field finds all settings and guides and marks the spot (also in the Me window). *Features*: one switch per capability in groups (conversation, my day, mail and documents, home, on the go and devices, access), each with a sentence, marks (*set up under Me → …*, *never for guests*, *read only*, *leaves the house*), a guide and indented settings that appear once it is on; each feature is first one short line to unfold. Filters *All*, *On*, *Off*, *Needs you* (on but missing e.g. SearXNG address, Wyoming addresses, Telegram token, Apple key, speaker firmware). Further pages: *Defaults* (for guests and new profiles), *Language model*, *Speech recognition* (Qwen3-ASR or Parakeet), *Speech output*, *Voices*, *Security*, *Operation* (memory guard, watchdog, GPU clock cap, logs, ports), *Update, Backup*. Each sub-page saves on its own; the bar at the bottom shows unsaved changes (with "Discard"), leaving asks first; only affected services restart.
- **Funktionen** (features; its own menu entry): all switches, each with its guide (per service: what it does, what you need, setup, use, what leaves the house, switching off) and *Who may do what*: only functions blocked for at least one profile, with one chip per profile (tap lifts the block); without blocks "No profile is blocked. You set blocks in the function under "Who may use it"." with *To the features*. New blocks are set in the function under *Who may use it*. Guests get no function. Clicking the name jumps to the function's switch (or the switched-off function it builds on). Every change is in the admin log ("Wetter für Anna erlaubt"); a Verwalter (manager) can lift blocks too. A new profile gets everything released at once. Under Me what is off or blocked stays visible with the reason (for admins with a jump to *Features* or *People and devices → Blocked functions*). The preset for "Rückfrage ohne Weckwort" and "Eigene Stimme überhören" sits in the detail of those functions.
- **List and detail**: on computers one line per function on the left, the open one on the right with explanation, marks, *Guide*, **Wer darf es** ("Who may use it: every profile · blocked: …"; "Block a profile …" opens the profile chips; applies at once, overview under *People and devices → Blocked functions*) and settings; only one open at a time, on phones below its line. The speed and accuracy switches have their group **Tempo und Genauigkeit**.
- **Me, people and apps:** on computers Me looks like the settings. *Gespräch* (conversation) has no tabs; what the admin switched off is collected at the bottom. *People and devices*: the open person beside the list, devices below. *Apps and interfaces*: addresses and one tile per app with steps and code; the MCP and Pebble tiles show whether the function is on.
- **One shell for everybody:** profiles get the same menu as the admin, without an admin role only Assistant and Me; guests the plain assistant page. A profile with the *Admin* or *Manager* role, signed in with the second step, sees the admin pages. The emergency access is the panel password via the address with `#admin` (e.g. `https://…/#admin`). "Einstellungen" is only the admin menu, the roles *Admin-Rechte*; the Me page *Werkzeug-Verlauf*, Logs → Update *Update-Verlauf*.
- **Ich starts with "Heute":** at the top of *Überblick* (phone: above the page list) stand the next appointments of today and tomorrow (at most three titles, calendars with a 3 second budget, cached for 5 minutes), open reminders, unread messages, own documents and remembered facts; only for allowed functions, only numbers and short titles, never mail or message text. Below it **Probier mal** with up to six sentences. Endpoint `GET /api/profile/today` (profile login or its own app key, rate limited), also for the iPhone app.
- **Rights arrive at once:** blocks and releases arrive without reloading. Open pages ask `/api/whoami` every minute, on coming back and on focus; its `rights` is a fingerprint of the rights (`features.stamp`, `stamp` in `/api/features`); when it changes the page reloads (never mid-conversation or after typing in the last 10 minutes; then a bar "Your functions were changed"). The iPhone app asks `hello` every minute in front; the Begleiter notices through `rev`. An ESP32 speaker checks switch, block and device every 2 seconds and disconnects otherwise (Live: "Rechte · Lautsprecher getrennt"). A confirmed MCP proposal only runs while the profile may still use MCP. A known device key with its function off (or Mein Notaus) gets 503 "Für dieses Gerät gerade ausgeschaltet" and keeps its pairing; an unknown one stays 401. MCP programs: the Spark refuses switched-off functions on every call, new ones show after the next connect.
- **Explain instead of silence:** asking for something the answer may not use puts a note with the reason from fixed rules under it (`intent.explain`, `features.reason`, never the model): switched off on the Spark (admins: *Under Features*), blocked by the admin (no button), switched off yourself (*Change under Me*), needs a profile (*Sign in*), needs the second login step, or at a shared speaker "personal things need your recognized voice". Nothing shows when the function is offered, the question is unclear or the reason is not a switch. Log `weiche: erklärt weather - spark`.
- **Switching on what a function needs:** every function knows its chain in `features.py` (the functions it builds on and their Spark switches). When a prerequisite is missing while switching on under Features, the Spark asks ""Dokumente erstellen" also needs "Eigene Dokumente". Switch them on too?"; the switches get ticked and are saved with the page. What the Spark may not switch itself or what still needs setting up it names with its way. Switching off asks "This also switches off …. Switch off anyway?". When something cannot be used in the chat, the answer names every missing switch with its way (`features.missing_text`), and so does the help about the Spark.
- **Personen und Geräte** (people and devices): profiles and devices with a key, plus *Apps and interfaces* with values for Open WebUI, OpenAI-compatible apps, the Pebble watch, Siri, curl and Python.
  **One profile in one place:** at the top **Guests** (guest access on/off; questions and answers only). Admin roles carry a badge. *Details*: **Access** (PIN, reset the second step, admin role – main admin only –, browser *Sign out*), **Call name**, **Devices**, **Functions** ("3 of 12 on", blocked ones to lift), **Rights** (background jobs: off, read only, also act; No daily limit "frei"; Spark update from the iPhone app; upload space in MB) and **Data** (delete profile). What the Spark switched off shows as "Off on the Spark". A manager sees no rights but may sign browsers out.
- **Text fields grow with the text:** every text field grows with its text up to half the window, then scrolls inside; the assistant's input up to six lines. Enter sends, Shift+Enter makes a new line (on a phone the arrow sends). From four lines ⤢ opens the **large view** with *Done* (also Esc) and *Send*; nothing is lost. From 80 % of the allowed length "n / max" stands below. The iPhone app does the same (up to five lines), the Android app shows the panel, the terminal asks in three to six lines.

The panel is available in English and German; the button at the top right switches (default: the browser's language).

**History on every device:** a profile's conversations live on the Spark. The panel, the Android app and the iPhone app send only the new messages after each answer, and the Spark appends them; when two devices ask in the same conversation, both questions stay. An open page fetches what other devices added as soon as it is in front and every 30 seconds (only changed ones, at most 120 requests per minute and profile). Only deleting replaces the Spark's copy. Questions from the Pebble watch are kept like Siri, Telegram and the companion as their own conversation per day ("Uhr 12.10."). Guests keep their history in their own browser only.

**Instant history sync, one conversation for all channels, offline reading** (each function off until the admin switches it on under Features; then for every profile; never guests):

- *Instant history sync*: each open page keeps one event connection to the Spark (`/api/profile/convos/stream`, at most 8 per profile, renewed after 30 minutes). When a conversation changes on another device, the page fetches it at once; a deleted one closes. While another device answers in the open conversation, a pale "Phone is answering …" shows (only the kind of device, never the question). The live view shows only the number of these connections.
- *One conversation for all channels*: Telegram, Siri, the companion, the watch and the own voice at a speaker write into today's main conversation (the first time the day needs it the newest of today that belongs to no channel alone, else "Heute DD.MM."; it stays the same all day), each message marked with its channel (white like the text in the own, blue bubble). More context, but also more foreign context. A voice that is not surely the owner's stays in the speaker's conversation.
- *Read history offline*: only a browser the profile trusts at the second sign-in step keeps a copy of the last 30 conversations and the page. Without a connection the panel opens it for reading; questions without a connection (at most 10) go out once the Spark answers, each into its conversation. Every other browser stores nothing and deletes what it had the next time it opens; signing out always deletes.

**Device instead of profile as the key (fixed rules):** whatever waits on an answer belongs to device and conversation (`profiles.quelle`; device `profiles.geraet`: the device key, in a browser a hash of its login). The "yes" to proposals, the command waiting for the code word, questions back and the short follow-up hang on it. Two devices or conversations never confirm each other. A "yes" for which nothing waits here, while something waits elsewhere or was replaced, carries out nothing, and the assistant says so (`chat_turn.YES_ELSEWHERE`, log `chat: ja ohne vorschlag hier | ersetzt|anderes geraet`). One open proposal per profile and module applies.
- *Notes to the device in use*: each device reports for itself, a page with its tab; an idle second window never outvotes the one in use, a hidden tab marks only itself away, and a note counts as seen only on the browser whose push subscription it went to (else it follows to the phone).
- *Today's main conversation*: fixed and kept the first time it is needed (`users/<id>/hauptgespraech.json`, only the day and the id); answers from Telegram, speaker, watch, Siri, companion and the cards of Open land there all day, also when a topic conversation changes later. Deleted, the next need fixes a new one.
- *Siri and companion*: the short follow-up (10 minutes) is per device; PC and Mac, iPhone and iPad never get each other's history.
- *Instant sync*: when a profile opens more than 8 pages, the oldest one's connection ends with the event "verdraengt"; the page connects again after a minute.

Self-test `test_geraet_statt_profil`.

Always, without a switch: sources, document passages and created documents under an answer are kept with it and show again on every device. The iPhone app fetches what is new when opened and during a conversation.

## All functions

Every function of the Spark with its switch, what it builds on, where you set it up and what it does (the first sentence of its guide). `hilfe.py --docs` writes this list from `features.py` and the guides, so a new function stands here and in the help by itself.

<!-- funktionen:liste -->
### Conversation

- **Web search** (Features → Conversation → "Web search"): When a question needs current information (news, opening hours, prices), the assistant searches the web itself and answers from the results.

- **Faster web search** (Features → Conversation → "Faster web search"; builds on "Web search"): The web search no longer waits for the slowest page: each page gets at most 3 seconds, what is not there by then stays the short snippet from the result list.

- **Wikipedia straight away** (Features → Conversation → "Wikipedia straight away"; Me → Conversation): For knowledge questions the Spark fetches the intro of the matching Wikipedia article and gives only that to the language model.

- **Own Kiwix archive** (Features → Conversation → "Own Kiwix archive"; Me → Conversation): The Spark looks things up in your own kiwix-serve: knowledge questions first in its Wikipedia books, "Look in my archive …" in the full text of all chosen books, "Tell me more" reads up to three parts of an article.

- **Look locally first** (Features → Conversation → "Look locally first"; Me → Conversation): Whether a question belongs to your own data, the web or both is decided by the source switch with fixed rules (never the model); what no rule knows it checks with a short look in your Find index.

- **Source note** (Features → Conversation → "Source note"; Me → Conversation): Under every answer that wants to know something it says where it came from: "Source: 2 mails, Read appointments, Web search" or "nothing looked up, model knowledge only".

- **Say the source in conversation** (Features → Conversation → "Say the source in conversation"; builds on "Source note"; Me → Conversation): At a speaker, on the phone, the watch, via Siri and in the car you see no line under the answer.

- **Roles by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Roles by voice"; Me → Conversation): You save up to 8 roles, such as "Butler" or "Explainer", and switch by voice.

- **Own conversation style** (Features → Conversation → "Own conversation style"; Me → Conversation): Each profile says in its own words how the assistant should talk to it: form of address, tone, form, e.g.

- **Follow-up without wake word** (Features → Conversation → "Follow-up without wake word"; Me → Conversation): After answering a spoken question the assistant keeps listening for a few seconds, so "And tomorrow?" or "Make it brighter" works without another "Hey Spark".

- **Ignore my own voice** (Features → Conversation → "Ignore my own voice"; Me → Conversation): Every device only filters its own echo.

- **Design own voices** (Features → Conversation → "Design own voices"; Me → Conversation): The answer voice is no longer only the fixed speaker list: 20 ready voices with more variety (calm, lively, narrator, butler, Bavarian …) and up to three of your own, designed from fixed fields and a few own words.

- **Barge-in: pause first** (Features → Conversation → "Barge-in: pause first"; Me → Conversation): Without this switch a quarter of a second of voice ends the answer, a cough or a "mhm" too.

- **Turn end by tone** (Features → Conversation → "Turn end by tone"; Me → Conversation): Today a word list decides whether your sentence is finished: when it ends on "and", "because" or "um", the assistant waits longer.

- **Learning from corrections** (Features → Conversation → "Learning from corrections"; builds on "Memory"; Me → Conversation): If you correct the assistant ("No, my brother is called Tim", "You should always check the calendar for appointments"), it turns this into a short sentence and asks: "Shall I remember …?" It is stored only after your "yes", in your memory.

- **Ask instead of guessing** (Features → Conversation → "Ask instead of guessing"; Me → Conversation): If a command fits several devices ("Turn on the TV in the living room", with a TV and an Apple TV box there), the assistant does not guess: it asks "Which one do you mean?" and shows the fitting devices as buttons.

- **Remember the choice** (Features → Conversation → "Remember the choice"; builds on "Ask instead of guessing"; Me → Conversation): After your choice the assistant asks: "Shall I remember that ‘TV in the living room’ is the Samsung?" After "yes" the same sentence switches that device straight away.

- **Ask which way** (Features → Conversation → "Ask which way"; builds on "Ask instead of guessing"): If a wish could go two ways that both change something ("Write Tom a mail that I am late": mail or message at home; "Meeting Anna tomorrow at 3": appointment or reminder), the assistant asks first: "Should I, first send it as an e-mail, …

- **Show steps and approvals** (Features → Conversation → "Show steps and approvals"): Below every answer a folded line shows which tools the assistant used, e.g.

- **Picture recognition** (Features → Conversation → "Picture recognition"; Me → Conversation): You attach a photo and ask about it: "What plant is this?", "What does the sign say?", "What does the error message mean?".

- **Exact calculations** (Features → Conversation → "Exact calculations"): A language model is bad at arithmetic.

- **Help with the Spark** (Features → Conversation → "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When you ask the assistant about the Spark itself ("What does targeted tool choice do?", "Where do I set the voice?"), it looks in the Spark's guides, switches and docs and answers from them, with the path in the panel and whether it is …

- **Suggesting settings** (Features → Conversation → "Suggesting settings"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When a setting fits your question, the assistant shows a card below the answer: which switches, before and after.

- **Ask Spark on every page** (Features → Conversation → "Ask Spark on every page"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): The sign "Ask Spark" at the top (on a computer next to "Spark" in the side menu, on a phone in the top bar) is the assistant on every page.

- **Suggesting functions** (Features → Conversation → "Suggesting functions"; builds on "Help with the Spark"; Me → Conversation): When you ask for something that is still off for you ("What is on my calendar today?" with the calendar off), the assistant says so plainly and shows "Set up" below.

- **You can also say** (Features → Conversation → "You can also say"; builds on "Suggesting functions"; Me → What I can do): The assistant shows functions that are on for you but that you never used, each with a sentence you can say as it stands.

- **Tips while talking** (Features → Conversation → "Tips while talking"; builds on "You can also say"; Me → Conversation): At a speaker, on the phone, in the car and at the watch there are no buttons.

- **Priority for people** (Features → Conversation → "Priority for people"): When several people talk to the Spark at once, chosen profiles get their answer first.

- **Note when a service is missing** (Features → Conversation → "Note when a service is missing"; builds on "Service watch"; Me → Conversation): When the service watch sees a service gone, the assistant says so at once when somebody needs it, e.g.

- **Instant history sync** (Features → Conversation → "Instant history sync"; Me → Conversation): What you ask on one device shows up at once in the open panel on your other devices, without reloading; a deleted conversation disappears there at once.

- **One conversation for all channels** (Features → Conversation → "One conversation for all channels"; Me → Conversation): Telegram, Siri, the companion, the watch and your voice at a speaker write into today's main conversation instead of their own conversation per day.

- **Read history offline** (Features → Conversation → "Read history offline"; Me → Conversation): A browser you trust at the second sign-in step ("trust this browser") keeps a copy of your last 30 conversations.

- **Settings assistant** (Features → Conversation → "Settings assistant"): Whatever goes wrong becomes a case: a tool fails, an error, a question no rule recognizes that ends in the web search, a refusal ("I cannot"), the same question twice, a 👎 or "Wrongly sorted" on the Weiche.

- **Explain requests** (Features → Conversation → "Explain requests"; builds on "Settings assistant"): Under Status → Logs → Requests a button explains a request in fixed sentences: which intent the Weiche saw, which tools ran, where it got stuck.

- **Ask the assistant for proposals** (Features → Conversation → "Ask the assistant for proposals"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): Admin profiles ask "What does the settings assistant propose?" and hear the open proposals.

- **Note on a new proposal** (Features → Conversation → "Note on a new proposal"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): When a new proposal comes, admin profiles get a short note with its title, at most three a day.

- **Prepare cases as reports** (Features → Conversation → "Prepare cases as reports"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When nothing from the catalog helps, the case becomes a draft under Reporting problems, with fixed sentences and a preview before sending.

- **Autopilot for small changes** (Features → Conversation → "Autopilot for small changes"; builds on "Settings assistant"): He takes stage A and B with a green test bench and at least two reports himself: one a day, at most three steps, back when it gets worse.

- **Ideas outside the catalog** (Features → Conversation → "Ideas outside the catalog"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When the fixed catalog has nothing for a case, the local language model writes an idea: text, at most one setting with a jump link and steps to copy.

- **Analyse a case** (Features → Conversation → "Analyse a case"; builds on "Settings assistant"): The local language model reads a case and picks a rule from fixed templates: must word, tool hint, extra tool for a group, word or test sentence for the Weiche.

- **Undo automatically** (Features → Conversation → "Undo automatically"; builds on "Settings assistant"): The re-check looks at every taken change after its waiting time: when the case came back, a test sentence turned red or the 👍 share of its group fell by at least 15 points (from 10 ratings before and after), the result is "worse".

- **Steps for the environment** (Features → Conversation → "Steps for the environment"; builds on "Settings assistant"): When a service outside the Spark is the cause (search, wiki, NAS, network), the proposal shows steps to copy.

- **Proposals for my profile** (Features → Conversation → "Proposals for my profile"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): A profile's own 👎 lead to proposals for its settings, e.g.

- **👍/👎 under answers** (Features → Conversation → "👍/👎 under answers"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): Under every answer 👍 or 👎.

- **"Why?" for every profile** (Features → Conversation → ""Why?" for every profile"; builds on "Settings assistant"; Me → Conversation): When you ask "Why did you just search the web?" or "Why didn't you look it up?", the Spark explains the way of its last answer: how it understood the question and where it looked, in fixed plain sentences, without rules, tool names or se …

- **Spark diary** (Features → Conversation → "Spark diary"; Me → Conversation): The Spark notes what it did for you itself: devices switched, reminders set, mail drafts saved, documents made, files put on the reMarkable, messages sent.

- **Status by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Status by voice"): When you ask "Is everything running?", "Is Home Assistant reachable?", "When was the last backup?", "Is there an update?", "Is the emergency stop on?" or "Is my mailbox connected?", the Spark reads its own state and answers only from it …

- **Undo by voice** (Features → Conversation → "Undo by voice"): When you say "Undo that", the Spark proposes to take back its last deed for you: delete a reminder, remove a list entry, forget a note, take an appointment out again, switch a device back, set the volume back, the pause of notes, restore …

- **Feedback when it is fixed** (Features → Conversation → "Feedback when it is fixed"; builds on "👍/👎 under answers"; Me → Conversation): When a change helps after your 👎, the assistant tells you once.

### Speed and accuracy

- **Thinking while choosing tools** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Thinking while choosing tools"; Me → Conversation): The model thinks briefly before it picks a tool (calendar, mail, web search …).

- **Targeted tool choice** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Fixed rules in the panel recognize from your own words what it is about (smart home, appointment, mail, web search, reminder, list, weather …).

- **Fetch clear questions directly** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Fetch clear questions directly"; Me → Conversation): For short, clear questions the panel calls the matching tool itself, by fixed rules: "Do I have new mail?" (mail list), "Which reminders do I have?", "What is the weather like?" (today, at home), "Where is my parcel?" and "Which appointm …

- **Names for listening** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Names for listening"): The speech recognition gets a short list of names with every recording: all profiles, the profile's contacts and the rooms and devices from its Home Assistant.

- **Answer simple things at once** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Answer simple things at once"): The panel answers a few simple questions itself with a fixed sentence, without the language model: the time ("What time is it?"), the date and weekday ("What day is it today?"), calculations with brackets, percent and roots ("What is 17 …

- **Trim a large memory** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Trim a large memory"): When the Spark has remembered a lot for a profile (more than about 6000 characters), the language model only gets the newest facts as a fixed part plus at most five older ones that share words with the question.

- **Instant acknowledgement** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Instant acknowledgement"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Right after your question the Spark says in a short sentence what it is doing now, for example "Let me check your calendar." or "Let me search the web.".

- **Tune the switch** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Tune the switch"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"): On Features → Routing, "Tune" starts a run: the language model proposes up to 6 words for groups that everyday sentences often miss.

- **Keep unclear questions anonymously** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Keep unclear questions anonymously"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): Questions no rule of the switch recognizes are listed on Features → Routing under "unclear": without names, numbers, addresses and codes, never with profile, device or time.

- **Fetch ahead on a question back** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Fetch ahead on a question back"; builds on "Targeted tool choice"; Me → Conversation): When an answer ends with a question back ("For which day?"), the panel already fetches what the next answer will most likely need: the calendar of the next days, the newest mails, the weather at home or parcels.

- **Classification with probabilities** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): The language model on the Spark answers fixed questions of the panel with probabilities instead of text (/v1/systemone of the LLM-Tor).

- **Classification when tidying the inbox** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification when tidying the inbox"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): Mails no fixed rule sorts while tidying get their category with a real probability.

- **Classification in the quality test** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification in the quality test"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"): The quality test (Status → Checks) gets a second opinion per question: how well the answer answers it (0 to 2) and how likely it makes something up.

- **Classification in the settings assistant** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Classification in the settings assistant"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"): The settings assistant picks the fitting proposal for a case from its fixed catalog.

- **Check answers in the background** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Check answers in the background"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): After an answer for which tools ran, the panel asks in the background whether the answer names details that are in no result.

- **Recognize complaints** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Recognize complaints"; builds on "Classification with probabilities"; Me → Conversation): When somebody says "still does not work" or "it is still on" right after a deed, the panel recognizes it from a fixed word list, otherwise with the classification from 80 %.

- **Helper model** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model"): A small, fast language model (Qwen3 4B) on the model computer takes simple work off the big language model: fixed questions with a fixed answer list (classification) for routing, the dungeon judge and narrator test bench and mail (tidyin …

- **Helper model spots attacks** (Features → Speed and accuracy → "Helper model spots attacks"; builds on "Helper model"): After an answer for which a tool read outside text (web, mail, document, calendar), the panel asks the helper model in the background whether that text held an order to the assistant.

### My data

- **Memory** (Features → My data → "Memory"; Me → Memory): The assistant remembers things about you, such as names, preferences or where something is, and uses them later.
